Still open: when to lift the calendar out into its own connector service (D21), and which model endpoint the question box should use.

## 5. First use case: a manager plans the week (built)

Persona: Hillevi, rektor. Four utvecklingssamtal remain before 28 November. The assistant finds slots next week that suit her and each employee, draws the proposals in her week, she confirms, invitations go out, and she can undo for ten minutes.

In scope:

- Question box at the top. Typeahead with completions. An answer becomes a thread under the box.
- "Läget" row: four figures, always the same shape: korttidsfrånvaro, budgetavvikelse, HME, utvecklingssamtal. Synthetic data behind a read interface in v1.
- "I dag" strip: today's meetings on a timeline with free gaps marked.
- One pushed thread: proposals rendered in a small week view, legend, confirm, "visa i kalendern", "vecka 47 i stället", a "varför nu?" line with source and time.
- Calendar page: the week as an Astryx Grid agenda (Mon–Fri), week navigation, the same thread as a side panel with checkboxes. No drag-to-move and no time grid (decided 2026-11-03): the assistant redraws instead of the user dragging.
- Mobile: chip row for today, checklist of proposals, confirm, link to the week view.
- Undo window after sending, with the result shown under "Redan hanterat".
- Real calendar through CalDAV. Reuse `packages/calendar-nextcloud` and the proposal logic from startsidan docs 28 and 34.

Out of scope for the first build: attest and incidents (no Heroma or Raindance MCP servers exist), standing consent, voice, news, Studio or any page editor.

Acceptance, the kind that can be checked: a logged-in manager sees four proposals that do not collide with any existing event in her or the employees' calendars; confirming creates four CalDAV events and an audit row; undo within ten minutes removes them and writes a second audit row; the thread survives reload and shows the same state on desktop and mobile.

## 6. UX principles to hold on to

- The question box is the page. No navigation, no tabs, no blocks.
- Every proactive item is a thread with a reply field and memory. No separate chat log.
- Attention budget: a fixed number of interruptions per day, visible in the header. The rest is bundled to lunch or the next morning.
- Every thread answers "varför ser jag detta?" with trigger, source and time.
- Figures are plain sentences with a comparison, not KPI tiles, except in the fixed "Läget" row.
- The conversation is the transcript in the page's own column, in place of the stream, desktop and phone alike (D31): never a modal, never an overlay, never the side panel. The row keeps focus throughout; sending never moves it. The side panel is for what the conversation or the page is about: a view, a source or a card (D45), beside the column wherever it fits.
- After an execute, the card becomes a receipt: each row shows the source system's answer read back (sent, moved, unknown with retry, not sent), the badge says when it was checked, the primary button becomes undo with a countdown, and any panel view re-reads from the source. Green only on a read-back, never on a sent request.
- Objects, chips, proposals (D12): everything rendered is an object with a ref; objects become chips in the conversation by click, drag or `@`; the conversation edits cards, never systems; the card's button executes. The side panel stays live under the dim for picking. It opens with a scope block that says what it is looking at. Cards hold state and actions and never contain a text input. (Replaces the session-1 line "no overlay on desktop", which assumed the box at the top. Canvas row "Samtalet får en egen yta", 2026-11-03.)
- Names of individual employees are shown inside the source system, never in the stream.
- Status is shown with lightness as well as hue. Touch targets 44 px. Real buttons, inputs and labels.
- Every thread card shows one next step, filled only when it is due today (D48). The rest sits behind one `Fler val`: the connector's other actions, Clew's `Lägg till i samtalet`, on a row `Inte nu` as a sub-menu, `Avfärda` and `Varför ser jag detta?`. A card keeps `Inte nu` in view; a card that waits on someone has `Påminn mig` instead (D49: one time action per card, and both end the same way). Connectors contribute actions, never layout (`docs/architecture/integrations.md`, thread action contract).
- Completion is a feeling the page earns, not a game: the "Behöver dig" counter counts down and reaches zero, the done state names what was done and how long it took, and routine obligations with a deadline get a meter (11 av 12 samtal). No points, levels, badges, leaderboards or streaks; dismissing never counts as done.
- Motion is subtle and functional: 160 to 220 ms ease-out on height and opacity when a card expands, collapses, enters from the composer or leaves after a decision; counters tick, nothing bounces or slides across the page; `prefers-reduced-motion` turns every transition off. Motion explains what moved, never decorates.

## 7. Guardrails carried over from startsidan

- **Open source only.** No closed-source dependency of any kind; check the licence before pinning. The system is meant to be shared with other municipalities and governments. Full rule in `CLAUDE.md`.
- Server-side enforcement of identity, scope and connector state. Visibility never grants access.
- No credentials, tokens, database clients or trusted identity context in the browser bundle.
- OIDC is the only way a person is identified. Every downstream call carries an audience-bound OAuth 2.1 token for that person; the model never holds a token. The dev login is local-only and refuses to start in production.
- Every mutation writes its audit row in the same transaction. External commands have explicit status and an `unknown` outcome when a reply is lost.
- Assistant proposals are validated by the server and executed only through a human decision endpoint that is not a tool.
- Every capability works without the model: a REST route, a button or a standing action. A provider outage degrades the composer, never the page (D8).
- Scope every source before joining. No raw SQL tools for the model.
- Astryx components and documented composition patterns only. No custom components, no custom CSS beyond the Astryx imports and body basics (decided 2026-11-03; the former week-view exception is withdrawn). No second component system (`ai-elements`, shadcn, Tailwind).
- The design canvas follows Astryx, not the other way round: when a drawn control has no Astryx counterpart, the canvas is redrawn in the component's shape (decided 2026-11-03 for the composer: `ChatComposer`, a rounded box with context line, field, microphone and send, chips as tokens in the field).

## 8. Repository layout (as built)

```
clew/
  .devcontainer/          compose.yaml (app, postgres, temporal, temporal-ui, the six fixture connectors; calendar profile), Dockerfile, devcontainer.json, calendar/ (Nextcloud image + seed)
  apps/web/               React 19 + Vite + Astryx Neutral: pages/start, pages/calendar, pages/login, components/
  apps/server/            Hono BFF, D20 layout: routes/ (auth, api, connectors, admin), threads/usecases (cards, reminders), connectors/ (registry, remote, model, describe, reconcile, usecases/admin, adapters/{postgres,memory}), modules/ (calendar/, people/, figures/, mocks/), assistant/, temporal/, scheduler, auth, system-context
  packages/contracts/     TypeBox schemas and types: thread, proposal, calendar event, figure, inputs
  packages/db/            Drizzle schema, migrations, auth/thread/job queries
  packages/calendar/      model (provider interface), dates, nextcloud provider, accounts, proposals (planner)
  packages/connectors/    capability layer: Connector, Capability, Registry (with Governance, D23), ScopeRule, the Clew call handle; protocol.ts (connector protocol, D21); remote.ts (transport); check.ts (the conformance check shared by the CLI and the admin page); mock/ (hr, finance, incidents, kommunikation, data; generic systems, no vendor names, D52); memory-state
  connectors/hello/       a connector (D21, D22): connector.mjs, server.mjs, Dockerfile, compose.yaml, README; depends on nothing else here
  connectors/{hr,finance,incidents,communication,timesheet,payroll}/  the fixture connectors (D61): generic systems as independent services (manifest.mjs, connector.mjs, fixture.mjs, state.mjs, server.mjs, Dockerfile, README), run and registered by the development stack, run in-process by the unit suites through tests/fixture-connectors.ts
  connectors/phonebook/   the phone book as a connector (D38): manifest.mjs (schemas and named capabilities with declared views), connector.mjs (reads and HTTP boundary; exports the manifest), directory.mjs (the phone book behind a port: HTTP adapter, `toPerson`, a made-up organisation), server.mjs, Dockerfile, compose.yaml, README
  packages/ui/            Providers (theme + sv-SE), Astryx re-exports, session boundary, Grid week agenda, the calendar family (calendar.tsx with the FullCalendar theme, calendar-embeds.tsx, agenda-items.ts), styles.css (imports only)
  scripts/                compose.sh, calendar.sh, a11y.sh, db-init, auth-setup, calendar-seed, auth-dev-check, dev-connectors, dev-reset, fixtures-reset.sh, connector-registry and job-bindings (shared by the CLIs)
  .github/workflows/      a11y.yml (scripts/a11y.sh) and check.yml (bun run check), on code pushes to main and in pull requests
  tests/unit/             bun test: domain examples and properties (fast-check), scenario tests over the memory adapters (threads, samtal, conversation, scheduler), the store contracts on the memory adapters
  tests/store-contract.ts, tests/proposal-store-contract.ts  the store contracts (D25), one suite each for both adapters
  tests/db/               the store contract against Postgres, `bun run test:db` in the devcontainer, not in check
  tests/a11y/             WCAG 2.2 AA suite: Playwright + axe against a production build with /api mocked, run by scripts/a11y.sh
  docs/                   README.md (map and conventions), vision.md (what Clew is, the Clew/Eneo line), decisions/ (one file per decision D01–D21, index and template), reviews/ (dated reviews), architecture/integrations.md (principles), architecture/integration-paths.md (catalogue of paths and status), architecture/capability-surfaces.md (REST, model tools, MCP), architecture/working-across-modules.md (joins on identity and refs, composites), architecture/scheduled-work.md (scheduler and Temporal), architecture/identity.md, architecture/backend-structure.md (D20), architecture/module-protocol.md (D21), protocol/ (generated JSON Schema), guides/ (creating an external module, creating an integration, worked example with a custom directory), personas.md
  HANDOVER.md             this file · README.md run instructions
```

No `apps/worker`: the Temporal worker runs inside the BFF process (D17).

## 9. Session log

MCP-backed native connector exploration, 2026-11-09: documented the requested options in `docs/reviews/2026-11-09-mcp-backed-native-connectors.md`, linked from the documentation home and integration paths. An independent native connector can use MCP as its source API under D4; D81's direct service path stays separate. The review maps the poll's actual tools/resources to proposed reads, scans and execution, identifies the missing generic answer form and native-to-App lifecycle binding, and distinguishes source idempotency hints from durable recovery. It also records the single synthetic identity, missing historical/expiry evidence, and the retention difference between the MCP decision fingerprint and native encrypted execution inputs. Recommendation: prove an isolated read/scan wrapper, strengthen source identity/history/recovery, then choose a bounded native form or explicit App handoff. No new product decision, connector, source mutation or dependency. Verified 9 poll/host unit tests and 1 isolated Node HTTP interoperability test in the devcontainer; existing shared-tree changes preserved.

The Qlik connector, D82 amended, 2026-11-09: the product owner asked for the connector to be named after Qlik, a connector that shows sick leave in the app when it is connected. `connectors/absence` is `connectors/qlik`: connector id `qlik`, source "Qlik", capabilities `qlik.sickLeaveByUnit` and `qlik.sickLeaveTrend`, area `sick-leave`, settings `QLIK_ADAPTER` (`fixture` or `engine`), `QLIK_UNIT_MAP`, `QLIK_MIN_GROUP`, `QLIK_CACHE_SECONDS`, `QLIK_SICK_LEAVE_WARN_ABOVE`, `QLIK_SICK_LEAVE_CUBE`, the adapter file `engine.mjs`, the test `tests/unit/qlik-connector.test.ts`. The capabilities still speak of sick leave, not of Qlik's fields (D1). The same day the local dev database, which had been at 0030, failed `db:init` at 0032 and 0033 with "pending trigger events"; the fix is its own PR (0032 sets constraints immediate as 0030 does). On a dev stack that registered `absence`, remove it (`bun scripts/connector-add.ts absence --remove`) and register `qlik` on 4048.

Sick-leave statistics from Qlik, D82 (first written as D63), 2026-11-06: the product owner brought an exploration of Qlik written by another agent and asked whether Clew should have a Qlik connector with a sick-leave widget. Checked against the code: the Läget tile "Korttidsfrånvaro 4,1 %" is hard-coded in `apps/server/src/modules/figures/domain.ts`, the HR mock's area figure is drawn nowhere, and the panel tables come from the HR mock's declared views. The exploration is in `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-sick-leave-from-qlik.md`, corrected where `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-manager-statistics-from-qlik.md` already answered it (Qlik Sense Enterprise on Windows) and where its decision numbers clash. Then built `connectors/absence` (D82, proposed): `absence.unitRates` and `absence.trend` as table views for managers, an area `absence` with a figure, aggregates only, a group below `ABSENCE_MIN_GROUP` (default 5, a placeholder for HR and the data protection officer) held back in the connector, a fixture for the synthetic units and `qlik.mjs` reading one session hypercube over the Engine API with a service identity. `tests/unit/absence-connector.test.ts` (15 cases) runs the connector over the protocol, the checker, and the Qlik adapter against a fake engine on the socket (paging over the 10,000-cell limit, master measures, Qlik date numbers, engine errors and timeouts without the engine's words). The connector ran as a process in the devcontainer and `connector-check.ts` passed; Node 26's WebSocket was confirmed to send the bearer header. Rebased onto `main` on 2026-11-09 for the PR: `main` had meanwhile taken D63 (Hem drawn as the canvas) and built D54, and the fixture connectors had moved to `connectors/` on ports 4040–4046. The decision became D82, the connector's port 4048, and its figure took D54's form (`label`, `value`, `comparison`, `opens: absence.unitRates`; tone `ok`/`warn` only with `ABSENCE_WARN_ABOVE`). Not done: a real Qlik (field names, closed months, the CSRF rule on a JWT virtual proxy since November 2024, licence), client certificates, a renewing token (a JWT signed per read or a Qlik Cloud token), a signed-in look at the tile on Läget.

Fixture connectors (D61), 2026-11-06, main checkout: the product owner asked for real test situations instead of mocks, a full chain shipped in the devcontainer, and no dummy connectors or data in Clew's code; `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-from-mock-connectors-to-real-test-sources.md` weighed a separate repository and real products behind the fixtures (Odoo, Zammad, WireMock on SupportManagement's OpenAPI, lldap), and the owner chose to keep the fixtures in this repository as independent services and English ids. Built: `connectors/{hr,finance,incidents,communication,timesheet,payroll}` in the phone book's shape (manifest.mjs, connector.mjs, fixture.mjs with records relative to the seed day, state.mjs with node:sqlite in the service and memory in tests), every write deduplicated by the operation id and read back through `<id>.lookup`, `FIXTURE_FAULT` (unavailable, slow, unauthorized, conflict, crash-after-commit), `FIXTURE_UNIT_MAP` and `FIXTURE_PERSON_MAP`, payroll importing the HR fixture's feed outside the protocol with its night rule; the six as services in `.devcontainer/compose.yaml` (workspace mounted read-only on Node's image, SQLite on a volume each, the BFF on `HOST=0.0.0.0`, published on 127.0.0.1:4040–4046, 4045 skipped because Node's fetch refuses it as a bad port), `bun run dev:connectors` (registration through `scripts/connector-registry.ts` and the `hr:absence` bindings through `scripts/job-bindings.ts`, both shared with the CLIs; `job-bind.ts --mocks` is `--fixtures`; `postCreateCommand` runs it), `bun run fixtures:reset` (`scripts/fixtures-reset.sh` and `scripts/dev-reset.ts`, audited `fixtures.reset`); the mocks in `packages/connectors/src/mock/`, `memory-state.ts`, `@clew/db/state`, `module_state`, `modules/mocks/`, `POST /api/mocks/reset` and `scripts/modules-check.ts` removed; migrations `0030_english_ids` (phonebook, communication, the calendar area `calendar` and the area ids of the fixtures; a renamed remote connector needs its secret set again, the seal is bound to the id, which the phone book showed on the dev stack) and `0031_fixture_connectors` (drop `module_state`, delete the six bundled rows); the unit suites run the services in-process through `tests/fixture-connectors.ts` (`mock-job.test.ts` is `fixture-job.test.ts`; 430 tests), a Node test proves the SQLite store across a restart and that `server.mjs` boots, the six manifests are generated examples beside hello and phonebook; cruiser rule `clew-does-not-import-connectors` (the served protocol's examples excepted). Verified: `bun run check`, `bun run test:db` on a scratch database `clew_fixtures` with both migrations, `connector-check` green against all six services in the running stack, the touched WCAG specs after the renames (69 passed, 135 skipped). Landed on `main` in steps at the product owner's word; the dev database was migrated and the phone book process restarted from its new folder. `scripts/jobs-check.ts` then ran the job across systems on the fixtures end to end on the dev stack (assembly from the three services, one approval, both writes read back, payroll pending, the watch's three checks, the expired card under `payroll`): the first runs found payroll `unavailable` to the BFF while Bun and curl reached it, because Node's fetch refuses port 4045 as a bad port (the fetch standard's list), so payroll moved to 4046; the check now prints a source's `reason` and expects the area `payroll`. A fourth run reached its card too, but 26 minutes late: the dev Temporal fired the watch's last timer (started 10:52:32 UTC for 10:53:17) at 11:19:30, with the three checks on time and no activity failed, so the script's 90-second wait past expiry timed out; the third run's timer had fired on time. Not done: a screen-reader pass, real products behind the fixtures, the calendar as a connector.

Fact kinds and dependency visibility, 2026-11-06, main checkout: the product owner asked whether Clew should own fact kinds, how a user would define events, and whether a down or removed connector breaks a chain. Review `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-fact-kinds-ownership-and-dependency-visibility.md`: ownership is per connector and enforced by the registry (not yet by `manifestProblems`), the vocabulary is per vendor, a job cannot cascade by design, a quiet dependency is a visibility gap. Recommended and agreed in principle: a small `clew:` catalogue (specific events, generic attributes, an optional `details`), a promotion rule instead of administrator-defined kinds, and D37's dependents view with a removal warning. No decision written yet.

The mocks declare a fact, a follower and a watch (D55 items 1 and 9), 2026-11-06, worktree `exciting-visvesvaraya-a4387e` (branch `claude/exciting-visvesvaraya-a4387e`, after the watch wiring was merged to `main` as `a595b78`): the product owner asked for the proposed card "make a job fire end to end in a signed-in session" to be done here. Nothing in a session could produce a job before: no mock declared `facts` or `follows`, no binding existed and there was no route. Built: the HR mock declares `hr:absence` (`AbsenceAttributes`: person ref, from, to, extent) and follows it itself with `hr.proposeAbsence` (`creates: 'hr:absence'`; no step when the fact has a `ref`, else one execute `hr.reportAbsence`, deduplicated by Clew's operation id and read back through `hr.absenceLookup`, undone by `hr.withdrawAbsence`); a new `timesheet` mock ("Tidrapporteringen", area `bemanning`) follows with one execute `timesheet.markAbsence` (lookup, undo); a new `payroll` mock ("Lönesystemet", area `lon`) follows with one watch of `payroll.absenceStatus` (`expect status = registered`, `within P1D`) and has no execute at all: it reads the HR mock's reported absences the way a payroll system reads an HR system, once a night (a report dated before today counts as imported), so a report made today stays `pending` until the window ends. Both are bundled in `connectors/registry.ts` and get their governance rows at boot (D23). Bindings: `scripts/job-bind.ts <kind> <capability> [--within] [--units] | --remove | --list | --mocks [--within PT3M]` writes the row and its audit row through the Postgres binding store, one row per kind and follower. Routes (thin, `routes/api.ts`): `POST /api/jobs/assemble` (fact in, proposal out, followers run as the person through `runCapability`), `POST /api/jobs` (decision id plus the echoed proposal, 202; the ledger re-authorizes every step) and `GET /api/jobs/:id`; `JobError` maps to 400 like the other use-case errors. The web shows nothing of the job yet; the expired watch's card is the generic pushed card. Proof: `tests/unit/mock-job.test.ts` (manifests of the three mocks pass `manifestProblems`; a sick report assembles into three steps and writes nothing; one approval writes HR and time reporting once each and reads them back by operation id, payroll's first check leaves the watch waiting and the next one, after the "night", sees it; a watch whose window ends leaves exactly one card "Lönesystemet har inte visat det som bevakades"; undo-all withdraws both writes through the mocks' own undo capabilities); `tests/unit/panel-labels.test.ts` now covers the two mocks (new labels `person`, `extent`, `importedAt`, `checkedAt`, `found`, `result`; a follower's step-draft keys are hidden fields). `scripts/jobs-check.ts` (host, real Keycloak login as Hillevi, like `modules-check.ts`) runs the scenario on localhost:3020: assemble, approve, both writes succeeded and read back, payroll `pending`, then the card in the stream after the window. Run three times on `main` the same morning after the merge (10:17, 10:21 and 10:25 local): each time the three mocks answered, the two writes succeeded and were read back, payroll answered `pending`, `jobWatch` ran the three checks at their committed times and the card "Lönesystemet har inte visat det som bevakades · Bevakas i lönesystemet: 3 kontroller fram till 10:27 visade inget" reached Hillevi's stream under Lön at the window's end; the script's two early failures were its own (the job view counts observations; an earlier run's card matched) and are fixed. Hillevi's dev stream now holds three such cards; `POST /api/mocks/reset` clears them. `scripts/modules-check.ts` expects the manager's two new areas. Open: the job card and panel step list (artboard J) with their a11y specs, the admin page's bindings view, the composer entry (a `creates` propose fanning out from the box), and a scan-driven pushed entry; `hr.whoIsOn` does not yet count a report made through Clew.

Merge of `origin/main` into the shared `main`, 2026-11-06, after D60 landed locally: 25 conflicts, every one origin's Prettier reformat (D57) against this tree's commits (the job watch step wired to Temporal, the served protocol and the manifest reference). Resolved by taking this tree's content for each code file, running `bun run format`, and proving origin's side was a pure reformat: origin's version equalled Prettier over the merge base for every file (the one residual, 16 lines in `modules/executions/adapters/postgres.ts`, was Prettier's own line-breaking of an unchanged statement). HANDOVER was merged as a union of both sides' notes; `docs/context.json` and the decision index were regenerated. Method to reuse: `git show :1:<f>` to a non-ignored path, `prettier --ignore-path /dev/null --write`, diff against `:3:<f>`; Prettier honours `.gitignore`, so a copy under `test-results/` is silently skipped.

The watch step fires without a caller (D55 item 5), 2026-11-06, worktree `exciting-visvesvaraya-a4387e` (branch `claude/exciting-visvesvaraya-a4387e` from `jobs-across-systems`, not merged): a side session asked to wire `executions.observe` to Temporal so a job's watch step runs on its own. Built the smallest wiring that follows D45/D47: `approveJob` commits one `WatchIntent` per watch step (`watch_intent`, migration 0029: the step's absolute checks and end, generation 1, outbox columns) in the transaction that writes the job row and its audit; `watchDispatcher` (the execution outbox loop, now shared as `outbox()` in `modules/executions/dispatch.ts`) claims it under a lease on the same two-second tick as executions and starts the `jobWatch` workflow (`temporal/workflows.ts`, id `watch:<job>:<step>:<generation>`), which sleeps to each committed check and calls the `observeWatch` activity, stops when the step settles, and at the window's end asks again until the ledger's own clock says the window has ended. `observe` is unchanged except that `expired` now pushes a `WatchCard` through the new `ExecutionTx.pushCard` port in the same commit as the settlement; `composition/watch-card.ts` writes the thread, its message, an automatic attention delivery and `thread.pushed`, idempotent by card id, filed under the connector's label and first area, and a Postgres store without that sink refuses to settle rather than pass silently. The watch step now carries the follower's `title` and the system's `source` from the proposal, so the card can name them ("Lönesystemet har inte visat det som bevakades"; `watchExpiredCard` in `executions/domain.ts`). No new authority in Temporal: it never computes a schedule, extends a window or decides a state. Tests: two new unit cases and one extended in `tests/unit/job-executions.test.ts` (intent committed with the job and absent on replay or crash, the outbox's retry and single delivery, the card's words and single push), a contract case for the watch intent on both adapters, a Postgres test for the card sink and the missing-sink refusal, and `apps/server/tests/watch-runtime.test.ts` (PostgreSQL, Temporal, Node, now in `test:durability`): delivery recovers an outage, one watch is seen on its second check, the other reads three times and leaves exactly one card at its end, and a lost acknowledgement redelivers without a second card. Verified in the worktree: `bun run check`, `bun run test:db`, `bun run test:durability`. Not built: a cancel of a running watch, a re-watch (hence generation 1), the job card and panel to artboard J, and nothing shows the expired watch's card in a signed-in session yet because the mock connectors declare no watches.

The served protocol (D60; written and first committed as D57 in `e49ee98`, renumbered at the second commit because origin had taken D57 to D59 meanwhile), 2026-11-06, main checkout: the product owner asked for an OpenAPI-style interface to the connector protocol, something visual to open or point a tool at, shipped with the Clew image so it matches the instance rather than a published SDK, and asked whether manifests should be versioned. Built and accepted as D60: `GET /api/protocol` (RapiDoc page from `node_modules`, English, no CDN, fonts off), `/api/protocol/openapi.json` (`openApiDocument()` in `packages/connectors/src/protocol.ts`: both sides of the protocol, `components.schemas` = the bundle's `$defs`, `x-clew-protocol` with current/accepted/retired, Clew-side server from `PUBLIC_URL` else the request origin), `/api/protocol/connector-protocol.schema.json`, and `POST /api/protocol/manifest?id=` (`previewManifest` in `packages/connectors/src/preview.ts`: every problem or what Clew would make of the document; `manifestProblems` factored out of `parseManifest` in `remote.ts` so loader, checker, admin page and dry run share one list; a retired or unknown protocol number is named with the accepted ones and the reason). Mounted before the readiness guard. `docs/protocol/connector-protocol.openapi.json` is generated and checked beside the schema bundle. Versioning policy written down in D60 item 4 (additive keeps the number; a break accepts N and N-1 for a window; a security change retires at once, as 1 was): `PROTOCOL_ACCEPTED`/`PROTOCOL_RETIRED` in `protocol.ts`; no N-1 adapter exists yet because no 3 exists. Tests: `tests/unit/protocol-served.test.ts` (16 cases). The viewer page is outside the WCAG suite (mocked `/api`), so it was scanned once live: a Node script in the pinned Playwright service (`sh scripts/compose.sh --profile a11y run --rm -T a11y node <script>` against `http://host.docker.internal:3020/api/protocol`, which Vite's `allowedHosts` admits; `http://app:5173` does not work from that container) running `@axe-core/playwright` with the WCAG 2.0 to 2.2 AA tags and listing requests outside the origin. First run: a `color-contrast` on RapiDoc's path label, `label` on its request-param inputs and request-body textareas, `scrollWidth` 360 at 320 px from RapiDoc's host `min-width`. Fixed on the page itself (try-it off, a host `min-width: 0`, a `::part(label-operation-path)` colour and wrap, a script that labels the textareas by operation): zero violations, zero external requests, 320 at 320. Repeat it when `rapidoc` is bumped. Local `main` was fast-forwarded to `origin/main` first (D56 came from origin). Later the same day, after the product owner asked whether a `tsconfig`-style reference would be easier to grasp than OpenAPI: D60 item 5, the manifest field by field. `packages/connectors/src/reference.ts` walks `ConnectorManifestSchema` into entries (path, type in words, presence, constraints, example from telefonbok/hello or a handwritten one, rules), renders Markdown (`docs/generated/manifest-reference.md`, from `scripts/docs.ts`, decision numbers linked when unique) and, after the product owner asked for one look across Clew and pointed at the hand-written page's cut-off sidebar, a web page: `/protocol/manifest` in `apps/web/src/pages/protocol.tsx`, public like the login, Astryx's `documentation-technical` template (Outline in a sticky LayoutPanel on the left and a 1280 px content width after the product owner's corrections to the first rendering, Selector on a phone, a TextInput filter over the outline with a spoken count, Heading/Markdown/MetadataList/CodeBlock, and TabList/Tab for a recipe's JSON and TypeBox views (the TypeBox source generated from the JSON by `typeboxModule()` and round-tripped through TypeBox in the unit test; the job recipe is now three manifests plus the FactEnvelope and both FollowResponses, validated against the declared schemas); rules as Markdown lists since a ListItem keeps to one line), reading `GET /api/protocol/manifest-reference.json`; `/api/protocol/manifest-reference` redirects to it. The reference types live in `@clew/contracts`; `Outline` and `CodeBlock` are new re-exports in `packages/ui`. Every manifest field got a description in `protocol.ts` and `packages/contracts` (the contracts `object()` helper now takes one), which the unit suite requires. `RULES` is the prose of what pairs with what; `tests/unit/manifest-reference.test.ts` (12 cases) breaks each loader rule and reads the problem, makes the registry refuse the two registry rules, loads the handwritten examples and the job recipe's fragments as manifests, and checks both renderings. `tests/a11y/protocol.spec.ts` covers it in the WCAG suite (desktop outline and headings, phone Selector and reflow, axe both); the a11y harness builds the reference from the real schema in `support.ts`. The integrator workflow against a test installation is a section in `docs/guides/testing-and-deployment.md`.
Calendar fixtures, 2026-11-06 (follows the D69 review): the product owner asked for richer mock data. `fixtureDirectory()` and `.devcontainer/calendar/seed.php` now hold the same seven rooms, from 2 to 40 seats, with Swedish equipment names, and `scripts/calendar-seed.ts` books ten meetings with rooms. Mikael's "Verksamhetsdialog" asks for Eken while Hillevi holds it, so a declined room can be seen in the app. Capacity and equipment are still only shown in the booking dialog; nothing checks or filters on them. Restart the `graph` service to load new rooms, since it reads them at start.

D69 review, 2026-11-06: ten findings from the review of the adapter's PR are applied. A declined room leaves `room` empty, no undo is offered while a room is pending because the ledger cannot carry a fresh revision, a declined invitation stores the revision that holds the answer so its replacement can cancel it, all-day times other than a UTC midnight are refused, `accept` tolerates a queued answer, and `bun run test:calendar` fails when Nextcloud is down. `readLimited` in `packages/calendar/src/http.ts` is now shared by both adapters. Telling a person about a room's late no is not built. Internal details were removed from this file and the Qlik review, but they remain in commit `30b0eee`.

D69, 2026-11-06 (continues the room booking note below): the product owner asked whether Microsoft could be mocked with everything local and said to build it. `packages/calendar/src/microsoft365.ts` is the adapter, `fake-graph.ts` beside it the fake, `tests/calendar-provider-contract.ts` the first adapter contract: eight cases pass on the fake in `bun run check` and on the real Nextcloud in the new `bun run test:calendar`, and an approved booking with a room passes through the execution ledger. `sh scripts/graph.sh start` runs the `graph` compose service and seeds it; a scripted run booked, saw `pending` turn `accepted` and cancelled over the network. Not run: the BFF and the UI on `CALENDAR_ADAPTER=microsoft365`, and any real tenant. No new dependency, so nothing for section 3. The room's answer changes the event's revision, so a 10-minute undo taken before it is refused and needs a fresh read; D69 records this and the other unverified points. Review finding 8 (eight guests and a room) applies to this adapter too, since it keeps the same limit.

Manager statistics and Qlik review, 2026-11-06 (no code): the product owner asked how big-boss-board's cron jobs get sick-leave and economy figures and whether the data can come directly from Qlik. `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-manager-statistics-from-qlik.md` traces the chain from the data warehouse through a daily Qlik export on an internal file share to the two CronJobs, and compares the file feed, Qlik's Engine API, the data warehouse and reading big-boss-board. Recommendation: keep the `Period,Enhet,Mått,Kolumn,Mätvärde` contract and ask the Qlik owners for one förvaltning-level object; a direct read depends on a service identity and on Qlik holding closed months. Argo CD, `oc`, Qlik and SQL Server were not reachable, so live state is unverified. No decision taken; six open questions in the review, and D54 plus a `series` decision are needed before Clew can draw any of it.

Sundsvall's room booking, 2026-11-06 (no code, follows the planning systems note below): the product owner asked which system the municipality uses to book rooms. Confirmed for bookings by the public: Vitec Actor Smartbook at `sundsvall.actorsmartbook.se`, which sundsvall.se links as its booking and grant system and whose own page names the supplier; it covers sports facilities and other rental premises, and the municipality's `web-app-business-center` links it as well. Some premises are booked outside it: sundsvall.se has separate pages for Opalens aula, Kulturmagasinet and the party and meeting premises. Still unconfirmed: staff meeting rooms. Sundsvall's public repositories only show "Lokalbokning" as an item on a manager's onboarding checklist in the Cypress test fixtures of `web-app-boarding`, the digital onboarding checklist, with no system named; a fixture is not proof that the live checklists say this. Nothing confirms that Sundsvall's rooms are room mailboxes, so Exchange room mailboxes stay the working assumption until IT confirms. No public Smartbook API was found.

Sundsvall's planning systems, 2026-11-06 (no code, same session as D68): the product owner asked which systems the municipality uses for meetings and occupancy. Confirmed by a Timecare case study (PDF dated 2026-07-08): the staffing unit runs Timecare Pool for about 2,000 temporary employees and 165,000 booked shifts in 2025, and two RPA robots sync Heroma to it. Heroma as master data and Microsoft 365 are the repository's own findings (D10, D11). Unconfirmed: hemtjänst planning, ordinary shift scheduling, staff room booking and school timetables. Shift workers' availability is not in Outlook, so a future slot search needs more than one availability source. `RPA-metakatalogen` is public and may mean Metakatalogen has no API; check this before relying on D68's provisioner reading it.

D68, 2026-11-06 (continues the two reviews below): the product owner decided that Sundsvall's Metakatalogen is the source and that people are pushed into Clew through standard SCIM. D68 records the contract: each installation runs its own provisioner, which maps its source to required user attributes and group keys in `externalId` (`unit:`, `unit-manager:`, `capability:`, `protected`); Clew holds no source knowledge and no validity dates. Documentation only, nothing built. Must land first: narrow `authorizationVersion`, and narrow `granted()` so the nightly scan does not run for a whole municipality. Open: production identity provider, protected people, and Metakatalogen's stable id.

Users, groups, roles and SCIM review, 2026-11-06 (same session as the calendar review below): the product owner asked to settle identity before the booking idea. No product code changed. `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-users-groups-roles-and-scim.md` records that SCIM and groups do not exist, people come from `scripts/auth-setup.ts`, one grant row limits a person to one unit and role, and `authorizationVersion` hashes the whole person and grant row, so a name change or a new column would stop durable work. Open for the product owner: the source of unit and manager role, Keycloak as broker or direct Entra, and whether people without a Clew login are provisioned.

WCAG suite by hand (D59), 2026-11-06: the product owner asked to stop the WCAG run and the wait for it while the product is in its lab phase. `.github/workflows/a11y.yml` keeps its `push` and `pull_request` triggers as comments and runs through `workflow_dispatch` only; its limit went from 15 to 25 minutes because run 42187541853 for `0857b46` was cancelled at 15 in the profile step after the main suite had passed. Touched specs still run per change. The triggers and the D51 gate come back in the run-up to a release.

Grid click starts a booking (D58), 2026-11-06 (on `main` in the shared tree, pushed at the product owner's request): the product owner found that a click in the calendar did nothing and that only a drag worked. `selectMinDistance` ignored a press without movement. `EventCalendar` now takes FullCalendar's `dateClick` behind the same `onSelectRange`: an hour from the row clicked, 09:00–10:00 for a day in the month or the all-day row, and a click is heard outside office hours too. A click on an entry, a drag, the phone and the keyboard path are unchanged. Temporary mutations removed two guards written first (a one-snap check in `select` and `selectAllow` for whole days): `dateClick` comes after `select` and wins, and `selectConstraint` already stops a drag across whole days. Verified: eight new cases in `tests/a11y/calendar.spec.ts`, whose four mouse-click cases fail without `dateClick`; `calendar.spec.ts`, `screens.calendar.spec.ts` and `touch.spec.ts` (34 passed, 11 skipped); `bun run check`. The product owner tried it in the running app and sent it on, which accepted D58 with its hour and its nine o'clock. Not done: a screen-reader pass.

Calendar and manager's team review, 2026-11-06: the product owner asked how the calendar integrates with a manager's team and what booking support with others would take. No product code changed. `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-calendar-and-manager-teams.md` records the state: the engine is sound, but the invite list is a fixture in `packages/calendar/src/accounts.ts`, only managers can invite and only inside their unit, slot search exists only for utvecklingssamtal, the assistant has no free-busy read or booking proposal, and Microsoft 365 is unbuilt. Open for the product owner: who may invite whom, and whose free-busy a person may see.

Capability ids without the grey bar, 2026-11-06: the product owner found the grey `Code` background under each function title on a connector's Funktioner tab ugly; `VStack` also stretched it across the whole cell. `apps/web/src/pages/admin/connector.tsx` now uses Astryx `Text type="code" color="secondary"` in the desktop table and the phone list. The model pages (`model.tsx`, `models.tsx`, `provider.tsx`) got the same treatment for model ids. `Code` stays in `check-result.tsx`. `admin.spec.ts` and `models.spec.ts` passed (axe on the tab at desktop and phone); no screen reader pass, the text and its order are unchanged.

Prettier (D57), 2026-11-06: the product owner asked whether the repository had a Prettier configuration; it had no formatter at all, so Prettier 3.9.10 was added with `.prettierrc.json` (single quotes, bare arrow parameters, width 120), `bun run format`, and `bun run format:check` as the first step of `bun run check`. The glob covers TypeScript, JavaScript and CSS; Markdown, JSON, YAML and SQL stay out. The first format took two passes and grew the code files from 27,788 to about 56,000 lines. `connectors/telefonbok/manifest.mjs`, uncommitted work from the note below, was held back at first and formatted last at the product owner's word. Verified after formatting: the whole of `bun run check` (format check, typecheck, dependency rules, purity, 365 unit tests, 4 Node tests, documentation) and `bun run build`, the latter before that last file. The WCAG suite, `test:db` and `test:durability` were not run. Open branches, including the worktrees `neutral-tema` and `ux-granskning`, will conflict with the reformat and need `bun run format` before they merge. The reformat is commit `334f580`, listed in `.git-blame-ignore-revs`.

Phone book readability, 2026-11-06: at the product owner's request, moved the declarative contract from `connectors/telefonbok/connector.mjs` to `manifest.mjs`. Each capability now has a named block with expanded input/output and view declarations; `directoryCapability.view.kind` owns the table choice. `connector.mjs` preserves its public exports, expands the read and HTTP flows, names the output mappings, shares the person lookup and removes unused verifier imports and the stale HS256 comment. The Dockerfile copies the new manifest. README and the integrator walkthrough explain where to change views versus source mapping. No source adapter, protocol, authorization or UI behavior changed. Verified under the global resource supervisor in the devcontainer: 38 existing tests across `telefonbok-connector`, `views` and `conversation`; both changed modules pass Node syntax checks; manifest JSON matches the existing generated contract exactly; `bun scripts/docs.ts --check` and dependency rules scoped to `connectors/telefonbok` pass. No full suite, browser tests, server startup or image build was run. No new dependency.

Stress test: one event, work in several systems (D55), 2026-11-06, worktree `jobs` (branch `jobs-across-systems`): the product owner asked how Clew handles an employee's sick report that means work in the HR system, time reporting, payroll, a 1:1 and a sick-leave pattern rule, with the constraints that connectors are standalone and Clew writes no code per flow, and asked what happens when a follower's contract drifts. Reviewed against the canvas "Clew startsida · dagen i ett svep" artboard J (https://claude.ai/artifact/9xn43bAwXy2VioBqiG3xc6): the ledger approved one operation on one connector, a card was one system's (D35), watches and generic assembly did not exist. Review `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-cross-system-work-stress-test.md`; decision D55 (proposed): facts and `follows` in the manifest, administrator bindings in the database, generic fan-out assembly, one approval, one execution or watch per step, D37's drift rule for fact kinds. Built as proof, no UI: protocol fields `facts`/`follows`/`creates` and the `FactEnvelope`/`StepDraft`/`FollowResponse` shapes; `Registry.followers`/`offered`; `modules/jobs` (bindings, assembler); `modules/executions` `approveJob`/`undoJob`/`observe` with `prepare`/`commit` factored out of `approve`; tables `execution_job` and `follow_binding` (migration 0028); `tests/unit/job-executions.test.ts` (20 cases) and two store-contract cases on both adapters. `bun run check` and `bun run test:db` green in the worktree. Naming hazard: `composition/jobs.ts` is the scheduler's job rows; the new wiring is `composition/jobs-across-systems.ts`. Not built: route, card and panel to artboard J, Administration › Anslutna system › Följer, the Temporal timer for `observe`, D19 ingest, personal binding overrides; the names-in-cards tension (F11) is noted, not decided.

Brief rows open the card beside the landing (D50 amended), 2026-11-05 (on `main` at `025a71c`, re-landed on `origin/main` at `c6adebd` because the same work had been pushed as D50): the product owner clicked "Granska 3" on the landing and asked why it switched to Att göra, which they found confusing. It was as the canvas drew it (Hem's "Granska" leads to artboard 3) and as D50 was built, but D50 item 4 already lets the landing name a card in its address, so the brief's title, "Granska", "Visa förslaget" and a matching "Menar du" row now lead to `/?card=` through `withCard` (`cardHref` in `pages/start.tsx`) and the card opens in the landing's own panel. The bell, the reminder toast and the rail's search still lead to `/todo?card=`. D50 has an amendment section and its status line says so. Tests: `structure.spec.ts` asserts the new hrefs and the "Menar du" address; `todo.spec.ts` has a new case at 1440 px (the card beside the landing as a complementary region, the h1 and the box still there, no tabs, axe, Esc back to the row). The canvas's Hem artboard still draws the old arrow. **In the same session, the calendar's reads in the panel**: the product owner showed "Dagens möten" opened from the landing's Kalender chips as raw label/value pairs (`Datum`, `events` with Nextcloud ids, `gaps`). `PanelBody` recognised only `calendar.day`'s shape (`from`, `events`, `proposals`); `calendar.today` answers `{ date, events, gaps }` and `calendar.week` answers `{ from, events }` without proposals, so both fell through to the generic dump. `components/panel.tsx` now draws today as the `DayStrip` with the free gaps named under it and the week as the `EventList` with a heading per day (D32's family, no new component). The mock serves both reads (`support.ts`), and `views.spec.ts` has two new cases with axe. Not seen in the signed-in product yet.

Generic mock systems (D52), 2026-11-05 (on `main` at `8093725`, in the shared tree): the product owner asked that the mocks in `packages/connectors` stop naming vendors. `heroma`, `raindance` and `treserva` are now `hr` ("Personalsystemet"), `finance` ("Ekonomisystemet") and `incidents` ("Avvikelsesystemet"), kept as generic stand-ins for the kinds of systems a municipality runs; `kommunikation` is unchanged. Refs (`hr:emp:1001`), topics, capability names (`finance.invoices`), the package exports, the registry, the fixed answers, the figure owners, the admin page's example id and address, the fixtures in `tests/a11y/data.ts`, the unit tests and the check scripts follow, and the synthetic suppliers are made up (Skolgrossisten, Energibolaget). The mocks' own Swedish says "i personalsystemet"; a source label stays the proper name ("Källa: Personalsystemet", "Kortet väntar på Ekonomisystemet"). Migration `0027_generic_mock_connectors` (data only, snapshot copied from 0026) renames the ids in `connector`, `module_state`, `thread` (connector, topic, trigger source, items, actions, receipt), `thread_message.meta`, `execution`, `refresh_request` and `work_occurrence`; audit rows keep history. The dev database was migrated with `bun run db:init`: 103 hr, 22 finance and 40 incidents cards came through and no old id remains. `docs/architecture/` names kinds of systems now ("an HR system without an API"); decisions, reviews and older session notes keep the vendor names as historical records, which D52 says. Verified in the devcontainer: `bun run check` (340 unit tests, four Node tests, docs generated and checked) and `bun run test:db` (42). WCAG, on the host: the seven touched specs (admin, structure, models, menu, reflow, axe, executions) gave 151 passed and 8 skipped in the shared tree and, rebased onto origin with `card-actions` added, 170 passed and 8 skipped. Not done: the live page was not opened after the migration; no screen reader pass, because no flow changed.

Commit language and the local main, 2026-11-05: the product owner decided that commit messages are English from now on, subject and body; `CLAUDE.md` and the Language row in section 2 say so, and commits already pushed keep their Swedish subjects. The same session pushed the workspace structure (D50) to `origin/main` and then replayed the two commits that had been made on top of its superseded local copy (the calendar per demo user, and the WCAG suite as CI's gate) onto `origin/main`, with their messages reworded in English because they were not pushed yet. The suite decision was written as D50 and is D51, because D50 was taken when it landed. After the push the whole WCAG suite was run on the pushed commit `1020b76`: 280 passed, none failed. Later the same evening the two commits and this rule were rebased once more onto `12ec21f` (the card beside the landing), with the handover's log and the generated indexes merged, `bun run check` green (341 unit tests), and pushed.

Läget's figures reviewed (D54 proposed), 2026-11-06 (on `main`, docs only, no code changed): the product owner asked whether Läget's tiles are mocked and whether a connector should provide them. They are: three values are literal strings in `apps/server/src/modules/figures/domain.ts`, the ids are a fixed union in `FigureSchema`, and the tile's question is hard-coded in `start.tsx`, while the protocol's `/clew/figure` route (D21) is implemented by every bundled connector and drawn nowhere since D50 took the areas out of the rail. `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-laget-figures-review.md` holds the findings; D54 proposes that Läget draws the areas' figures from the connectors (one tile per area that answers one, the richer tile as D38's `figures` view kind, `opens` naming the read capability the tile leads to, one administrator switch per area, nothing for the person to arrange), status proposed until the product owner decides. Built the same day on the product owner's yes ("Yes, add the views"): the mock reads declare the views a real connector would (`packages/connectors/src/mock/*`): the invoices and the budget position (now with a `label`) for finance, absence per person (its row opens the pattern) and the leave requests for HR with `modelReads: 'summary'` so health data stays out of the model, the deviations and the notices as lists. `tests/unit/views.test.ts` holds every mock read over rows to a view that keeps the rules. The absent-today, shift and balance reads keep the generic panel: their outputs are not rows and no view kind fits them yet. Seen in the screenshot that prompted the review: Mikael, an employee, got a budget tile from the fixed list and a model answer of no access; the tile goes with D54, the answer would now be the budget view for a manager.

Hem is the start page (D53), 2026-11-06 (worktree `hem`, branch `hem` from `main` at `0acceba`, not merged): the product owner found the landing empty of emphasis (the sentence and the footer's day line hidden, a 720 px column at 1440) and asked for design examples thought from the personas. Four directions and seven flow artboards drawn on the canvas **Clew startsida · dagen i ett svep** (link in section 0); the product owner chose A, had the summary sentence, the suggestion chips, the shift sentence on the phone and the name "Pågår utan dig" taken out, then said "Yes, build A". Built: `/` is Hem and `/todo` is gone (`main.tsx`, `nav.tsx`, `shell.tsx`, `reminders.tsx`); the greeting is the h1 with the day under it, the box anchored under it, then the tabs (Behöver dig, Väntar på andra, Inte nu, Klart) with the area filter and the attention tip on one line, the groups, Läget and the areas with their cards (`pages/start.tsx`); beside the column `Today` without its gap sentence and the new `components/since.tsx` (what closed since the day began, what waits on others, the next review) as the panel at rest (`usePanel`'s `rest`, a `LayoutPanel` named Vid sidan av) from 1336 px, after the day's work below that; the box on Hem takes `about` and says Om kortet with Ny fråga i stället when a card is open (`composer.tsx`). `Brief`, `PrimaryAction` and `digestSentence` deleted; `BriefSkeleton` stays for the first read. Every a11y spec that opened `/todo` opens `/`; the D39 tests on the box's block are replaced; new cases in `structure.spec.ts`, `todo.spec.ts` and `answer.spec.ts`. Not built, named in D53: the phone's shift strip (no schedule capability), the panel's tabs, a job across connectors, the "something happened" mark. Owed: the full WCAG suite in CI after the merge, a manual screen-reader pass, a look at the built page against artboard A.

WCAG suite as CI's gate (D51), 2026-11-05 (on `main` at `8093725`, in the shared tree): the product owner asked whether the WCAG suite could stop running on every change. Measured first: the full suite on one worker, `sh scripts/a11y.sh --workers 1`, 287 tests, 247 passed, 40 skipped, 458 s wall clock, green. The config was then set to `workers: '50%'` and a three-run trial started; the product owner stopped it and chose CI-only, so the config is back at `workers: 1`, unchanged. D51: a change runs only the specs it touches (`--timeout 15000`), the full suite runs in CI on every code push and pull request and is the gate, and because the GitHub plan cannot block a merge the previous landing's check is read before the next landing on `main`. `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/architecture/testing.md` and `docs/maintainers/local-development.md` say so. Not done: the parallel-worker trial, so no number for a four-worker run; D49 is taken by a file in another session's worktree, hence D51. (Written as D50; renumbered to D51 when this commit was replayed onto `origin/main`, where the workspace structure had taken D50.)

Calendar per demo user, 2026-11-05: `mikael` (systemförvaltare) was the one sign-in persona with no calendar; `bindCalendar` threw "Ditt konto har ingen kalender kopplad." for him and Nextcloud had no account. He now has both: `.devcontainer/calendar/seed.php` creates the Nextcloud user `mikael` (password pattern `calendar-<id>-local-only`, group `calendar-staff`, calendar `personal`) and `packages/calendar/src/accounts.ts` binds him over CalDAV like Hillevi and Hassan. `scripts/calendar-seed.ts` also gives Hassan and Mikael meetings in this week and next (five each), appended after the original fixtures so existing UIDs `clew-fixture-<week>-<index>` keep their index and the seed stays idempotent; Hassan's calendar was provisioned but empty before. Run `sh scripts/calendar.sh seed` from the host after pulling; it was run here and PROPFIND on `/remote.php/dav/calendars/<id>/` answers 207 for all three personas with 12, 5 and 5 events. `bun run check` passes (340 unit tests). Not done: a signed-in pass as Mikael on the calendar page (no dev server was running in the container; the binding is read at the next `bun run dev`), and the unit members Anna, Erik, Maria and Johan still cannot sign in, by design.

Workspace structure (D50), 2026-11-05 (worktree `samtalet-pa-ytan`, cut from `main` at `3853116`, rebased onto `main` at `f6a7cb2`, onto `origin/main` at `b73ae1d` and again at `e36ac9d`, pushed at the product owner's request): the product owner asked for a fresh take on the UX: should Clew work with chat sessions, how do they relate to the static views, and is a side panel needed. **Explored on a canvas first** (**Clew · ett sammanhängande flöde**, link in section 1; review `docs/reviews/2026-11-05-ux-flow-exploration.md`): two models were drawn, A with the conversation in the panel on every page and B with the conversation always in the column, and the product owner chose B. The canvas shows B only. **D50 written and built** (first written as D45, then D48, then D49; other records took those numbers on `main` meanwhile), web only: no server, contract or dependency change. `apps/web/src/panel-address.ts` (new) names what the panel holds in the address. `components/panel.tsx` is `usePanel` and `PanelBody` in place of `PanelView`: a `LayoutPanel` in the page's `end` slot where it fits, the drawer where it does not, with the focus rules in D50 item 3. `components/conversation-context.tsx`: `ConversationProvider` takes no props, reads its route itself and sits above the pages in `main.tsx`, inside the protected tree that a policy change resets; `submit` and `ask` take where the question was asked (`AskFrom`), `/conversations/new` is a conversation on its way, "Stäng samtalet" leads back to the page it was asked on, and a question where no transcript is on the page starts a new conversation (it used to be appended to the one just closed). `components/composer.tsx`: the place `footer` is `page`, with `about` for what the placeholder names and what goes along. `components/nav.tsx` and `shell.tsx`: the rail is Nytt samtal, the search, Att göra, Verktyg, Samtal, then Administration with its two sections and the account row; `ShellNav.tools`, the Områden row, and the rows for the groups and the piles are gone. `pages/start.tsx` is rewritten: the modes are `fraga` and `todo`; Att göra with tabs (`TabList` with `role="tablist"`; a menu on a phone, because a strip that scrolls sideways fails 1.4.10 and a selector is not 44 px under a finger), the area filter, rows whose title opens the card in the panel, cards in the list on a phone, and the source status lines from the reliability work; the landing is as before with "Allt i Att göra"; the conversation has the panel in its `Layout`. `pages/tool.tsx`: the box at the foot, the row's view through `usePanel`. `card.tsx` and `thread.tsx`: `beside` gives a card in the panel the id `card-<thread>`, a row has `isOpen`. The reminder toast and the bell lead to `/todo?card=`. **Folded into the UX review of sessions 43 to 45 (D48, one next step; the other D50's neighbour D49, one time action per card; the reminders under the bell)**, which landed on `origin/main` while this was built and was written against the overview: its words (Nytt samtal, Sammanfattning, Kort), the row with one next step and the rest behind Fler val, the open card that keeps its one time action in view, the headings without captions, the one-line box at a page's foot whose placeholder says what it does, `titled()` and the five latest conversations in the rail are all kept, on the new structure. Three things of that review's moved because the overview is gone: the tip "Så här hör jag av mig" sits beside Att göra's heading, Läget's figures follow the groups under Behöver dig on every screen, and a row whose decision is on its way puts its step under the line so the title keeps its words. A card in the panel keeps the focus when its own button changes it. The overview's "Dagen" column and its Klart i dag bar are not on Att göra. **Tests**: `tests/a11y/todo.spec.ts` is new (11: the tabs, the filter, a phone, the panel beside the page at 1440 px with its focus and Esc, a view over a card, asking from Att göra and from a tool's page, the narrower screen, a new conversation after a closed one); every spec that named `/overview`, `/areas/…`, the piles, `/views/…` or the Områden row follows, the ones from `main` and from the review included; `snoozeFirst` takes Inte nu from the card where it is in view and from Fler val in a row; `MockApi.asked` records what the box sent; `screens.flow.spec.ts` takes the pictures for the canvas comparison on `CLEW_SCREENS=1`; `screens.review.spec.ts` has its addresses mapped but its pictures were not retaken. **Documentation**, to the standard on `main`: the decision is one new record with an unused number; the five earlier records it changes (D28, D31, D34, D42, D43) keep their bodies and say so in their status lines; the review ends in an Outcome section; `docs/reference/status.md` names the panel and the box on a tool's page under Views and menu tools; the decision and review indexes and `docs/context.json` are generated (`bun run docs:generate`). **Verified**: on the tree rebased onto `b73ae1d`, `bun run check` green (340 unit and web tests, types, dependencies, purity, the documentation check) and the WCAG suite whole, 276 passed and none failed. `origin/main` then moved four commits; on the tree that was pushed `bun run check` is green and the specs that rebase touched pass (card-actions, shell, announcements, keyboard, todo, touch, structure, axe: 133 tests); the whole suite was not run again before the push, because the remote kept moving. The pictures from `screens.flow` were compared with the canvas before the two rebases: the rail, Att göra with the card beside the list, the conversation with a view and with a card beside it, the tool's page with the box, and the phone agreed in structure; they were not retaken after the review's restyling. **Differs from the canvas**: a row in Att göra carries its next step and Fler val (the canvas draws title, source and day only); the rail has the heading Verktyg over Kalender and the tools; the box on Att göra and on a tool's page spans under the panel; a card in the panel is drawn as the card it is, in a `Card`. **Not done**: a look at it in the running product; a manual screen-reader pass; the items D50 lists as not built (the panel's tabs for what a conversation has opened, the box on the calendar page, a conversation's origin shown on its card or tool page, a resizable panel); a place for the day beside Att göra.

Session 46, 2026-11-05 and 2026-11-06 (same worktree and branch, moved forward to `main` at `6b540ba`, which brought in D50 to D55): the rows under a rail row that opens now hang from a line (D56), because they were at the same x as the parent's label and read as its equals. `SubRows` in `apps/web/src/components/shell.tsx` is an Astryx composition of `HStack`, `StackItem` and a vertical `Divider`, with no custom CSS, used for Administration, Fler verktyg and the account. Tests: `tests/a11y/shell.spec.ts`, "a row that opens marks its rows", and the Fler verktyg test in `tests/a11y/menu.spec.ts`. The capture `74-rail-open` no longer clicks Områden, which D50 took out of the rail. Before D50 the rail's non-scrolling foot covered the rows above it on short screens; measured after D50 the foot is at most 222 px, and 146 px remain for the scrolling list at 1280 × 500. The local database was also brought up to 27 of 27 migrations this session, in the main checkout. Open: two tests in `tests/a11y/keyboard.spec.ts`, "a card that receives focus from the page shows it" and "Inte nu moves focus to the next card", fail now and then on their own. Repeated six times each, they failed 3 of 24 on a clean copy of `1020b76` and 4 of 24 with this change, so the line is not the cause. With logging added in `apps/web/src/pages/start.tsx` they passed 44 of 44, so the cause is not established. The suite has no retries.

Session 45, 2026-11-05 (same worktree and branch, pushed to `main` at the product owner's request, who then had `main` checked out in the main tree): the bell's list, written in session 40 and left uncommitted on `feature/notiser-och-kontonamn`, is now on `main`. The reminders hang from the bell as an Astryx `Popover` with `presentation="adaptive"`, a `BottomSheet` with the same content on a touch phone (D39, last amendment). `apps/web/src/components/reminders.tsx` owns the bell, its state and its query; the `Drawer` and Genvägar are gone, and `Shell` keeps its own `threadsQuery` for the toasts. Carried from main's side: `Banner collapsible={false}`, the word Kort in the card's name, and an empty state that speaks of a chosen time, since D49 lets Inte nu come back here. The uncommitted tree was saved first as the local branch `backup/notiser-wip-2026-11-05`, because the rest of it was already on `main` and a checkout needed it clean.

Session 44, 2026-11-05 (same worktree and branch, pushed to `main` at the product owner's request): the question left open by session 43, whether Inte nu and Påminn mig should be one choice, was handed back with "Gör det val som är mest logiskts och mest användbart". Settled as D49: one time action per card, `Inte nu` on an open card and in the open thread, `Påminn mig` on a card that waits on someone, and one way back for both, first under Nu with the badge and the toast. A snooze with no time, sent only by the assistant's confirmed action, does the same at the next 06:00. Two defects surfaced in the code that became the only use of `remind`: it overwrote a real `waitingOn` with `påminnelsen 10:16`, so a card lost who it waited on and Ångra put it in Behöver dig; fixed, and the trigger keeps `Väntade på …` when the reminder fires. A snooze broken by activity ended its timer without a word on the scan path; it now admits a `woken:` delivery. An open page needs no timer of its own: the database triggers from migration 0019 raise the change signal, and `/api/changes` re-reads the stream. Known and left: several cards coming back at once give one toast each; `Om 10 sekunder` still shows the word demo, now on the waiting card's picker alone. D17, D28, D29 and D48 carry amendments, review item 11 says what happened. Not tested: a real Temporal timer firing a snooze end to end, a real toast from the running stack, a screen reader, real devices.

Session 43, 2026-11-05 (worktree `ux-granskning`, branch `feature/ux-granskning` from `main` at `0c314c8`, merged with `main` at `f6a7cb2` and pushed to `main` at the product owner's request; the decision was D47 in this branch's commits and became D48 in the merge, because `main` had taken D47 for durable identities; `feature/notiser-och-kontonamn` and its dirty tree were not touched, and it will conflict in `apps/web/src/components/nav.tsx`, `shell.tsx`, `apps/web/src/pages/admin/shell.tsx`, `HANDOVER.md`, `docs/decisions/README.md`, D34, D39 and `tests/a11y/shell.spec.ts`, `branding.spec.ts`, `support.ts`): the product owner asked for a full UX review of every part of the interface, with screenshots, without leaving Astryx, then approved every finding and asked for all of it to be fixed. `docs/reviews/2026-11-05-ux-review.md` holds the method, the measurements, the findings and what was not inspected. The verdict: the foundation is good, the problem was amount (one fact said up to five times, the design's reasoning printed as captions, every control on every thread). D48 is the direction: one visible next step, filled only when it is due today, the rest behind one `Fler val`; type base 15; 44 px under a coarse pointer through the theme's `adaptations`; the area as a word; a calmer overview, rail and footer box; Funktioner, Kort, Nytt samtal and Sammanfattning as the words. `GET /api/cap/:name` now returns the connector's `source`, `viewAnswer` no longer takes the time, and `Utseende` follows the computer by default. New specs: `tests/a11y/card-actions.spec.ts`, `touch.spec.ts`, `appearance.spec.ts`; `tests/a11y/screens.review.spec.ts` retakes all captures with `CLEW_SCREENS=1`, and each capture's JSON in `metrics/` now lists text cut off (`truncated`) and words on two lines (`brokenWords`), for the whole height of the page: the 15 px base had quietly cut a primary button and broken a word in the phone book, which neither the suite nor a reading of the code showed. `hiddenFocusStops` waits for a closing tooltip. Playwright's role level reads an `h3` as 3 whatever its `aria-level` says, so a test of a drawn-small `h1` uses the `heading()` helper. Verified on the final code: `bun run check` exits 0, and the full WCAG suite passes on both profiles, Neutral 266 and Sundsvall 266, both on the tree merged with `main`. The model dialog test in `models.spec.ts` failed twice in about ten full runs in axe's contrast check on its button, most likely measured halfway through the 125 ms fade from disabled (not reproduced on demand); the test now waits for the fade. Owed: comparison with the design canvases, a manual screen reader pass of the ⋯ sub-menu, a pass with real data and real devices. Left as a product decision: whether Inte nu and Påminn mig become one picker (D48, Not changed).

Documentation and upstream reconciliation, 2026-11-05: after implementation commits `50c7e24` and `1ac4d02`, fetched upstream from the host and merged through `0c314c8`, preserving both histories. Resolved overlaps in the connector checker, shared shell, browser fixtures and documentation; retained protocol 2 transport/signing/governance and durable delivery together with structured administrator check results, account/navigation changes and the Neutral default. Updated the new upstream checker test to the current signing fixture. The documentation remains aimed at integrators, with executable examples and generated references. Preserved all 53 decision files, including the additional D44–D46 collisions, and regenerated the machine context and indexes. Validation and limits: `docs/reviews/2026-11-05-documentation-upstream-reconciliation.md`. Implementation completion remains distinct from real-source and operational acceptance; no pilot was selected.

Merged-tree validation: `bun run check` passed with 340 unit/web tests, four Node runtime tests, types/dependencies/purity and documentation checks. The full Neutral browser suite passed 237 tests, with 34 optional screenshot cases skipped. A later alternate-profile run exposed an appearance-test navigation race; waiting for the target route and expected computed theme preserved the assertion. The final branding rerun passed on Neutral (8), Sundsvall (8 with one previously documented contrast skip) and custom (9). Generated references cover 53 decisions and 109 documents. No database/durability rerun, live source, manual screen-reader or hosted-CI result is claimed by this reconciliation. A second host fetch confirmed upstream remained at `0c314c8` before committing.

Reliability roadmap continuation, 2026-11-05 (D47, migrations 0020–0026): calendar create/move/cancel/confirm/undo now prepare immutable decisions through the execution ledger; API bookings require a stable decisionId and return pending cards. Calendar uses stable UIDs, conditional revisions and per-item read-back, and preserves original evidence during uncertain compensation. Missing attendee/room availability cannot authorize a create. Follow-up has an approved policy (two reminders maximum, two working days apart, 30-day expiry), current assignment/authority checks, atomic invitee cards/counters/audit and timer generation fences. Legacy invitations are visibly paused, not silently authorized. Source refresh is persisted/coalesced with separate leases/deadlines, source occurrence key/assignment/period/revision and local disposition; partial/unavailable observations never imply completion. Unresolved executions remain visible beyond both history limits, including old uncertain compensation receipts. Reminder/snooze intent and cancellation commit before Temporal, use absolute due times and generations, and display queued/blocked status. New/queued jobs have Temporal ownership; legacy-running jobs await explicit drain evidence. Attention admission/bundles and cross-tab delivery claims are durable; the observer lives in the shell on every page. The operator endpoint/CLI reports aggregate reliability counts. Added isolated reliability CI and synthetic upgrade/backup/restore rehearsal. Updated current architecture, connector guides/protocol, support matrix, repository guidance and generated documentation; added a vision-to-acceptance matrix and operations runbook.

Validation: full `bun run check` passed with 338 unit/web tests, four Node tests, type/dependency/purity checks and generated docs/signed fixture smoke. After the final adapter/test-fixture correction, typecheck and all 42 PostgreSQL tests passed. Both real PostgreSQL/Temporal/Node durability tests passed, including prior workflow history replay. The isolated rehearsal upgraded a synthetic migration-0019 database through 0026, verified repeated migration, restored pg_dump into a separate database and preserved the evidence digest; both rehearsal databases/artifact and the failed-run follow-up fixtures were cleaned up. No production/source data was restored. The last full Sundsvall browser run was 221 passed, 9 failed, 34 optional screenshot cases skipped. After fixing a profile locator and waiting for finite entrance animations, the 16-case header/branding rerun was 8 passed/8 failed: the remaining failures all expose existing sidebar-label clipping, with assertions retained. New reminder/execution/calendar states passed in the full run; focused final rerun is recorded below. The direct Playwright configuration ran inside the devcontainer; its host wrapper requires a Docker CLI unavailable there. No manual screen-reader pass was performed.

Final affected browser rerun: 28 passed, 11 optional screenshot cases skipped (calendar, durable executions, reminder delivery/queued/blocked states and unavailable-source state). Final generated docs/link/signed-fixture checks passed. The running application's `/api/ready` returned 200 with `ready: true`.
