Session 14, 2026-11-04, second half: the D22 rename carried out before the D21 work is committed. `packages/modules` → `packages/connectors` (`@clew/connectors`, type `Connector`), `modules/hello` → `connectors/hello` (`connector.mjs`, `createConnector`, `CLEW_CONNECTOR_SECRET`, sidebar "Hello-anslutningen"), registry and remote adapter out of `apps/server/src/modules/` into `apps/server/src/connectors/`, `routes/connectors.ts`, callback `POST /api/connectors/call`, `GET /api/connectors`, `CLEW_CONNECTORS` and `CLEW_CONNECTOR_SECRET_<ID>`, `scripts/connector-check.ts`, `docs/architecture/connector-protocol.md`, `docs/protocol/connector-protocol.schema.json` (regenerated), `docs/guides/creating-a-connector.md`, `working-across-connectors.md`, and the connector field on threads and areas (`thread.connector`, migration `0005`, `Thread.connector`, `Area.connector`). `module_state` and `scripts/modules-check.ts` keep their names; D20's `apps/server/src/modules/<name>/` folders keep the word module (bounded context) with `connector.ts` as the descriptor file. Living docs, `CLAUDE.md` and `README.md` follow; decisions D01–D21 and reviews are untouched, so their links point at the old names.

Session 14, 2026-11-04, first half: planning only, no code. Two proposed decisions and one design for the administration of integrations: D22 renames the D21 "module service" to **connector** (Swedish "anslutning", admin page "Anslutna system"; "module" keeps only D20's code meaning; "källa" stays on cards) because Eneo uses both "modules" and "integrations" for other things; D23 makes the database the only registration path (no `CLEW_MODULES`, no restart; secrets sealed with the `auth-crypto` envelope; an audience of roles, units and later SCIM groups per connector enforced in the registry so a person outside it cannot learn the connector exists; `admin.connectors` grant capability with step-up and audit; the checker shared between CLI and the admin page). `docs/architecture/connector-administration.md` holds the table, the admin API, the three screens (list, four-step add dialog, detail with Översikt, Förmågor, Områden, Vem ser det, Historik), the states to design, the a11y tests and the order of work (rename first, before the uncommitted D21 work lands). Open: the name itself (connector vs integration vs source), and a canvas row for the admin screens before building.

Session 13, 2026-11-04: backend structure and modules shipped outside the repository. D20 (a modular monolith with ports and adapters, not tactical DDD): `docs/architecture/backend-structure.md`, the rule block in `CLAUDE.md`, `dependency-cruiser` 17.4.3 as `bun run deps` inside `bun run check`. `apps/server/src` reorganised with history kept: `modules/registry.ts`, `modules/remote.ts`, `modules/{calendar,people,figures,mocks}/`, `threads/usecases/{cards,reminders}.ts`, `system-context.ts` (one builder for the scan, figures and module callbacks); routes no longer import `@clew/db` (`weekOverview`, `attentionFor`, `threadFor`, `dismissAny`, auth re-exports). Capability layer: declarative `ScopeRule` with `inScope`, and a `clew.call` handle passed to every `run`, `scan` and `figure` (reads and proposals only, scope re-checked, execute refused), so modules never import each other. D21 (every integration is a module service): `packages/modules/src/protocol.ts` and the generated `docs/protocol/module-protocol.schema.json` (`bun run protocol:schema`, kept current by a test), `packages/modules/src/remote.ts` (manifest validation, transport), the loader from `CLEW_MODULES` and `CLEW_MODULE_SECRET_<ID>` with retries, HS256 assertions per call (jose, two minutes, `aud` = module id), the callback `POST /api/module/call` that rebuilds identity from the grant, `HOST` for the BFF bind address, `modules/hello` (one dependency-free file, Dockerfile, compose, README), `scripts/module-check.ts` (conformance checker), `docs/architecture/module-protocol.md`, `docs/guides/creating-an-external-module.md`. Verified: `bun run check` green (43 unit tests, 11 new, dependency rules pass), the hello module served over real HTTP inside the container and checked with the checker (only the capability that calls back into Clew warns, since no BFF was running). Not verified this session: a remote module loaded by the running BFF and seen in Chrome. Nothing committed.

Session 12, 2026-11-04 (in parallel with session 11): accessibility review against WCAG 2.2 AA (`docs/reviews/2026-11-04-accessibility-and-motion-review.md`) and its fixes. Findings 1 to 14 are fixed. New: `tests/a11y` (74 tests, `sh scripts/a11y.sh`, `@playwright/test` and `@axe-core/playwright` in the pinned Playwright image as compose profile `a11y`, a production build with `/api` mocked, so no Keycloak or database), `apps/web/src/page-title.ts` (title per view and spoken navigation), `components/panel-labels.ts`, `LayerProvider` in `Providers` (without it toasts mounted outside the Swedish catalog), and the WCAG rule in `CLAUDE.md`. Busy no longer disables card buttons, because that dropped focus. Use `Text` for `ListItem` label and description when the text must not truncate. Open: finding 15 (session warning, needs a decision), skip link, the stream's tab order under the conversation scrim, the 44 px promise, manual NVDA and VoiceOver tests, the accessibility statement and the motion spec. CI: `.github/workflows/a11y.yml` runs the suite in about 3 minutes and keeps the report for 7 days when it fails. `.github/workflows/check.yml` runs `bun run check` in the dev image without the stack, in about 1 minute, so that check must never start to need Postgres or Temporal. Both only report, because branch protection needs GitHub Pro or a public repository. Documentation-only pushes are skipped.

Session 11, 2026-11-04: read-only review of Sundsvall's errand service `api-service-support-management` (`a530b0f`, MIT, Java 25 and dept44 behind WSO2, multi-tenant by `{municipalityId}/{namespace}`) against the vision and D1–D17, with `api-service-case-data` and the Draken UI for comparison. Conclusion in `docs/reviews/2026-11-04-support-management-integration-review.md`: errands are a Clew-owned domain contract and object type but a pass-through module kind, never core; one `ErrandProvider` contract with one adapter per case system, as D11 does for calendars. SupportManagement already has what the scan needs (notifications per AD owner, `assignedUserId`/`assignedGroupId`, suspensions, `dueAt` on investigations, decisions, statements and measures, subscriptions), per-errand access reporting, `If-Match` versions for the re-validate and read-back loop, and a link-out from namespace `baseUrl`. Gaps: no outbound change notification (poll first, then a `WEBHOOK` channel in its subscriber dispatch), no errand-level deadline, identity asserted by `X-Sent-By` header behind a gateway credential rather than a user token. Order of work and open questions in the review and in section 4 item 7. Then D18 (errands as a Clew-owned pass-through domain) and D19 (change notification as a Clew-owned contract: payload-free CloudEvents over HTTP, signed per source, idempotent, scan or workflow signal as the effect, polling as fallback) written as proposed decisions; the Change notification row in `integration-paths.md` moved from open to proposed. No code changed; the landscape table in `integration-paths.md` and the docs index gained a row.

Session 10, 2026-11-04 (in parallel with sessions 8 and 9): Temporal adopted (D17) and the server runtime settled on Node (D16, supersedes D7). `temporal` (auto-setup 1.29.7, databases `temporal` and `temporal_visibility` in the shared Postgres) and `temporal-ui` (2.58.0, port 3023) added to the base compose stack; `TEMPORAL_ADDRESS` on the app container. SDK 1.25.0 in `apps/server`; `src/temporal/` holds the worker (in-process, task queue `clew`, retries every 30 s if Temporal is down), the `reminder` workflow (timer, cancel signal, then the `surfaceReminder` activity) and the client. `pushDraft` extracted from the scan for reuse. A reminder brings the original card back (reopened if snoozed or dismissed, `reminded_at` column from migration `0004`, trigger rewritten to "Du bad 09:44 om en påminnelse · …") with attention level `now` (red badge and counts; the card keeps its place by deadline, no group pushes the stream down) and a persistent toast "Påminnelse: …" with Visa that scrolls to it; while the timer runs the card is parked under Pågår utan dig and the toast's Ångra cancels the workflow (`…/remind/cancel`, signal `cancel`); never a second card; the first design pushed a separate "Påminnelse:" card and was withdrawn the same morning because it duplicated the card and its actions. `POST /api/threads/:id/remind { seconds }` starts the workflow and audits `reminder.set`; the card has a visible standard action `Påminn mig` (Astryx `DropdownMenu`, adaptive presentation) with Om 10 sekunder (demo), 10 minuter, en timme, tre timmar; the page re-reads when the timer should have fired. Verified with `bun run check` (23 tests) and `bun scripts/modules-check.ts` on the host, which now snoozes a card, sets a 3-second reminder and finds the same card back open with the reason, and no second card. Not done: moving the nightly scan and the undo windows from the `job` table to Temporal; a duration picker instead of the fixed ten seconds; "Glöm det" on the toast (the cancel signal exists, no button yet).

Session 9, 2026-11-04: pre-merge review of PR #1 found that Nextcloud preserves `custom_apps` in an existing volume, so changing the image's app archives alone would not apply the pinned Calendar/RoomVox versions. Added the image-owned `before-starting` hook and `refresh-calendar-apps.php`: validate both versions before writes, reject downgrades, checksum-sync only the two pinned apps, migrate before Apache starts, and retain a pending marker on failure so restart repairs files and retries the migration. Four focused PHP/rsync filesystem contracts pass in a network-isolated, read-only cached Nextcloud-derived CLI container as www-data, with a 256 MiB Docker memory limit and the host command under the global supervisor. Other apps and meeting data survive; unchanged versions are idempotent. No full image build or live Nextcloud database migration was run. README and the dependency review's Outcome record the upgrade and recovery behavior.

Session 8, 2026-11-04: reviewed all 30 direct npm dependencies and the pinned development infrastructure. Node 26.13.0 LTS landed on main in `e403480` during the review; the refresh retains it and aligns Bun and dependency pins. Bun is 1.4.4, Astryx CLI/Core/Neutral 0.7.7, fast-xml-parser 5.12.0 and temporal-polyfill 1.0.7; `bun.lock` regenerated. PostgreSQL stays on 17 (17.11), Nextcloud on 33 (33.0.10), Calendar moves to 6.6.4, RoomVox to 1.6.1 and Mailpit to 1.33.0, with verified immutable digests and app hashes. The root Astryx command now runs from its owning UI workspace, so README no longer needs a Bun cache hash. A narrowly scoped esbuild override removes the old Drizzle-loader dependency; SQL export is byte-identical and `bun audit` is clear. Added three CalDAV contract tests and four Node runtime contracts; the latter are in `bun run check` and also run with `bun run test:node`. Host checks used exact temporary Node/Bun versions under the global resource guard: typecheck, eight focused Bun tests, four Node tests, frozen install, Astryx doctor and Compose validation pass. No containers were rebuilt, full suite/build or live database/login checks run; D7's Bun migration and postgres 4 remain separate work. See the dependency review for evidence, licences, rollback and deployment checks.

Session 7, 2026-11-04 (in parallel with session 6): the composer row stays docked at the bottom when the stream is short (`.stream-column` fills the scrollport, the Overlay grows with `.stream-fill`, `styles.css`); design exploration "Clew uppmärksamhet" (Claude Design, https://claude.ai/artifact/1P8XeGdLnBtRv87iHAwJxJ, private until shared): five attention levels drawn only with Astryx Neutral status tokens and existing props (Banner for page state and D8, Badge variant+icon by deadline, StatusDot in section headings and sidebar counts, Card elevation/muted, primary Button only where an action is due, List rows for Till kännedom). Rule: colour comes from state, never from category; always a word or icon beside the colour. The attention budget is the assistant's own cap, never a measure of the person: no progress bar, wording "Jag stör högst 3 gånger i dag". Built the same day: `attentionLevel()` in `packages/contracts` (urgent ≤3 days, soon ≤30, later, notice without deadline), `Area.urgency` from `listAreas`, the stream grouped by level with StatusDot/Icon headings in `pages/start.tsx`, `NoticeList` rows and the shared `useThreadActions` hook in `components/card.tsx`, the D8 Banner from `GET /api/assistant/status`, coloured sidebar counts in `components/areas.tsx`. Verified in Chrome as Hassan; `POST /api/mocks/reset` (with the session's `x-csrf-token`) brings the mock cards back. The conversation follows the canvas row Chips: a chipped card is lifted above the dim with an accent ring (one `:has()` rule in `styles.css`, z-index 1 so it stays under the row), proposals from the conversation sit in a Card with a yellow "Att bekräfta" badge and the only primary button, a card holding a draft shows the same yellow badge, and the done message has a green check. The suggestion list on focus (Fråga om verksamheten · Om det som visas · Gör något · Tidigare samtal) was dropped as decided on the canvas: focusing the row only dims the stream and lifts the chipped cards; the conversation panel opens on send as its own white Card above the row, detached from the composer (not the composer's drawer, which is grey and inside the body), fits its content for short conversations (ScrollableArea only beyond four messages, since it has no max height) and carries a Samtal badge, the title, Nytt samtal and ×. The dock no longer adds a shadow of its own; the composer's and the card's elevation do that. The chips a person attached are drawn inside their bubble above the words, not in the bubble's metadata footer, which has no room for them. Devcontainer image moved to `node:26.13.0-bookworm` (Node 26 became LTS 2026-10-28; Node 24 is in maintenance since 2026-10-20), `.mise.toml` follows; rebuilt with `sh scripts/compose.sh up -d --build --wait app`. `GET /api/completions` is no longer called by the web app; earlier conversations are reached through Redan hanterat › Frågor. A mention lifts whatever it points at: a chipped notice row or item row sets ListItem `isSelected` (aria-current, the selected background), and the one `:has()` rule in `styles.css` lifts the enclosing card with the ring, never the row itself, so the ring is not clipped by the card's rounded edge. The scope line sits in the composer's left header slot (`headerActions`) with ⌘K on the right, as drawn. Chips are inserted one frame after `focus()` with a trailing space (`insertChips` in `composer.tsx`): an insert in the same tick landed before the caret and left the token with nothing after it, so Backspace could not reach it when the row had been blurred first. Erasing a chip takes two Backspaces (space, then token), as in Astryx's own typeahead insert. A capability chip (`capability:<module>.<name>`, not on the page) marks the sidebar area that owns the capability with an "I samtalet" badge beside its title (`chippedArea` in `areas.tsx`); event and proposal chips still have nothing to mark until the panel's day view exists.

Session 6, 2026-11-04: docs restructured so decisions keep accumulating: `docs/decisions.md` split into `docs/decisions/D01–D13` with an index and `TEMPLATE.md`; the session-3 platform review against startsidan recovered from the session transcript and saved as `docs/reviews/2026-11-03-platform-review-against-startsidan.md` with an outcome section; `docs/README.md` added as the map and the conventions for adding decisions and reviews. Then `docs/vision.md` written (mission control for every public-sector employee, tie subsystems together, minimise dual work, own the model where nobody else does, the Clew/Eneo line with a who-does-what table; search named as the place you search from with a fan-out to modules, and orchestration of a person's work named as a core function, and the employee directory kept in Metakatalogen with Clew holding only the SCIM essentials, after review) and D14 recorded; `docs/architecture/integration-paths.md` added as the catalogue of supported integration paths with auth mode and status per path, reduced from startsidan docs 08, 13, 14, 16, 18, 19, 33 and ADR-094, with a list of startsidan concepts that do not carry over. `docs/guides/creating-a-connector.md` (the two D10 paths, the order of work, what a review checks) and `docs/guides/example-custom-directory.md` (made-up Fjällby kommun connects its custom Personalregistret: contract, adapter, module, registration, tests) added; the example's code is typechecked against the current `@clew/modules` types but not added to the repository. Committed as `eb79bfd`. Then `docs/architecture/capability-surfaces.md` added: one descriptor, three surfaces (deterministic REST, model tools, Clew as MCP server) plus MCP as a transport behind an adapter, with what each kind may do on each surface and a Treserva example (commit `c2a340e`). Then `docs/architecture/working-across-modules.md`: Clew joins on identity and refs inside capabilities at request time, no query engine; five mechanisms (actor, refs and chips, composite capabilities, derived modules, enrichment on demand), combinations by module kind, and the identity mapping table as a designed next piece. Noted that the planner should call the calendar through the registry (commit `9fdfd7f`). Then `docs/architecture/scheduled-work.md` and D15: what runs on a timer, how the row-locked `job` table works, its deliberate limits (no retry, timeout, signals or visibility), the four triggers for adopting Temporal, and what stays unchanged when it comes; fit assessed: Temporal is the engine, the runtime moves to Node when it arrives, hosted operation means container count is not a criterion, DBOS and pg-boss considered and not chosen. Vision gained "Hosted by a provider, self-hostable by anyone". Product direction stated: the scheduler is crucial, user-set reminders and watches ("påminn mig om tio minuter om X") are the fifth trigger and expected to be the first, to be built on Temporal from the start. No code changed.

Session 5, 2026-11-03: design review against the canvas; conversation endpoint with chips, verbs, drafts, model path and router fallback; composer drawer overlay, card standard actions with undo, Redan hanterat with filters (commit `6eedc14`); real routes for areas, Redan hanterat, panel views and conversations through react-router and Astryx `LinkProvider` (commit `1dddfc9`). See section 0. Next session starts with the live model: set `AI_*` in `apps/server/.env.local`, run `bun scripts/modules-check.ts`, then ask in Chrome.

Session 4, 2026-11-03: module architecture implemented with four mock integrations, the core people module and the calendar as a module; threads generalised to cards with action slots and receipts; mission-control start page. See section 0.

Session 3, 2026-11-03: platform review against startsidan (no hard blockers; risks are Microsoft 365 calendars needing Graph plus an Entra token path, Heroma having no API, and Astryx still being beta). Decided D10 (integrations fulfil Clew's API first, adapters as fallback) and D11 (one calendar contract, `caldav` and `microsoft365` adapters selected per account); `packages/calendar/src/adapters.ts` added and the synthetic bindings routed through it. Next step 10 added.

Session 2, 2026-11-03: architecture and identity decisions documented (D1–D7); mission-control exploration added to the design canvas (row "Mission control", artboards Mission, Mission-omrade, Mission-mobil, Entiteter) with validation against the startsidan personas in `docs/personas.md`. Nothing built from it yet; the open conflict is the sidebar versus the "no navigation" principle.

Session 1, 2026-11-03: checklist items 1–6 done (devcontainer, Hono decided, Astryx under Vite verified, schema and migration, Keycloak login for real, calendar page, start page with one pushed thread, mobile). See section 0 for the state and the ordered next steps.

Keep this file updated. It is the only context the next session has.


Second opinion, 2026-11-06: assessed the architecture, external connector authoring and proposed shared facts. Saved [the independent review](docs/reviews/2026-11-06-architecture-and-connectors-second-opinion.md). Recommendation: keep the core, narrow the supported journey, improve maintained starters and standalone conformance, prove one shared kind and direct dependency visibility before expanding. No architectural decision accepted or application code changed. Verification in the devcontainer: 43 focused job/mock/protocol tests passed (290 assertions), documentation smoke passed, and an isolated copy of the phone-book connector passed signed HTTP reads and identity/credential checks outside the checkout.


Second-opinion update, 2026-11-06 (fixture implementation checkpoint 838b3da): reassessed the supplied independent-fixtures plan. Added the current assessment to [the architecture review](docs/reviews/2026-11-06-architecture-and-connectors-second-opinion.md). Next: finish remote bootstrap/unbundling and acceptance, then one real-source adapter. Three acceptance corrections: payroll unit scoping (reproduced synthetic cross-unit read), named-volume reset, and old execution lookup/state cutover. Six fixture test files pass: 27 tests, 148 assertions. Implementation and generated documentation were being edited concurrently; no application code or generated index was changed by this review.

Session follow-through, 2026-11-06 (current shared checkout, no worktree): implemented the architecture review's
fixture corrections. Payroll enforces source unit/person scope with its own synthetic directory; bootstrap requires
nonproduction CLEW_POC and preserves existing connector and binding settings. Explicit fixture reset fences old
executions with source-reset (unknown remains unknown, receipts remain), blocks watches, cancels timers and archives
card grouping keys before removing only five named fixture volumes. New PostgreSQL and isolated shell tests cover
retirement, audit, repeatability, preservation, ordering and failure. Node tests now exercise a genuinely dropped HTTP
reply after commit, killed/restarted HR process, read-back/retry/undo and the actual HR/payroll HTTP feed.
The Eneo adapter's cross-user document cache was removed; every read rechecks source access, and source outages/denials
are no longer hidden as empty searches. Seven Eneo regression tests include two users and revoked access.
No dependencies added; the running container's Bun was aligned from 1.4.2 to the already pinned 1.4.4.
Verification: 439 unit/web tests, 6 Node tests and 49 PostgreSQL tests passed, with format/types/dependency/purity
checks green. Regenerated the context index after the final review update and reran the documentation check/smoke.
Live limits: dev:connectors found six legacy fixture executions and refused cutover; reset --check refused the running
BFF. Live data was preserved. Eneo container inspected read-only: API stopped, OIDC resource-server disabled, accepted
issuer/audience unset. Await the intended test tenant/collection/users before the live source pilot. No files in the Eneo repository changed.

Cutover done, 2026-11-06 12:25 UTC, main checkout: the follow-through landed as `8ba4562`, the dev server was stopped, `bun run fixtures:reset` ran from the host (audit `fixtures.reset`; the eleven legacy fixture executions, all succeeded, are marked `source-reset`; the five named volumes were recreated), the server restarted and `dev:connectors` kept all six registrations and the three `hr:absence` bindings. The stored `hello` connector still declares protocol 1 and logs a rejection on every registry load; it is noise, not a fault.

Users boundary, 2026-11-06: the product owner identified the built-in “Startsidan” entry as misleading and approved treating users as core entities. D62 separates the internal `people.*` read provider from managed connectors; labels use Användare, new boots do not create its connector row, the admin list omits it and direct connector administration refuses it. Reconciliation keeps its lifecycle enabled while preserving legacy audience/capability/model restrictions; an old disabled row does not re-enable assistant tools. Core identity/grants were already independent and are unchanged. Remote registration cannot replace core capabilities. No user-administration screen, migration, dependency or manifest field added. The read adapter now sits behind UserReads, with one contract against memory and PostgreSQL. Connector CLI listing/lifecycle operations also exclude the internal provider. Verification: full check passed (444 unit/web tests, 6 Node runtime tests, formatting, types, dependency/purity rules and docs); 3 targeted PostgreSQL tests and 23 administration accessibility tests passed. CLI register/disable/remove reject users before database or network access. The signed-in browser confirmed the users row is absent and its old administration address offers no controls. The remaining Startsidan/clew row is the separate composite-search feature. No live user or grant data changed.

Hem pixel pass, 2026-11-06 (main checkout, at the product owner's wish to follow the work there; another session had uncommitted server and documentation edits in the same tree, left alone, so staging was by explicit paths and HANDOVER by patch): the product owner asked for Hem to match the artboards of "Clew startsida · dagen i ett svep" pixel for pixel and for every drawn element without backing to be asked about. D63 and `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-hem-against-the-canvas.md`. Built: the type scale at Astryx's base 14 (D48 item 1); the lead drawn small with the day, the week and the unit at its line's end; the box one line and full width; a three-track `Grid` with the day's work in two and I dag and Sedan i går as cards in the third (the resting `LayoutPanel` of D53 item 6 is gone; the side column is not drawn while the panel holds something); tabs with `Badge` counts and the filter as a ghost `DropdownMenu`; the groups in the artboard's order with their captions; Nu in a red-tinted `Card` and the tint tokens set in `theme.ts`; rows with a day cell, a `Link` title, the line, "Lägg till i samtalet" and the next step, and no ⋯ (the card keeps Inte nu, Avfärda, "Varför nu?"); the open card without a `Card` around it, its time action in the panel's header (`CardTimeAction`), "Varför nu?" and "Historik" as `Collapsible`s with `useAnimatedLayout`; Läget's tiles with the dot on the label line; Sedan i går as the artboard's three sentences; the quiet page as a green card with Det du kan göra under it (first area chosen) and the areas gone from Hem with work on it (the product owner: "the design doesnt have these"); the rail's "Ny fråga", "N i dag"/"lugnt" badge and the conversations under "I dag"/"I går"; the assistant's turn named with what it read. The artboards were rendered to PNG at their own size in the Playwright image and read beside `CLEW_SCREENS=1 … screens.hem` captures (new spec, A, B, E, G, I, D, K); the Astryx gaps (Inter, the 272 px rail, sizes off the 4 px scale, small capitals, the dashed divider, the phone's top bar) are in D63. Twelve questions to the product owner are in D63 under "Asked, not built" and in the side-session notes; one is answered. Specs updated: `structure`, `todo`, `answer`, `announcements`, `keyboard`, `card-actions`, `reflow`, `touch`, `admin`, `screens.landing`, and `support.ts` (`quietDay`, `snoozeFirst` through the card). Also on the product owner's word, with the two pages side by side: the conversation takes Hem's width, 40 px in from the rail, not a centred 800 px column; and the tool page (artboard I) has its heading and the view's controls on one line, the table in a card and the choice as a `Selector`. Verified: `bun run check` green but for the docs index, regenerated at the commit; the touched WCAG specs green (`structure`, `todo`, `answer`, `announcements`, `keyboard`, `card-actions`, `reflow`, `touch`, `admin`, `shell`, `reliability`, `tools`, `views`, 160 and 44 tests over two runs). Not done: a manual screen-reader pass, a dark-appearance comparison, the full suite, and the elements that wait for the product owner's answers.

Answers, 2026-11-06 (main checkout, second commit of the pixel pass): the product owner answered the twelve questions. Changed from the defaults: Läget is off Hem until D54 is decided (`lage.tsx` removed, the figures query no longer read on Hem); a meeting on I dag links to the conversation that pointed at it ("underlaget ligger i samtalet", an `event:` chip in a question thread's messages) and to the card whose confirmed proposal made it ("från kortet …"); and, on a later word with a picture of the toggles, "Det du kan göra" is gone entirely, the quiet page included (D41 items 3 to 5). The rest stands as built. D63 records the answers under "Asked and answered". Specs: the three area tests in `structure.spec.ts`, the areas reflow test and the `03-landing-area` capture are gone; the panel-close focus test in `keyboard.spec.ts` runs on the tool page; the `fraga` fixture carries an `event:e1` chip. Verified: format, types, dependency rules, purity and the unit suite green; `structure`, `todo`, `answer`, `keyboard`, `reflow`, `card-actions` and `announcements` green.

Optimistic cards, 2026-11-06, worktree `optimistic-cards` (branch of the same name, cut from local `main` at `d01fc1c` while the Hem session was editing `card.tsx`, `thread.tsx` and `start.tsx` in the main checkout): the product owner saw a card flip through states after one click. Cause: the ledger answers with committed intent (D45/D47) and every later commit's change notice refetched every read, so Attestera drew the spinner, then "Ditt beslut är sparat och väntar på källan" with Kontrollera status, then the receipt, each announced; Avfärda waited a round trip. D64 written and built: `apps/web/src/optimistic.ts` (predictions and `atRest`), `previewThread` and `changeNotices` in `thread-cache.ts`, `run(fn, { optimistic, settle })`, `holdSettling` and `useSettling` in `mutations.ts` (`SETTLE_MS` 4 s), `main.tsx` routes the stream's notices through `changeNotices`, and `card.tsx` passes the predictions for Avfärda, Inte nu and Ta upp nu, `settle: true` for execute actions, keeps the pressed button `isLoading` and skips the pending row, the narrow-row rule and the announcement while settling. A refused optimistic request restores the card and shows the reason as an error toast (the row's own alert would unmount with the card). `tests/a11y/support.ts` gained `mutationDelay` and `mutationFails`; new `tests/a11y/optimistic.spec.ts` and `tests/unit/web-optimistic.test.ts`, three cases added to `web-thread-cache.test.ts`. Not done: `thread.tsx` (proposal confirm/undo) and Påminn mig keep the earlier presentation; no screen-reader pass. Merging into `main` touches `card.tsx` and `tests/a11y/support.ts`, which the Hem session has uncommitted; its `card.tsx` has moved the action-row code (button sizes, `CardTimeAction`, the quiet row), so expect to re-apply the eleven small hunks by hand rather than a clean fast-forward.

Collapsible panel, 2026-11-06 (main checkout): the product owner asked whether the right side could collapse and chose the open panel alone, not the resting day column. `usePanel` now draws the inline panel as Astryx's resizable region (`ResizeHandle` + `useResizable`, collapsible, width saved per browser as `clew-panel-width`, the fold reset when the panel's content changes), with "Fäll ihop panelen" in the header and `slot.collapsed`/`slot.expand` for the pages, which draw "Visa panelen" beside Hem's tabs, in the conversation's toolbar and on the tool page's heading line; the fold and the return are spoken and place the focus on the handle and the heading. D63 item 14. Hem's day's-work markup became a function so it can read the slot. Test: `todo.spec.ts`, "beside the page the panel folds away …"; capture `E · the panel folded away` in `screens.hem`. Verified: format, dependency rules and the unit suite; `todo`, `keyboard`, `structure`, `tools` and `axe` green.

Resting panel folds too, 2026-11-06 (main checkout): the product owner sent Hem with nothing open and said there was no way of opening the sidebar, so the resting panel (I dag and Sedan i går, `rest` in `usePanel`) is now the same foldable `LayoutPanel` as an open card: handle, "Fäll ihop panelen" over the cards, "Visa panelen" beside the tabs; `slot.collapsed` counts the rest too. At 1440 px the box therefore ends at the panel's edge (D63 items 3 and 14; the grid of three stays for 1024 to 1336 px). Also: a row's day cell never wraps, tomorrow is its weekday ("lör") instead of "i morgon". The fold test in `todo.spec.ts` now folds and expands the resting panel as well. Another session's uncommitted D65 (`since.ts`, `since.tsx`: a sentence names a few cards and sums the rest) was left alone. Verified: `todo` and `structure` green; typecheck green but for that session's `tests/db/fixture-maintenance.test.ts`.

Sedan i går at any count, and the panel's fold button on its edge, 2026-11-06, main checkout (side session of the optimistic-cards session; nothing committed): the product owner saw Hillevi's "Sedan i går" card read "34 klara: lönesystemet har inte visat det som bevakades 13:25, …" as one sentence the whole column high. Two causes. **Presentation:** `since.tsx` joined every closed card into one sentence. D65: `apps/web/src/since.ts` builds the three sentences, at most three cards named newest first and the rest "och N till", a card closed more than once named once, the waiting sentence the same; `components/since.tsx` draws them. Tests: `tests/unit/web-since.test.ts`, `structure.spec.ts` ("when many cards closed since the day began …"). **Data:** none of the 34 was a card Hillevi had closed that day. `retireFixtureWork` in `scripts/fixture-maintenance.ts` set `handled` and `handledAt = at` on every fixture thread, open or not, so the cutover at 13:25 restamped 218 threads (134 Hillevi, 82 Hassan, 2 Mikael) that had been closed on 3 to 5 November, 169 of them dismissed by the old `mocks.reset` (26 runs; each let the next scan push the same topic again, hence one title twenty times) and 31 by the person or the system; the page showed the 50 newest of them as closed today (`listThreads` keeps 50 history rows). The five "Lönesystemet har inte visat det som bevakades" are five expired payroll watches from the `jobs-check` runs, distinct cards, open until the cutover closed them. Fixed: the reset archives every fixture card's topic and clears its actions but closes only open cards (`closed` in the result and the audit row); `tests/db/fixture-maintenance.test.ts` covers a closed card keeping its state and time and an open one being closed. The dev database was repaired from the audit table (SQL and findings in the side-session notes folder, `2026-11-06-sedan-i-gar-*`): 200 threads got their state and closing time back, each with a `thread.closing_restored` audit row and one `fixtures.closing_restored` summary; the 18 open at the cutover keep it. Hillevi now has 11 klara today, all closed by the cutover. Then the product owner, looking at the fold of D63 item 14, asked for the open control as "an icon at top right corner … that animates in and out with the sidebar panel as a handle" instead of "Visa panelen" among the page's buttons: `usePanel` now puts one `IconButton` in the `end` slot on the handle's content side, at the top, chevrons right to fold and chevrons left to show, and returns `{ end, overlay }` alone; the header's "Fäll ihop panelen", the rest panel's header and the page-side buttons in `start.tsx` and `tool.tsx` are gone; folding keeps the focus on the button, expanding moves it to the panel's heading. Then, with the button live, the product owner asked for the fold to clear the selection and for the card's token in the box to be removable: `collapse` now calls the panel's `onClose` (a card or view closes as with ✕, the focus goes back to what opened it; the resting panel keeps the focus on the button), and the `about` token in `composer.tsx` carries `Token`'s `onRemove`, which navigates to `clearHref` like "Ny fråga i stället". `todo.spec.ts` ("beside the page the panel folds away and comes back with the one button on its edge …" and "the card's token in the box has an X …") checks both; `screens.hem` waits for the button. `tools.spec.ts` line 119 asserted a level-1 heading by role, which Playwright derives from the tag (D63's `Heading level={2} accessibilityLevel={1}` is an h2 with aria-level 1), so it has failed since b2cba1c; it now uses the same CSS locator as the spec's first test. Verified: `bun run check` green twice (after since.ts and after the panel), `structure` green (28 passed); `todo`, `tools` and `keyboard` run in this session, see the next note or the session's report. Not done: a live comparison with the artboard signed in as Hillevi (the built-in browser has no Keycloak session); a screen-reader pass; a reset-closed card still counts as "klar" (D61's amendment chose `handled`).

Startsidan proof of concept, 2026-11-06 (main checkout; another session's uncommitted D62 edits in the same tree, left alone): the product owner sent https://start.sundsvall.dev/portal/start and asked whether Clew's core still holds under that design, and whether it could be built as a separate web app on Clew's infrastructure. [The review](docs/reviews/2026-11-06-startsidan-poc-against-clews-core.md) maps the demo element by element to the thread, area, figure, day and view contracts: every work element is a view over what the server already holds; the intranet chrome (sections, news, "Anpassa startsidan"), Min AI as a general assistant and a chip that executes are outside the vision and stay outside. Built `apps/portal` (D67, proposed): a second Vite app on port 5174 over the same BFF, session and `@clew/ui`, importing the composer, the conversation, the rows, the cards, I dag, Sedan i går, Redan hanterat, the declared view, the bell and the login from `apps/web/src` by relative path; the start page, `/activities`, `/cards/:id` and `/tools/:capability`; Sundsvall as its default profile; `bun run dev:portal`, `bun run build:portal`, and `127.0.0.1:3024:5174` added to `compose.yaml` (the app container must be recreated once to publish it; this session reached it through a throwaway TCP proxy container instead). The first sign-in on 3024 was ended on the first click: the CSRF check compared `Origin` with `PUBLIC_URL` alone, and the session boundary's activity call is a POST; `verifyMutation` now accepts the request's own origin too, so a second front end on another origin keeps its session. Verified: typecheck, format check, a production build, the dev server answering in the container, and the page in the browser against the dev stack. Not verified: the WCAG suite (no portal specs yet), a screen reader, the phone. A mistaken `prettier --write` over `docs/**/*.md` was reverted: clean files from git, the six files the other session had edited by a three-way merge that kept only its edits (status.md rebuilt from HEAD with its two rows), the generated indexes by `bun run docs:generate`. Next: the product owner compares the portal with Hem side by side on the same data; if it stays, move what both apps share into a package, add `tests/a11y` specs for it and decide D54 (the figures' owner), which the demo's tiles make urgent.

The panel's tabs, 2026-11-06, main checkout (same side session, nothing committed): with the fold button live the product owner opened two cards after each other and saw the second overwrite the first ("maybe we need some kind of tab view or dropdown showing currently selected"). The canvas's artboards F, H and J draw the panel's header as a row of tabs, "Det samtalet rör", which D53 and D63 had left for later. D66 written and built in `usePanel`: everything opened since the panel was last empty is kept as `OpenTab`s (id, name, search) in the hook's state, the address names the current one as before (D50 item 4), from two a `TabList role="tablist"` of `Tab`s named by the thing itself (`PanelFrame.tab`, the card's title from `start.tsx`; views keep their title) takes the heading's place with every tab controlling the one body region `panel-body`, in the drawer the row stands above the body; closing (✕, Esc, Stäng kortet, Ny fråga i stället, the token's X) takes the current thing out of the address and the hook navigates to the tab before it (else the first) with `replace`, so the panel empties with its last tab; the fold drops every tab first; the page says which tabs may stay (`isOpen`: on Hem a card that is snoozed, dismissed or done leaves the row); focus lands on the current tab when there are several (waiting one frame for the row to replace the heading) and the tab's name is spoken. Weighed and left in D66: a dropdown, every open thing in the address, ✕ closing all, an X per tab (Astryx's `Tab` has none). Test: `todo.spec.ts` ("a row opens its card beside the list …" now covers two tabs, aria-selected and aria-controls, a tab leading to its address, Esc moving to the neighbour and the last Esc emptying the panel). Status row "The panel beside a page". The adapter stands as built. Reliability lane red since the D61 migrations reached `origin/main`, 2026-11-06, main checkout: `.github/workflows/reliability.yml` failed on 60f6d60, 49e2711 and b939b54 in `bun scripts/recovery-rehearsal.ts prepare` with `cannot ALTER TABLE "thread" because it has pending trigger events` on the first statement of `0030_english_ids`. Cause: Drizzle's migrator runs every pending migration in one transaction; the rehearsal migrates a database to 0019, inserts its synthetic rows, then runs 0020 to 0031 in one go, and `0026_conservative_reliability_backfill` updates `thread` rows, which queues the deferred change-signal trigger of 0019 (`thread_content_changed`, `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`); PostgreSQL then refuses to alter a table with queued trigger events. `db:init` on an empty database never hit it (no rows), the lane had last run at f59457c, before 0029 to 0031 existed, and the dev stacks had applied 0030 one migration at a time. An installation behind 0020 with data would fail the same way on upgrade. Fix in `0030_english_ids.sql`: `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` before the `ALTER`, `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL DEFERRED` after it, with the reason in the file; the signals are notifications and lose nothing by firing earlier. An applied migration was edited: Drizzle does not re-run it, and 0030 shipped the same day on development stacks only. Verified on the dev stack as CI runs it: `prepare` (upgraded from 0019, duplicate migration safe), `pg_dump` and `pg_restore` into the second database, `verify` (restore equal, evidence preserved, migrations idempotent), `cleanup`; a failed `prepare` leaves its two databases behind, and `cleanup` refuses them until the marker row exists, so after a failure drop `clew_reliability_rehearsal` and `clew_reliability_restored` by hand. `bun run test:db` green.

The panel's menu of open things, 2026-11-06, worktree `panel-tab-menu` from `main` at `49e2711`: the product owner compared the panel's header with the Claude desktop app's pane and named three faults (the Inte nu picker in the header, ✕ at the far right reading as "close the pane" while it closed one tab and was labelled "Stäng panelen", and the tab strip scrolling so that neither the current title nor the rest could be read). With Inte nu inside the open card, Hem's `replace` saw the change come from the panel card and kept the focus there, which the snoozed card took away with it: it now sends the focus on to the next row (else the first heading) when the card leaves, keeping it on the card only while the card is still there (`keyboard.spec.ts` 2.4.3 and 2.4.7).
A side question beside the conversation, 2026-11-06 (worktree `keen-bhabha-606f2b`, exploration only, side session of the panel work): the product owner compared Clew's panel with the Claude desktop app, where a "btw" side chat opens on the right beside the running session, and asked whether Clew should explore the same. No decision, no product code. `bun run docs:generate` adds the review to the index; `bun run check` run in the worktree, see the session's report.

Microsoft 365 seeded in dev and on par with the fixtures, 2026-11-06 (main checkout, side session of the D70 session; D69 amended a second time): the product owner asked to seed the Microsoft 365 connector in the development stack and bring it to the level the six fixture connectors have. Microsoft 365 is an adapter of the core `calendar` connector (D11, D61), so it is not a row `dev:connectors` can register and `connector-check.ts` cannot check it; the amendment says what parity means for an adapter instead. Built: the `graph` service (the local fake of Microsoft Graph) left its compose profile and runs in the base stack beside the fixture connectors, published on `127.0.0.1:4047`, and seeds itself as it starts (`scripts/fake-graph.ts` books `scripts/calendar-fixtures.ts`, the one list now shared with `calendar-seed.ts`, through the adapter over its own loopback; `/health` is 503 until seeded, so `--wait` means seeded; `sh scripts/graph.sh seed` is a restart). `accounts.ts` is a mixed estate: Mikael on Microsoft 365 over the fake, Hillevi and Hassan on CalDAV, `CALENDAR_ADAPTER=microsoft365|caldav` forcing one adapter for all. The calendar connector's reads declare `TodayViewSchema`, `WeekEventsSchema` and `DayViewSchema` (new TypeBox schemas in `@clew/contracts`, replacing the interfaces) instead of `Type.Any()`, `panel-labels.ts` has Swedish names for their fields (`id`, `revision`, `eventRef`, `threadId`, `canCancel` hidden), and `calendar.rooms` is a new read with a `table` view (Rum, Platser, Utrustning) and the menu hint Mötesrum, declared in the leaf `modules/calendar/views.ts` so the WCAG harness draws the real declaration. Today, the week and a day declare no view on purpose (D32). Tests: `tests/unit/calendar-connector.test.ts` (view rules, menu eligibility, `viewFor` and `menu` through the registry), the panel-labels test now holds the calendar's outputs too, `tests/calendar/graph-provider.test.ts` runs the contract against the stack's fake over the network in `bun run test:calendar` (8 pass), and `views.spec.ts` and `tools.spec.ts` each got a rooms case (25 passed, 11 skipped). Verified: `bun run check` green (503 unit tests), the fake seeded 39 meetings and 7 rooms at start, and a container script bound each persona through the directory: Mikael `microsoft365:account:mikael@clew.test` with 8 meetings and 7 rooms, Verksamhetsdialog without a room because Eken declined it; Hillevi and Hassan on `nextcloud:account:…`. Not done: a signed-in browser pass as Mikael (the Keycloak sign-in is the product owner's), and switching Mötesrum on under Administration › Anslutna system › Kalendern. Noticed in passing: this stack's Nextcloud answers 2 rooms, so `sh scripts/calendar.sh seed` has not been run since the seven rooms landed. The app container's compose definition is unchanged, so nothing needed recreating.

Visual hierarchy, area marks and figures, 2026-11-06 (same day, evening; the product owner sent seven pictures of the product: "the titles are all the same size and weight everywhere", cards should tell their connector apart, icons, and what the figures should look like): drawn as a pattern on the canvas **Clew · hierarki, områden och figurer** (https://claude.ai/artifact/YVw6FJL6YjKZprBAiAEA2f) and written up in `docs/reviews/2026-11-06-visual-hierarchy-area-marks-and-figures.md`. Read from the code: nearly every heading is `Heading level={3}` with only `accessibilityLevel` varying, the row's meta line is body size beside a body-size title, the connector's area icon is drawn only in the menu, and two bare status fields in a view read "Ja · Ja". Proposed: seven Astryx type roles with a place each (level 1 on the page title, 2 on the open card, 3 on sections, 14 semibold rows over a 12 px meta line, display-3 only for a figure's value); an area mark where the hue is the system's (six Astryx category hues in registration order, Clew's own areas outlined without hue) and the icon the area's, as a `Badge` chip above titles and a secondary `Icon` glyph in rows; one `figureItems()` mapping to the tile, the line and the rail's word, with the tone words Lugnt, Bevaka and Behöver dig; the view's header under an answer; status fields as labelled badges. Nothing built, nothing committed; the next step is the product owner's decision and a numbered decision file.

The fold keeps what is open, Stäng alla closes all, 2026-11-06 evening (worktree `keen-bhabha-606f2b`, same side session, after the merge of the side-question review): with the row fix live the product owner saw that ✕ closed one thing, the fold button was the only way to close all, and folding threw the open cards away ("if I click the arrow the sidebar resets, is that what you'd expect?"). D63 item 14 had made the fold clear the selection on an afternoon remark, and D66 item 3 had leaned on it to leave "✕ closes all" out, so a layout act carried a state act. Now `collapse` in `apps/web/src/components/panel.tsx` only collapses: the tabs, the current thing and the address survive, the row keeps "öppet bredvid", the box its token, the focus stays on the button, and a `revealing` ref keeps a closed tab's neighbour from unfolding the panel (a thing opened while folded still does). From two open an Astryx `DropdownMenu` on a ⋯ "Fler val för panelen" beside ✕ holds "Stäng alla (N)", which drops the tabs and closes the current thing; the drawer shows it beside "Öppna som sida". D63 item 14 and D66 item 3 carry the evening revision. `tests/a11y/todo.spec.ts`: the fold test now asserts the card stays, and the tabs test ends with Stäng alla; axe on the open menu waits for `document.getAnimations()` to finish, since the fade gave a false colour-contrast failure on one run in three.

The canvas grew to twelve artboards the same evening (the product owner asked whether the calendar and everything a connector can expose were covered): 6 the calendar family as the house (level 1 week title, 12 px grid titles, ✦ proposals as the only blue), 7 the four view kinds and search hits under one header, 8 the mark on small places (tokens, the `@` menu, the review dialog, the D8 banner, toasts, the rail), 9 Administration › Anslutna system with the colour swatch per system and the area chip on the Områden tab, 10 a D55 job card with one chip and a glyph per step, 11 and 12 Hem and the card in the sheet on the phone. The review's section "The remaining surfaces" records them. The product owner then asked for the whole pattern to be built truthfully from the canvas by child sessions on Opus 5.5; see the next entry for the sessions and their branches.

The foundation of the pattern, 2026-11-06 late evening (main checkout, child session "ui-foundation" of the pattern session; D71): artboards 1, 2, 5, 9, 10, 11 and 12 of **Clew · hierarki, områden och figurer** built on `main`. The mark: a `connector` row carries `ordinal` (migration `0032_connector_ordinal`, the lowest free when the row is added, so a removed system frees its colour; existing rows numbered by `created_at`, applied on the dev database with `bun run db:init`), the registry carries it in the governance, `GET /api/areas` adds `systems` (connector, source, ordinal), the admin summary adds `ordinal` and the admin area view the area's `icon`. `packages/ui/src/system-hues.ts` holds the six hues (teal, purple, orange, cyan, pink, gray; Clew's own the house) and `mark.tsx` the components: `AreaChip` (Astryx `Badge`, gray and the outlined house added as theme variants in `theme.ts`), `AreaGlyph`, `AreaLine` (glyph and meta words that never part), `SystemSwatch` (Astryx's colour-only Badge with `role="img"` and a name such as "Färg lila, 2:a anslutna systemet") and `clewIcon`, moved from `apps/web/src/icons.ts` (which re-exports it). The web asks `useMarks()` in `apps/web/src/marks.ts`. Type roles: page titles level 1 (Hem's greeting, the conversation, the tool page), the open card level 2 everywhere, sections level 3; the row's link `weight="semibold"` (Astryx's `Link` resets the weight of the `Text` around it), its meta line `AreaLine` in supporting with a status badge after it only when needed. The card's mark row replaces "Medarbetare · hr"; the receipt keeps its check and gets semibold rows; a card whose rows name other systems (D55 refs) gets each row's glyph, system and area words and "N steg i M system" in its list row. Anslutna system sorts by ordinal with the swatch; the connector page's title has the swatch and "2:a anslutna systemet · färg lila · direkt från källan · protokoll 1"; the Områden tab draws each area's chip. On a phone Hem draws rows (next step under the line, the `@` beside it) and the card in a `BottomSheet`; the old inline cards are gone (D71 item 8 replaces D53 item 2's phone rule). The portal (D67) lost its `areaTitle` props with them. Colours are named in Swedish (blågrön, lila, orange, cyan, rosa, grå) where the canvas wrote English. On the development stack the order is hello 1 (teal), phonebook 2 (purple), hr 3 (orange), finance 4, incidents 5, communication 6, timesheet 7 (teal again), payroll 8: the demo connectors were registered first, so Personalsystemet is orange there, not teal as drawn; the canvas drew the phone book as Clew's own, but it is a connector row and gets a hue. Tests: `tests/unit/system-marks.test.ts` (ordinals, the registry's `systems`, governance, hues), a store contract case on both adapters (`bun run test:db` green on Postgres), `tests/a11y/admin.spec.ts` (registration order, swatch names, the ordinal line, the area chip), `todo.spec.ts` (phone rows and the sheet), `card-actions.spec.ts` (Inte nu from the sheet), `structure.spec.ts`. The a11y mock's areas now name real connector ids (hr, finance, incidents, communication) and the admin mock has the calendar as the one bundled row. Not done: a signed-in pass on localhost:3020 (the Keycloak sign-in is the product owner's), a screen-reader pass. For the other sessions (their files were not touched): the figures session draws the tiles and the phone's two-across grid in `start.tsx`'s landing after the rows, "Visa nyckeltalet" and "Skickar kort" per area on the Områden tab, and display-3 only there; the views session puts the mark row (`AreaChip` + source + count) on `DeclaredView`'s header, including the tool page's header in `tool.tsx` (its title is level 1 now), and the panel's view title at level 2 in `panel.tsx`; the calendar session gives the calendar page's week title level 1 and uses `AreaChip` with `ordinal={null}` for the house. `useMarks().ofArea/ofThread/ofRef` and `AreaLine` are the shared entry points.

An earlier, archived attempt at the same foundation left a patch (a stored `hue` slot 0–5 with `nextHue`, a `connector_hue` migration and a separate marks map in the registry fed by `reconcile.ts`). It was weighed after D71's foundation had landed and not applied: D71 stores the ordinal itself (lowest free positive integer) and derives the hue from it, which gives the same rules (a freed place is reused, the others keep theirs, existing rows numbered by `created_at`) and also the "2:a anslutna systemet" words and a seventh system that starts over at teal, which a six-value slot cannot say; and it rides on the row's governance, which `applyRow` and the watcher already apply, so no second path into the registry was needed.

The calendar is the house, 2026-11-06, on `main` in the main checkout beside the views and figures sessions (D71, amended: artboard 6 of the canvas **Clew · hierarki, områden och figurer**). The page's title is the week, day or month at `Heading level={1}` on every width (the phone's was level 3 drawn as 1). The panel beside the grid is the proposal card: the mark row from `useMarks().ofThread()` (the outlined "Kalender" chip, "Kalendern · vecka 46", the one badge "Förslag att bekräfta" or the dot "Hanterad"), the thread's title at level 2 in place of the assistant's lead sentence, and the proposals as `CheckboxListItem` rows whose label is the day in a 52 px start cell, "✦ name" semibold and the time in supporting (the checkbox's name is "tis 10 Anna Lindqvist 10:00–10:45"; the ✦ is `aria-hidden`). The "N av M valda" line is gone; the button carries the count. The grid's event content (`packages/ui/src/calendar.tsx`): title in supporting semibold, the range and the place (or the person, for a samtal) in supporting under it; in a week's column both lines end in an ellipsis and a short block keeps its title alone with the time in its name, in the day view they wrap (the 320 px reflow check counts an ellipsis as clipped content); the month is one supporting line; a list is the row level with the time in its cell and no dot. `displayEventEnd` is off everywhere; the grid computes its range with `timeRange`. Theme tokens (`calendar-theme.css`): a meeting and a booked samtal are `--color-background-surface` with a `--color-border-emphasized` edge three border widths at the start; a proposal `--color-info-muted`, `--color-on-info-muted` text and a dashed `--color-border-info` edge (Astryx has no `--color-background-info`; `--color-info-muted` is the nearest semantic token, and is a touch more saturated than the artboard's 10 % blue); the past is a `--color-border` edge, no longer 0.7 opacity, which failed contrast once the meta line was there; the purple and green kind colours are gone from the grid. `agendaItems()` gained `marker` (✦ on a drawn proposal), `place`, `meta` ("30 min · Personalrummet", "1 tim · Anna Lindqvist") and an optional third argument, the day's gaps, mapped to `kind: 'gap'` rows ("Ledigt till 17:00", the longest with `LONGEST_GAP`); `EventCalendar` and `WeekAgenda` drop gaps, `Today` now draws its rows from it (`duration` moved there). `WeekAgenda`'s proposal token is blue with ✦ (purple is a system hue), the booked one stays green. `PROPOSAL_MARK` is exported from `@clew/ui`. Not as drawn: Astryx `List` has no dashed divider, so I dag's gap row keeps the ordinary divider; the grid's second line names the person where the artboard wrote "förslag"; the panel keeps its sentence on when invitations go and the reply field. Tests: `tests/unit/agenda-items.test.ts` (meta, marker, gaps), `tests/a11y/calendar.spec.ts` (one h1 per page at both widths, a meeting's name carries title and time, a proposal's name ends in "förslag", the panel's mark row, level-2 title and row names), `structure.spec.ts`'s I dag case ("1 tim · A12 · …", the longest gap's line). Figures from connectors and the figure's three forms, 2026-11-06 late evening (main checkout, child session "ui-figures" of the pattern session beside the views and calendar sessions; D54 accepted and built, D71 amended for artboard 3): the protocol's figure gains `value`, `comparison`, `progress` and `opens`; the registry makes the one warning figure for a connector that throws or answers outside the shape, checks `opens` like a menu row (same connector, a read whose view needs no input; else dropped and reported as `figure:<area>`) and resolves it to the view's title; `GET /api/figures` and `apps/server/src/modules/figures/` are gone and the router reads the areas' figures; `listAreas` stamps `observedAt`. Läget's repeated areas found, 2026-11-07 (same session, on the pattern session's console log as Mikael): the duplicates survived a hard reload, and React warned of duplicate keys in three lists, so they were in `/api/areas` itself, intermittently, and not left over from hot reloads as the note above guessed. `Registry.run`, `call`, `scan` and `validate` first re-apply every connector row (`setRefresh(refreshConnectors)`), and a remote row goes back in with `replace`: unregister, then register, which re-adds it at the end of the `connectors` Map. `areas()` and `scan()` iterated the live Map and awaited inside the loop, so a refresh from a concurrent request (Hem fetches the areas and the threads at once), or from a figure that calls another capability, brought connectors already listed round again. Both loops now iterate a snapshot. Case in `tests/unit/connectors.test.ts` (a figure and a scan that replace a connector mid-loop; two reads, each area once, each connector scanned once); a race of eight concurrent area reads against a refresh loop on the dev stack's real connectors lists ten unique areas each time. Left as it is: a refresh still moves replaced connectors to the end, so the order of the areas can change between reads.

System foundation review, 2026-11-07 (baseline `5c0810d`): new assessment in `docs/reviews/2026-11-07-system-foundation-and-connector-review.md`. Recommendation: keep the modular core and external connector boundary; correct authorization gaps and prove a real source before expanding composition. Isolated reproductions found that scanned cards remain readable after a unit transfer and authorization-version change; a valid remote figure is returned after its connector is disabled during the read; ten unchanged governance refreshes rebuild the six fixture connectors 60 times (505 ms with a synthetic store); figure calls are serial; and manifest preview accepts four documents registration rejects. No application fixes were made. The review also records the current delegated-action `needs-session` limit and targeted simplifications. Verification in the devcontainer: standard check passed (536 unit/web tests, 6 Node tests, formatting/types/dependencies/purity/docs and signed walkthrough); PostgreSQL 52 passed; real PostgreSQL/Temporal/Node durability 3 passed. The standard check preceded unrelated UI edits to the review overlay, which were left untouched. No live-source, browser, screen-reader or production-load verification in this review.

A model's reasoning, 2026-11-07 (D84, branch `model-reasoning-setting`): the product owner got "Modellen svarar inte just nu." on free text with `gpt-6-luna`, because the provider refuses function tools while the model's default reasoning is on. A model now has a `reasoning` value (`provider-default` sends nothing, `none` sends `reasoning_effort: none`) and a `model.reasoning` audit row; migration `0036_model_reasoning`. "Testa" sends a call shaped like a turn, with a tool, and settles the value: the default first, `none` only when the provider refuses the default and takes `none`. Revised 2026-11-09 on the product owner's word: the first version's switch "Utan resonemang" is gone, the page shows the value as a sentence under "Resonemang", and `reasoning` is no longer part of the model patch. Adding a model now runs the same test at once and returns `{ model, report }`; the add dialogs' announcement names a model that did not answer. Migrations 0032 and 0033 carry `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` in the same working tree, needed for a database with connector rows that upgrades through them in one run. Open: Testa on the live `gpt-6-luna` and a real turn after it have not been tried, because the sign-in runs on `keycloak.orb.local` and is left to the product owner. The phonebook connector still logs an unreadable stored manifest (cause not found).

Foundation review fixes, 2026-11-07 (product owner: “start fixing all findings”; local working tree, not committed): implemented all five concrete findings in `docs/reviews/2026-11-07-system-foundation-and-connector-review.md`. D75 records the snapshot policy. New source cards persist immutable `sourceAuthorization` from the authenticated person/grant; the shared reader requires the original version and current connector visibility. A transfer, changed role or renewed grant cannot read old content through history, chips, suggestions or model context. New observations create separate history without retagging old messages; unchanged confirmed/dismissed occurrences and uncertain executions do not manufacture another approval. Watch-expiry cards and calendar execution cards also retain the producing authority. Migration `0034_source_authorization` was generated and applied with `bun run db:init` to the development database. It adds a nullable column with no backfill: legacy external-source cards are hidden and may be replaced by a fresh scan; stored records, receipts and execution evidence are retained. Independent source ACL changes still require explicit revocation propagation into Clew's grant or connector access before enabling persisted scans.

Reconciliation now caches descriptors by canonical manifest/address/sealed secret and refreshes governance without reconstructing unchanged connectors; serialized fresh-read batches coalesce concurrent callers without allowing a final access check to reuse an older in-flight snapshot. Manifest updates without a version bump, secret/address changes and removals remain effective. Figures run at most four calls concurrently within one two-second source budget, abort remote requests at expiry, preserve area order and recheck person authority and current connector/figure/link permissions after the full batch. Replaced descriptors are omitted until the next read. Completions skip figures. Preview, parsing and registration share descriptor validation and schema compilation rules; replacement validates before uninstalling the old connector, and reused schema ids do not retain the old validator. Updated the manifest reference, implementation status, authorization guide, acceptance evidence and generated indexes.

Verification in `clew-app-1`: `bun run check` passed (552 unit/web tests, 6 Node tests, formatting, types, dependency boundaries, purity, generated docs/local links and the signed connector walkthrough); `bun run test:db` 53 passed; `bun run test:durability` 3 passed against PostgreSQL/Temporal/Node; `git diff --check` clean. New regressions cover transferred/renewed/legacy source snapshots, PostgreSQL projections and history, chip/item/object/model-context access, unknown execution and dismissal preservation, unchanged descriptor reuse and concurrent fresh reads, figure revocation/concurrency/deadline/HTTP cancellation, and preview/registration parity. No new dependencies. No live-source, browser, screen-reader or production-load verification in this backend pass. The separate real-source pilot, connector starter/profiles and gradual legacy cleanup remain follow-up recommendations; these fixes do not claim production readiness. Concurrent UI work was left untouched.

Page loading and entrance motion, 2026-11-07 (D76; working tree, not committed): the product owner reported asynchronous blocks popping in on load and hard refresh. Added Astryx-only loading compositions in `apps/web/src/components/loading.tsx`: shared row placeholders, shaped side-card placeholders and a section-level `useEntryAnimation('fadeIn')`. Hem reserves its work/figures and both side cards, assembles each region before revealing it, and covers waiting/snoozed/done without premature empty states or counts. Calendar, tool and administration pages use skeletons and the same entrance; session verification has a named Spinner instead of a blank screen. Background refetches keep content mounted and preserve focus/drafts; reduced motion disables the fade. Hem checks `isFetched` as well as `isPending` to prevent failed first reads retried by nested mark readers from cycling between skeleton and content. Muted cards scope Astryx's skeleton colour to the emphasized border token, because Neutral's light skeleton originally matched the card background. No dependency or custom CSS added. D76, the status guide and generated indexes updated.

Verification: `bun run check` passed (552 unit/web tests, 6 Node tests, formatting/types/dependency boundaries/purity/docs); types, formatting and docs rechecked after the final theme/loading-label adjustments. `tests/a11y/loading.spec.ts`: 16 passed, including cold loads and hard refreshes at 1440/320 px, failed/optional reads, all work tabs, other pages, light/dark placeholders, reduced motion, session isolation and background refetch preserving the composer. Existing answer/structure/calendar/tools/admin/models/cache specs: 127 cases passed across the run and focused rerun; 19 opt-in screenshot cases skipped. The model-label assertion was stale: source is in the assistant article's name and model in its footer, not a single combined line; updated the assertion and reran that case successfully. Reviewed loading and loaded screenshots at desktop/phone sizes. `git diff --check` clean. The devcontainer lacks the Docker CLI for scripts/a11y.sh, so tests ran through docker exec in a temporary container using the repository's pinned Playwright image, removed afterwards. No live authenticated-source or manual screen-reader pass; placeholder heights are representative, not exact predictions of arbitrary payloads.


Documentation review, 2026-11-07: reorganized the repository README, documentation home and agent reading path to explain Clew's purpose and current architecture before connector implementation. Preserved decision/review history and clarified vision/persona scope.

The connector quickstart now includes a complete minimal read/view manifest, source-handler/adapter responsibilities, a copy-and-adapt path, testing/registration, and optional feature progression. Authorization, action and support guides explicitly describe sessionless delegation limits. Existing docs:smoke now validates the marked quickstart manifest and output example against the real validator/schema as well as exercising the signed phonebook handler. Updated llms.txt, CLAUDE.md's entry pointer, generated context entrypoints/categories, and minor current-guide/example corrections. Documentation follow-up, 2026-11-07: the product owner corrected the impression that Clew excludes task orchestration across connectors. README, the product/architecture overviews, the cross-connector guide, connector onboarding and implementation status now make multi-system writes and follow-up central, illustrate HR/time-reporting/payroll under one approval, and restore D55's planned receipt → fact → next proposal → approval chain. Existing API/ledger/watch support is distinguished from planned job/binding screens, entry paths and generic receipt-driven continuation. No architecture policy or application behavior changed.

Also reproduced the GitHub approval diagram's Mermaid parse error: a raw semicolon in its final message ended the statement. Reworded it and validated all four repository diagrams with Mermaid 11.12.0. Temporary validation tools mermaid@11.12.0 and jsdom@31.0.0 are MIT (checked before installation), installed only under /tmp/clew-docs-mermaid-check in the devcontainer; no repository dependency or lockfile changes. The maintainer guide notes that docs:check does not parse Mermaid. The five fixture-job tests passed, proving the documented two writes plus payroll watch and per-step undo. Review follow-up: docs/reviews/2026-11-07-documentation-product-architecture-and-connectors.md.

Documentation generation/checks (including local links and the signed connector smoke), required formatting and git diff --check passed. The browser session was signed out of GitHub and could not access the repository, so verification used Mermaid's parser rather than GitHub's rendered preview.


Multi-connector task exploration, 2026-11-07 (reviewed 8bae24e): the product owner asked how to manage tasks spanning systems. Added docs/reviews/2026-11-07-multi-connector-task-continuation.md: one visible task with independent writes approved together, confirmed results preparing later reviewed proposals, and watches for source-owned propagation. Proposed immutable reviewed proposals, evidence-backed facts, durable deduplicated continuation intents and stage links over the existing ledger/outbox/Temporal. Covered partial proposals, unknown writes, edits, undo, connector replacement/identity, dependent approval policy and other approvers. The three-increment recommendation is exploratory; no new architecture decision, protocol, runtime behavior or dependency was introduced.

Ran 27 job/fixture tests (171 assertions), all passing. Three additional isolated experiments using the existing signed fixture setup and in-memory stores reproduced open gaps: replaying the same approval after advancing time one second recalculates its watch times and fails the ledger digest check; disabling a follower binding removes it from fresh assembly but an old proposal still approves it; readJob returns the confirmed HR receipt to the same person with a different unit and authorization version. Recorded exact triggers, source paths and recommended fixes in the review, implementation status and cross-connector guide. These are unresolved findings, not fixes or claims of source-permission bypass. Temporary experiment: /tmp/clew-job-exploration.ts in the devcontainer. No live source or database changed.

Exploration verification: docs:generate and docs:check passed (15 generated files, 82 decisions, 168 documents, local links and signed connector smoke); all five Mermaid diagrams parsed with the existing temporary validator; required formatting and git diff --check passed. No permanent test or runtime files changed.


Cross-connector approval fixes, 2026-11-07 (D77): implemented the three reproduced gaps from the task-continuation exploration. Job approvals now digest relative watch offsets/duration but retain the first committed absolute schedule and timer intent; later and concurrent replays create no duplicate audit, execution or watch rows. Assembled steps carry binding identity, sources carry configuration revision fingerprints, and approval refreshes/rechecks the binding set, revisions, eligibility and own-connector association before and after step authorization. Step ids are binding-specific and watch caps are keyed by binding, fixing collisions and cap interference with several followers in one connector.

Job/execution snapshots require the original person, unit and authorization version. Reads check current capability access and recheck person authority before returning; jobs fail closed as a whole if a participating source is unavailable. Repeat approvals, reconciliation, compensation and calendar approval lookup enforce the same producing-authority boundary. Evidence is retained. Approval responses now use the shared receipt reader after commit. No dependency or database migration; the external connector StepDraft/protocol version stays unchanged. Job API clients must echo steps[].binding and sources[].bindingRevision and treat ids as opaque. Older unapproved proposals require reassembly; previously committed jobs retain their identifiers, schedules and digests and must be recovered through those identifiers, never a fresh decision.

Added regression coverage for elapsed-time and concurrent approval replay, stale/removed/changed/audience-narrowed bindings, revocation during approval and reads, per-binding watch caps, and job/individual execution receipt, replay, undo and reconciliation access. PostgreSQL regression recreates adapters/use cases and proves durable replay and authority checks. Updated current guides/status and appended the exploration's outcome. Full job/binding UI, immutable reviewed proposal storage, creator-schema validation/fact provenance and receipt-driven continuation remain planned.

Verification through docker exec: bun run check passed (567 unit/web tests, 6 Node runtime tests, formatting, types, dependency boundaries, purity, generated docs/local links and signed connector smoke); bun run test:db passed (54 tests); bun run test:durability passed (3 real PostgreSQL/Temporal/Node scenarios); git diff --check clean. No UI changes, live vendor writes or deployment verification.

Product vocabulary, 2026-11-08 (D78): adopted work item for the work Clew tracks, card/row for its presentation, work item detail for the opened content, and side panel for its container. Added docs/reference/vocabulary.md with product, interface and integration terms, two relationship diagrams, the development-meetings example, naming rules and mappings to current code. Distinguished proposals, decisions, jobs, steps, executions, watches, receipts, source records and conversations. Reserved case/ärende for actual source-system cases and retained concrete Swedish interface wording.

Updated the product overview, architecture explanations, connector action guide, repository/documentation entry points and generated decision/context indexes. Existing Thread and calendar-specific Proposal names remain unchanged; the vocabulary does not establish new tables, job interfaces or stored continuation links. Earlier decision and review bodies retain their historical wording. No application behavior, dependencies or protocol contracts changed.

Verification through docker exec: docs:generate and docs:check passed (15 generated files, 84 decisions, 171 documents, local links and signed connector smoke). Both new diagrams parsed with the existing temporary Mermaid 11.12.0 validator in the devcontainer. Git diff whitespace check passed.
