Connector view catalogue and invoice research, 2026-11-08: added `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-connector-view-catalogue-and-document-previews.md` and `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-invoice-review-and-document-walkthrough.md`. The catalogue separates business views, document representations and human decisions; proposes document reading first, then additional attachment formats, a real-source pilot and metric/series drill-down. The walkthrough preserves D73's current desktop drawer/mobile sheet and covers source/version-bound review, one submission, receipts, access loss, changed documents, failures and non-AI operation. These are research proposals, not new protocol fields or accepted renderer exceptions.

Inspected Eneo's primary SvelteKit frontend at `ccb7dccd5e8f66dea7f1f9652d742ec13849bc7a` through its devcontainer: browser DOCX rendering with docx-preview 0.4.1, native browser PDF iframe, shared Markdown components and read-excel-file 9.3.10 sheet previews; traced original-byte URLs separately from extracted-text knowledge previews. Recorded size/row limits, native PDF quotation limits, workbook fidelity, source revision/cache implications and the difference between bearer original links and Clew's current-person authority. Direct DOCX preview is recommended for evaluation before mandatory conversion. PDF.js/docx-preview (Apache-2.0) and read-excel-file (MIT) were checked against upstream project documentation as candidates only; no dependencies were added or pinned. No application or Eneo files changed. The source comparison is code-based; browser fidelity and real-source behaviour are implementation gates.

Verification through docker exec: docs:generate and docs:check passed (15 generated files, 84 decisions, 173 documents, repository links and signed connector smoke); git diff --check passed. Concurrent D78 vocabulary documentation was preserved, and the new walkthrough uses its work item/source record/card distinction.

Full document and semantic catalogue, 2026-11-08 (D79): following explicit user confirmation to include the full proposed catalogue, added document, record, metrics, series and activity declarations and semantic output validation to protocol 2. Original files use a separate, bounded, authenticated binary route with metadata reread, revision/size/MIME/SHA-256 verification and a final authority check. Clew issues stateless review evidence bound to person, unit, grant and the complete document bundle; the shared approval gate covers cards, generic executions and job steps, while committed retries retain their original intent. The independent finance fixture supplies PDF invoices, DOCX agreements and two-sheet XLSX specifications, record facts/items/history, budget metrics and synthetic line/bar series. Its atomic source write rejects stale document versions; undo contains only confirmed refs.

The shared Astryx reader uses PDF.js canvas/selectable text plus a reflowing page-text alternative, a no-script DOCX iframe and bounded XLSX worker parsing. Original download, attachments, page/zoom, sheet selection, failure/retry and explicit metadata reload are provided. Required files gate the invoice approval button. Semantic charts always have data tables; metrics retain units/period/data time, and null remains missing. Reading positions survive attachment switches during the open reader. Markdown file previews remain deferred. All renderer-specific markup/styles are confined to D79's exception; surrounding layout and controls are Astryx.

Verification: `bun run check` passed with 579 unit/web tests, six Node runtime tests, formatting, types, dependency boundaries, purity, generated documentation/local links and signed connector smoke. `bun run test:db` passed all 54 tests. The targeted document/review browser suite passed all 13 scenarios, including actual PDF/DOCX/XLSX rendering, retained page position across attachments, metrics, both charts, activity, failed-read approval gating, keyboard/focus and 320 px review. DOCX's no-script frame cannot host axe messaging: host controls are audited and frame text is asserted separately, without weakening the sandbox. Screenshots were inspected. Scan items now carry an optional source revision; finance uses it to prevent preview or approval from mixing stale card facts with fresh documents.

The opted-in local development finance service was restarted and its stored manifest refreshed with a `connector.manifest` audit entry. Its enabled state, audience, credentials and tool settings were preserved; 13 capabilities and document transport are available. Other checkouts need the same restart/manifest refresh, and pre-existing pending cards adopt versioned review on the next source scan. Real municipal-file fidelity, manual screen-reader validation and a live source's conditional writes remain release work. No Eneo source edits, real-source writes or production deployment.

Local login startup fix, 2026-11-08: the document catalogue's wildcard re-export from `@clew/contracts` did not expose its new schema names across the CommonJS-to-ESM boundary used by the Node server. Startup failed with `DocumentBundleSchema` missing, leaving Vite's login proxy returning 502. Replaced the wildcard with explicit named value/type exports, preserving the public contract. Added a Node regression that loads the live API and composition without external services; it reproduced the startup error before the fix and passed afterward. The earlier unit and browser checks did not cover this production import graph.

Verification: formatting, TypeScript and all seven Node runtime tests passed. Restarted the local development processes and verified backend health/readiness return 200 and both web/portal login routes redirect to Keycloak with 303. No authentication settings or credentials changed.

Document review UX, 2026-11-08: the product owner reported scattered controls, repeated reloads and poor scaling, then requested retaining the stepper with a limit, clickable steps, identifying metadata, per-step/flow status and a small completion animation. Amended D73. Document and decision controls now have separate stable locations; two to four rows keep a clickable stepper with amounts below supplier names, and five or more use compact progress plus an overview for arbitrary-order navigation. Choices persist by source ref. Blue/amber icons and explicit text distinguish selected approvals and rows left behind; the flow reports review, ready, sending and failure. The existing Astryx check animation appears briefly inside the review only after all selected rows are confirmed done, with static reduced-motion feedback and the persistent receipt afterward.

Ordinary change notifications no longer invalidate open original bytes or review snapshots. Preview keys include the source item revision, so changed source records are reread; explicit reload and retry remain, while access/policy changes still clear all protected content. The browser regression exercises repeated notices, retained page/focus and changed-revision refusal. Kept the execution status row's layout stable during status checking to avoid replacing the focused button on desktop. No development server was started or restarted; the user owns that process. Browser checks use an isolated test container.

Verification: `bun run check` passed (579 unit/web cases, seven Node cases, formatting, types, dependency rules, purity and documentation checks). All 31 targeted browser scenarios passed across the final runs: the document/review/execution run passed 29, then the receipt-timing fix passed both motion modes, uncertain-result refusal and the complete approval-to-receipt flow. Completion uses the confirmed mutation response as well as the live cache, since cache notification can follow mutation completion. Desktop, 320 px, 4/5/10-item batches, clickable repeated-supplier steps, source revision changes, policy revocation and focus retention are covered. Production test builds and whitespace checks passed; desktop/phone/batch/completion screenshots were inspected. No manual screen-reader conformance is claimed.

False review expiry, 2026-11-08: the product owner hit “Granskningen har gått ut” immediately after reviewing. Reproduced under Node: contracts register TypeBox's date-time format in its CommonJS instance, while the server's ESM `Value.Check` has a separate registry and rejected a fresh `expiresAt` as an unknown format. Added `checkContract` beside the format registration so review validation uses the same registry as the schemas. Malformed evidence now reports verification failure separately from genuine expiry, which compares parsed instants and still expires at the two-hour boundary. No proof or version checks were relaxed.

Added a Node regression using the real finance fixture transport and review use case: fresh and just-before-expiry evidence pass, invalid timestamps and tampering fail, and evidence fails at exact expiry. It reproduced the user's error before the fix. All eight Node runtime tests, 12 document/approval tests and TypeScript checks passed; local health/readiness returned 200. No local invoice was approved and the user's development process was not restarted.

Clickable invoice amounts, 2026-11-08: the complete item label (supplier and amount) now forms the step button's visible and accessible name. Removed the separate amount text and put decision status in its own paragraph, fixing the amount/status concatenation. Updated D73 and the repeated-supplier browser scenario to select invoices by amount and exercise mouse and keyboard navigation. The scenario waits for the existing next-step focus transition before moving keyboard focus. All four targeted browser checks passed across the final runs, covering repeated suppliers, document review at 320 px and four-invoice batches on desktop and phone. Types, formatting, documentation and whitespace checks passed; desktop and phone screenshots were inspected. The user's development server was left running.

Review recovery and shared completion, 2026-11-08 (D80): fixed receipt-toast undo so a failed request stays visible and can be retried; the toast now reads live receipt state and expiry through the existing query provider. Unknown source outcomes do not offer undo or a fresh write. Status checking retains its controls and focus instead of briefly restoring the ordinary approval actions. Completed reviews remain addressable from their card and Klart, including after undo expires, with read-only per-row results and current source documents. The phone card now also remains available as the return target after expiry. Shared `CompletionMark`/`CompletionFeedback` use Astryx motion for review completion, confirmed receipts and successful saves, with reduced-motion support; late confirmation can trigger the success cue, but partial/unknown results cannot.

The user chose correction in Clew when the connector supports it, otherwise a source-system link. Optional record recovery metadata declares up to 12 bounded text fields and same-connector authorized correction/source capabilities. Corrections use the existing human execution ledger, source revision validation, audit and outbox; only confirmed success reloads the document and invalidates the row's previous selection/evidence. A definitive failed/partial receipt can create one deterministic retry card containing only failed rows, with a transactional `thread.retry_created` audit. Repeated/concurrent requests return the same retry even after it completes; confirmed source effects and the original receipt are preserved. Every new approval requires fresh review evidence. No database migration or new dependency.

The finance fixture now resolves the delayed invoice to an explicit missing-order rejection on operation lookup, offers `finance.correct`, and succeeds on a freshly reviewed retry after the order number is supplied. It refuses another approval of an already approved invoice under a new operation id. The source link remains a synthetic placeholder. Restarted only the opted-in local finance service and refreshed its registered manifest through the normal database/audit transaction (14 capabilities); preserved configuration, credentials, audience and invoice data. No user invoice was approved, undone or reset, and the user's application development server was neither started nor restarted. For the existing uncertain batch: Kontrollera status → Visa granskning → rejected row → Spara rättelse → Granska avvisade igen → fresh approval.

Verification through Docker: the full unit/web run passed 587 cases; the final affected finance/thread/receipt unit rerun passed 37. All eight Node runtime cases, nine PostgreSQL store cases, TypeScript, dependency boundaries, purity, documentation generation/checks and whitespace checks passed. The targeted browser runs cover 37 distinct scenarios across documents/review/executions/undo: real file rendering, 4/5/10-row navigation, completion/reduced motion, uncertain-result refusal, delayed status checks, undo rejection and retry, and correction/reopening after expiry on desktop and 320 px. Final recovery/archived-return rerun passed all four cases; screenshots were inspected. No manual screen-reader or live vendor conformance is claimed. Global `bun run check` is currently blocked only by formatting in the concurrently edited `fixtures/mcp-pulse/service.d.mts`. An earlier missing D81 documentation link was resolved by that concurrent work and the final documentation check passes. Those other-chat changes were preserved.


MCP chat services, 2026-11-08 (D81): accepted a second standards-based integration lane for provider-owned interactions in AI-enabled chat. Native connectors remain for Clew-owned/model-independent work. Added architecture and official-provider research: several employee SaaS vendors provide MCP, but pending respondent polls with an MCP App are not verified for those vendors. Standard OAuth, including manually registered clients, remains production work.

Replaced the initial browser-only lab after the user requested the real mock path. No product code imports fixtures or checks a person/provider name. Independent Compose services mcp-pulse and mcp-status expose standard MCP HTTP; the official client SDK discovers tools and reads results/App HTML. Migration 0035 stores separate MCP registrations, deliveries and decisions. dev-mcp.ts requires --person and seeds only endpoint/policy. Registered both sources for hillevi; both containers remain running. The default development interval is 30 seconds, configurable at initial registration.

Real Temporal mcpWatch workflows read approved tools without an open browser, rechecking person/model entitlement. Configured JSON pointers define novelty and eligibility; deliveries are deduplicated and audited transactionally. Home reads the generic inbox and links to /chat/services; /labs/mcp is a protected alias. Signed into the actual synthetic Hillevi account and verified her greeting, both services, the scheduled poll, and an outage delivered after changing only the status mock. The poll remains unanswered and open in the browser; status mock remains degraded for the demonstration. Source state is synthetic/in-memory; Clew registration/evidence/inbox persist.

App writes use the session/CSRF-protected human endpoint, operator allowlists and discovered schema validation. Durable decisions are claimed before dispatch, with audit and keyed fingerprints; encrypted receipts contain no raw poll score. Replays return saved receipts; unknown outcomes never automatically retry. The browser bridge is a restricted development sandbox. Vendor OAuth, production Apps lifecycle/origin/budgets, normal model tool routing/conversation opt-in, admin UI, unread/expiry and attention policy remain unfinished. Backend requires CLEW_POC=true and refuses production; normal web production builds omit the preview. D25 checks cover the new MCP boundary and forbid product imports of fixtures.

Verification: 8 unit scenarios, 1 Node HTTP interoperability case, 2 PostgreSQL cases (concurrency, actor filtering, audit rollback and encrypted receipts), and all 4 browser scenarios passed. Browser coverage includes Home discovery, AI-off, App cancellation/confirmation, keyboard, axe and 320px reflow. Typecheck, dependency/purity rules, documentation, production build, formatting and whitespace checks passed. Inspected desktop/mobile screenshots and the signed-in live page; proof is test-results/mcp-hillevi-live.png. No manual screen-reader or real-vendor conformance is claimed. Unrelated shared-tree edits were preserved.


MCP delivery cards, 2026-11-09 (D81 amendment): replaced the low-visibility text lines above the composer with separate Astryx cards in the work column below it. Each uses the existing work-card surface, source/time line, heading and prominent link button. The catalogue remains available through Alla tjänster. Opening a service/delivery now shows only that provider App or notice, with Till Hem; the catalogue and unrelated messages are absent from detail pages. Tool-only details and submission notices use the existing Banner component. No provider-name checks, custom CSS, provider schema or native-thread action assumptions were introduced.

Each delivery link now carries its own delivery id, included in the read cache key. The server verifies the card belongs to the current person/connection, compares its configured source identity to the latest read, and refuses to mount a new poll in an older invitation: the original delivered notice remains visible with a separate latest-state link. This is a read-path change with no database migration, new dependency or source mutation. Amended D81, architecture and fixture instructions.

Validation: all 5 MCP host unit cases and all 4 browser cases passed, including the new older-card/source-identity case, separate Home cards, isolated detail, keyboard focus, cancellation/confirmation, axe and 320px reflow. MCP card overlays, 2026-11-09 (D81 amendment): moved provider Apps and tools-only notices into the existing Astryx end Drawer, with a tall BottomSheet on phones, matching card reviews. Home and the service catalogue remain underneath; page filters and context stay in place. The selection lives in open-service/open-delivery query parameters. Close/Escape restore the opening control, browser Back/Forward follow the selected delivery, and the provider iframe is released after the exit animation. Validation through Docker: all six targeted MCP browser cases passed, including 320 px reflow/axe, desktop and phone confirmation, focus return, preserved filters, old delivery/latest source, AI-off and Back during a pending confirmation. MCP completion refresh, 2026-11-09 (D81 amendment): fixed completed poll cards remaining on Home. The watch now reconciles eligibility before unchanged-source-identity suppression. An authorized source read on opening/manual refresh or after a successful human decision also resolves the matching delivery; the browser immediately invalidates the shared service inbox. Resolution uses the configured JSON-pointer identity/condition over standard MCP results, never a poll-specific name, provider HTML or the mutation receipt. Missing fields, provider/read errors and uncertain mutations retain attention. A failed refresh preserves the committed receipt and leaves subsequent checks to the durable watch. Migration 0036 adds nullable resolved_at; active queries exclude resolved rows, direct delivery lookup retains old links and uniqueness prevents stale reads from resurrecting them. Resolution and its audit are transactional and scoped to person, connection and delivery. The open App remains visible after removal; close falls back to the service section when its opener has disappeared. Generic notices without an attention condition retain their existing behavior; expiry/acknowledgment and list-wide reconciliation remain separate work.

Validation: targeted memory scenarios and a shared memory/Postgres resolution contract cover immediate completion, outside answers on open/watch, unchanged identity, incomplete/failed reads, retained receipts, deduplication, next-week delivery, actor isolation, history and transactional rollback. Seven focused MCP browser tests passed including submission/removal, refresh after an outside answer, persistence across reload, old links, keyboard focus fallback, 320 px and axe checks. TypeScript, dependency and purity checks passed. Applied migration in dev and read the existing answered poll through Hillevi’s live overlay; its card disappeared from Home with focus on Alla tjänster. Screenshot: test-results/mcp-hillevi-completed.png. No new live poll answer was submitted, provider state was not reset and no dependencies were added. Prior overlay work and concurrent documentation/presentation changes were preserved.


Service catalogue placement and interface spaces, 2026-11-09 (D83): replaced the MCP catalogue page with the existing end Drawer/tall mobile BottomSheet over the current page. The catalogue stays mounted while its selected App/notice opens as a sibling overlay above it; closing returns through the catalogue to the opener. Confirmation remains above the App. Old /chat/services and /labs/mcp catalogue links redirect to Home plus the catalogue query; existing selected-item links still open their exact item. Moved catalogue content into labs/mcp/service-catalogue.tsx, with services-overlay.tsx composing the parent and existing service-overlay.tsx; the former page is gone. If completing a delivery removes its catalogue entry, focus stays inside the parent instead of moving behind the modal.

Consolidated the existing D42/D50/D66/D72/D73/D81 placement rules into docs/reference/interface-spaces.md, recorded D83, and linked it from the documentation home, architecture, vocabulary, llms.txt and CLAUDE.md. Destinations own the main page, ordinary related content uses the native context panel, focused interactions use overlays, and confirmation/feedback belongs to the owning interaction. Page-owned day content remains distinct from opened panels. No generic native-panel rewrite, new dependency or backend change in this turn. Updated MCP docs and implementation status. Inspected desktop/mobile captures and Hillevi's live dev flow: All services → unanswered week 47 App → close back into the catalogue with focus on its entry. Left the catalogue open; saved test-results/mcp-hillevi-catalogue-overlay.png. No live answer was submitted. The existing Astryx compositions and user screenshots remain the visual reference; private canvas and manual screen-reader verification remain unavailable.


MCP overlay/completion commit preparation, 2026-11-09: prepared an isolated snapshot excluding the concurrent MCP-backed connector research and presentation. Reconciled the newly merged absence connector from origin/main and assigned the interface-space decision D83, preserving its already-published D82. Frozen dependency installation, formatting, the full check (617 unit/web tests, nine Node runtime cases, types, dependency rules, purity and documentation/signed-connector smoke) passed on the combined snapshot. The 12 targeted MCP browser and three Postgres scenarios were already verified during implementation; the production build excludes the development overlays.

DBOS spike, 2026-11-09 (branch `spike/dbos`, worktree `.claude/worktrees/dbos-spike`): the product owner asked whether DBOS Transact could replace Temporal and, since the project is pre-release, for a spike to decide early. `spikes/dbos-reminders/` (outside the workspace, MIT dependencies only: `@dbos-inc/dbos-sdk` 5.4.0, `pg`) ports `durableReminder` with the D47 contract to DBOS in two variants and drives several instances through SIGKILL, restart, replacement, takeover and code changes against one Postgres database that holds both the spike's tables and DBOS's `dbos` schema. Findings and the recommendation are in `docs/reviews/2026-11-09-dbos-spike-durable-reminders.md`.

Proven: the contract holds in every kill scenario (fire once, cancel, replace, stale generation); start and cancel of a workflow run inside the ledger's transaction through `dbos.enqueue_workflow` and `dbos.send_message`, 1.8 ms median commit on a quiet host; a dead executor's workflows stay PENDING until an executor with the same id restarts, or a live instance calls the unexported `recoverPendingWorkflows` for it; only the latest registered application version dequeues, so a pinned version per build and a promote-on-launch are needed; `resumeWorkflow` does not move an old-version workflow but `forkWorkflow` onto the current version does; a queue concurrency limit holds sleeping reminders; `DBOS.recv` polls every 10 s while `DBOS.sleep` is an in-process timer. Not covered: the other six workflows, load with thousands of sleepers, backup/restore and the runbook, the missing UI. No product code changed; the spike database `clew_dbos_spike` was created in the devcontainer Postgres. The branch is not merged: the owner decides between Temporal and DBOS on the review.

DBOS replaces Temporal, 2026-11-09 (D85, branch `dbos`, worktree `.claude/worktrees/dbos-spike`): the product owner chose DBOS after the spike. `apps/server/src/temporal/` is replaced by `apps/server/src/dbos/`: the seven workflows on `DBOS.registerWorkflow` with steps through named activities bound by composition (`activities.ts`) or by a runtime test, the engine runtime (`runtime.ts`: Clew's `DATABASE_URL` as the system database, `CLEW_EXECUTOR_ID` defaulting to the host name, `CLEW_RELEASE` as the pinned version, promote-on-launch), stable-id start and cancel helpers, the heartbeat and takeover (`heartbeat.ts`: `clew_durable.executor`, claim by delete, adoption through the engine's unexported recovery routine), and the three delivery adapters. The legacy `reminder` workflow, `fireLegacyReminder` and the v46 replay fixture are gone; `ReminderTimers` stays as a port for the use cases and memory adapter. The outboxes remain and still deliver committed ids after commit; the in-transaction start through `dbos.enqueue_workflow` is the next step and is named in D85 and the status page. The MCP watch reconciler starts rounds of at most a hundred reads under numbered ids instead of `continueAsNew`. Compose loses `temporal` and `temporal-ui` and the 3023 port; the reliability workflow starts PostgreSQL only. The dependency cruiser names `dbos/` where it named `temporal/` and the circular-import exception is gone. The `job.status` value `temporal` is kept and documented as the engine owner's historical name.

Verification in the worktree: `bun run check` (formatting, types, dependency rules, purity, the unit suite, the Node suite, documentation) and `bun run test:durability`: the execution lane with the independent SQLite source, the watch lane, and the timer lane extended with a second instance (`tests/fixtures/durable-child.ts`) SIGKILLed while a reminder waits, whose heartbeat the test ages, claims once and adopts, and whose reminder then fires exactly once here. Not verified: the development server itself, which runs from the main checkout with the Temporal code until this branch is merged and the stack is recreated; the first launch on a database that still has the Temporal containers is harmless, since nothing reads them. The WCAG suite was not run; no UI changed.

In-transaction workflow starts, 2026-11-09 (D86, branch `claude/friendly-tu-5530d4`, built on `main` at 2a90c8f after the DBOS switch landed as D85): the ledger now starts every durable workflow inside the transaction that commits its intent, through `dbos.enqueue_workflow` on the transaction's own connection (`packages/db/src/durable.ts`), and the three outbox loops and their delivery adapters are gone (`dbos/timers.ts`, `dbos/executions.ts`, `threads/usecases/timer-dispatch.ts`, `modules/executions/dispatch.ts`, `dispatchJobs`). `ThreadTx.enqueue` and `ExecutionTx.enqueue` take a `WorkflowStart`; `scheduleJob` enqueues the job's workflow with the row; the start helpers (`reminderStart`, `executionStart`, `watchStart`, `jobStart`) live in the domain files and keep the old ids. Every Postgres store adapter takes a `DurableQueue` (`composition/durable.ts`: application `clew`, queue `clew.ledger`, since queue names are unique across the applications sharing a system database); the runtime registers the queue on launch with no concurrency limit and the four enqueued workflows are registered `portable`. A cancelled or replaced timer generation is still cancelled with `cancelDurable` after the commit, collected per transaction by the thread store (`ThreadStoreOptions.cancel`, also behind the follow-up store); the fence covers the rest. `bun run db:init` now also runs `DBOS.migrate` so the engine's schema exists before the first write. The outbox tables and columns stay as evidence (a drop migration would have collided with 0037); an active timer is `scheduled` as soon as it exists and `queued` is produced by nothing; the reliability counts read the engine's queue (`ENQUEUED` rows older than thirty seconds). Memory adapters record `enqueued` (and the thread store `cancelled`) for the unit tests; `tests/db/durable.ts` gives the Postgres tests an application no runner serves and reads the engine's rows back.

Verification in the worktree through Docker: `bun run check` (formatting, types, dependency rules, purity, 621 unit/web cases, the Node suite, generated documentation) and `bun run test:db` (59 cases, including the new rollback case: a rolled-back intent leaves no `dbos.workflow_status` row, a committed one is `ENQUEUED` in the application's ledger queue with the JSON input). `bun run test:durability`: the timer lane commits a reminder before the engine launches and sees it fire once the engine does, cancels a waiting reminder and a replaced generation after commit, refuses the old generation's late fire, and still adopts the killed child's workflow; the execution lane commits the approval's start before launch and replays it against the finished workflow; the watch lane commits both `jobWatch` starts before launch and replays one. Two findings from the lane: DBOS queue names are unique across the applications that share a system database, so the queue is `<application>.ledger` and a test engine registers and removes its own; and a cancelled reminder keeps its in-process `DBOS.sleep` timer until the due time, which held the Node test process open after `DBOS.shutdown`, so the lane runs with `--test-force-exit` (the server never exits on its own, so nothing changes there). Not verified: the development server itself (it runs from the main checkout) and the WCAG suite (no UI changed; the `queued` card text is unreachable now, not removed).

Shared header for the portal and the intranet, 2026-11-09 (D87, proposed): the product owner asked for a header shared by Clew and a Sitevision intranet so a person cannot tell the sites apart (Start to `/portal`, the sections to `/intranat`, reference https://start.sundsvall.dev/intranat/), built with Astryx here first. Built `packages/site-header`: the menu as JSON with a strict parser, `SiteHeader` (TopNav, logo, places, host controls; a MobileNav drawer when standalone below `xl`), `SiteHeaderPerson`, and `<site-header>`, a custom element that renders in an open shadow root with Astryx's CSS and `createClewTheme` adopted into it (Theme handed the theme as built so nothing reaches the host's document but the font face). The product owner added that parts of the intranet are public: with `sitevision-root` the element lists the sections from Sitevision's headless endpoint with the visitor's own session, so anonymous visitors get the public pages; `scripts/sitevision-menu.ts` (later `scripts/site-menu.ts`) writes the public menu anonymously by default and `--as-user` warns that its file is not for anonymous visitors. `@clew/ui` gained `./theme` and `./locale` exports (the Swedish catalogue moved to `locale.ts`) so the embed does not pull in the calendar and documents. The portal's top bar is now the shared header and Clew's places a SideNav (D67 amended); the portal's dev server serves an intranet stand-in at `/intranat/` with loud global CSS, a fake headless endpoint (`fixtures/sitevision-tree.json`, two sections not public), a demo sign-in cookie and the built bundle at `/intranat/?built`. Verified: typecheck, formatting, 11 unit cases, the 3 new WCAG cases (`sh scripts/a11y.sh site-header.spec.ts`, Sundsvall profile), the production bundle (193 kB gzipped) and the dev embed in the browser at 1280, 1100, 375 and 320 px, anonymous and signed in to the stand-in. Not verified: the portal signed in (the dev sign-in is on `keycloak.orb.local`), a live Sitevision, a screen reader. Next: the product owner compares the two sides signed in; run the menu script against the real intranet; decide on an overflow menu and a cached per-visitor menu; then move the package to its own repository with the theme.

Shared header sources, 2026-11-09 (D87 amended): the product owner asked that the header not be bound to Sitevision (another installation may list from WordPress; Clew should only receive addresses and map them) and asked whether Sitevision returns whole URLs. It returns both: `URI` (the path) and `URL` (absolute). The listing is now a source port in `packages/site-header/src/sources/` (`MenuSource.list`, `walkTree`, `withListing`, `absoluteHref`) with two adapters, `sitevision` (path resolved on the public origin, preferred over `URL`, which may be an internal host behind a proxy) and `wordpress` (`/wp-json/wp/v2/pages?parent=`, absolute `link`, `title.rendered` to text, `X-WP-Nonce` from `source-nonce`), named only in `sources/index.ts`. The element takes `source`, `source-root`, `source-depth`, `source-base` and `source-nonce`; the script is `scripts/site-menu.ts --source …` with `SITE_MENU_USER`/`SITE_MENU_PASSWORD` for `--as-user`. `selectedItem` treats a trailing slash as the same page. The stand-in also answers `/wp-json/wp/v2/pages` (`/intranat/?source=wordpress`). Verified: 16 unit cases, the 4 WCAG cases (one new for WordPress), typecheck and the WordPress source in the browser. Neither adapter has met a live site.

Portal sign-out in the dev stack, 2026-11-09: the portal (3024) signed the person out on the first click because `apps/server/.env.local` had no `FRONTEND_ORIGINS`; `.env.example` said `bun run auth:setup` writes it, but the script never did, so the CSRF origin check refused the portal's first mutation (D67 item 6). `scripts/auth-setup.ts` now writes `FRONTEND_ORIGINS=http://localhost:3024` and keeps an operator's own list. The product owner's local `.env.local` got the line by hand (setup was not re-run, so Keycloak was not touched) and the server was restarted; the allowed origins now resolve to 3020 and 3024.

Shared header colour and person, 2026-11-09 (D87 amended): the product owner asked to fix the colour support and could not switch away from dark mode on the portal (3024), whose person menu had only "Logga ut". The intranet's header had been always light and never drew the white logo on a dark bar. Now: the embed follows the person's stored choice (`clew:color-mode`, exported as `APPEARANCE_PREFERENCE_KEY` from the new `@clew/ui/appearance-preference`, which `appearance.tsx` imports) or the system, live; `SiteHeader` picks `darkSrc` by `useDarkSiteHeader`; `SiteHeaderPerson` opens a `Dialog` with Utseende (Samma som datorn, Ljust, Mörkt) and "Logga ut" in both hosts. Three shadow-root faults found and fixed: `:host { all: initial }` cleared `color-scheme`, which Astryx's reset sets only on `<html>`, so every token resolved light (the host carries `data-site-header-mode`); a colour under a transition kept its old `light-dark()` value on a scheme change (one frame of `transition: none` in the shadow root); and Astryx's `DropdownMenu` reads `document.activeElement`, so its rows could not be chosen by keyboard inside the shadow root (replaced by the dialog). The product owner then asked whether the person was mocked: the stand-in showed a demo cookie's "Kim Andersson". It now reads the Clew session from the BFF, as an intranet with single sign-on would; the demo cookie stays for the WCAG suite. D87 records that the person is always the host's to name and that the same person on both sides needs a shared identity provider. Verified: typecheck and the 6 WCAG cases (colour by system and by stored choice, keyboard choice in the dialog with the dialog kept open and focus returned). Not verified: the stand-in signed in as a real Clew user (no session here), the portal's own live switch. `tests/a11y/appearance.spec.ts` fails on an unmocked `GET /chat-services` from the MCP work (`bc3006a`), not from this change.

Sign-out from the portal and the stand-in, 2026-11-09: the product owner could not sign out. `POST /api/auth/logout` (`apps/server/src/routes/auth.ts`) compared `Origin` with `PUBLIC_URL` alone, missed in D67's `FRONTEND_ORIGINS` change, so the portal's "Logga ut" got 403; it now takes the same list as every other mutation (checked live: 3024 403 before, 200 after; an unknown origin still 403). The intranet stand-in had no sign-out for a Clew-signed-in visitor; `/intranat/logga-ut` now ends the Clew session through the BFF's own sign-out with the session's CSRF token and redirects to the provider's end-session address, and refuses a cross-site request (`Sec-Fetch-Site`). Verified: the full check, the 6 WCAG cases, the stand-in's sign-out for the demo person by curl. Not verified: signing out as a real Clew user (no session here).
Users, SCIM and group mapping, 2026-11-09 (branch `model-reasoning-setting`, documentation only): the product owner asked how to manage users in the platform with SCIM and how it relates to the municipality's SSO and to Eneo. Settled in conversation and recorded as D85: SSO (OIDC login) and SCIM (provisioning) stay separate and both hold; the identity provider choice decides only the claim that binds a login to the provisioned `userName` and the Graph token path (HANDOVER item 10), not SCIM, since the provisioner is the client. Eneo has no SCIM: users exist per tenant beforehand and are matched on e-mail, which is why D68 requires a work address. The source records a department and a manager flag, from which the provisioner derives D68's `unit:` and `unit-manager:` groups. Directory groups are arbitrary and dynamic, so the SCIM server keeps every group and Clew maps groups to capabilities in a small administered table (`admin.users`, audited, derived never granted, fail closed), amending D68 item 2; capability names become a closed list in `@clew/contracts` (`admin.connectors` today; `calendar:plan` is seeded and never checked). D68, `docs/architecture/identity.md` and the status matrix link D85. Nothing built; D68's must-land-first items (narrow `authorizationVersion`, narrow `granted()`) and its open questions (production IdP claim, Metakatalogen's API and stable id, protected people) stand. Two questions raised and closed by the product owner the same day: startsidan is Clew's earlier name, so there is no outside capability list to review, only checks to port; and everyone the provisioner pushes gets an account, since who is pushed is decided outside Clew and an account without a `unit:` membership can do nothing.

Step 1 of the SCIM order of work, 2026-11-09 (branch `model-reasoning-setting`): D68's must-land-first items. `authorizationVersion` in `apps/server/src/auth-crypto.ts` now hashes `authorityOf(access)`, a fixed-order projection of enabled, organisation, unit, role, sorted deduplicated capabilities and grant version, instead of the whole `{ person, grant }` row, so a name correction, a unit relabel or a new column (SCIM's `externalId`, `userName`, e-mail) no longer stops durable work; `tests/unit/authorization-version.test.ts` holds the field list. `granted()` in `apps/server/src/scheduler/adapters/postgres.ts` takes enabled people with a grant and an identity binding, so a whole municipality can be pushed in without the nightly scan running for everyone; `tests/db/scheduler-people.test.ts` covers it on Postgres. The runbook gained "Changing the authorization fingerprint": the formula change itself invalidates every stored version once on first deployment (executions and watches block with `authority-revoked`, retained answers hide until the next scan), reasoned from code and not rehearsed on a populated installation. `composition/mcp.ts` had reused `authorizationVersion` as a keyed hash for poll answers; it now calls `keyedDigest(purpose, value)` in `auth-crypto.ts`, which computes the same value as before, so stored MCP decision fingerprints are unchanged. D68, `identity.md` and the status matrix updated. Verified in the container: `bun run test:db` (58 Postgres tests, including the new scan-population case) and `bun run check` green (625 unit/web tests, types, dependency rules, purity, Node tests, documentation check). Not done: a rehearsal of the fingerprint change on a populated database, and a signed-in pass; nothing in the browser changed.

Step 2 of the SCIM order of work, 2026-11-09 (same branch): the identity data model for D68/D85. Migration `0038_identity_provisioning` (**run `bun run db:init` after pulling**; applied to the dev database) adds `external_id`, `user_name` and `email` to `auth_person` (nullable, unique), `auth_unit`, `auth_assignment` (person, unit, role; several per person), `auth_group` and `auth_group_member` (every group as pushed, members cascade with the group), and `auth_capability_map` (group external id to capability, no foreign key so a mapping outlives a removed group and applies again on its return). The ALTER on `auth_person` is wrapped in `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE/DEFERRED` because of the deferred policy trigger from 0018/0019 (as 0030 did). A backfill makes a unit row of every unit the grants know and an assignment of every grant, idempotently; `auth_access_grant` stays as the derived projection (active assignment and capabilities) that `readAccess`, the actor and the fingerprint use, so nothing in the request path changed. `@clew/contracts` gained `CAPABILITIES` (`admin.connectors`, `admin.users`), `Capability`, `CapabilitySchema` and `isCapability`; `calendar:plan` is gone from the seed (D85: seeded, checked nowhere) and Mikael holds both capabilities. `scripts/auth-setup.ts` writes the person's `dev:<id>`, user name and `<id>@clew.test`, the unit, the assignment and the grant; `postgresUnits()` reads `auth_unit` instead of distinct grant columns. Tests: `tests/db/identity-model.test.ts` (several assignments, units by label, one assignment per unit, group removal cascades to members and spares the mapping, unique user name, legacy rows with empty columns), `tests/unit/capabilities.test.ts`. Docs: `identity.md` data model paragraph, D68 and D85 statuses, the status matrix. Verified in the container: `bun run check` green (627 unit/web tests, documentation check) and `bun run test:db` green (60 Postgres tests). Not done: the derivation (step 3), the seed has not been rerun against Keycloak here (it needs the administrator credentials), and no browser change.

Step 3 of the SCIM order of work, 2026-11-09 (same branch): the derivation. `apps/server/src/modules/people/domain.ts` is pure: `deriveAuthority(memberships, mapping, knownLabels)` reads D68's keys in a group's `externalId` (`unit:`, `unit-manager:` wins for the same unit, `capability:` from the closed list, `protected`) and D85's mapping rows (only for groups the person is in, only closed-list names), and returns units labelled by their `unit:` group, every assignment sorted by unit id, the active one (`activeOf`: manager before employee, then the lowest unit id), capabilities and the grant projection; a manager-only unit takes its label from the unit table or falls back to its id. `ports.ts` adds `ProvisionTx`/`ProvisionStore`; `usecases.ts` has `provisioningUsecases(deps)` with `deriveWithin(tx, actor, personId)` for a caller's own transaction, `derivePerson` and `deriveGroup` (every member, for a mapping change). An unchanged grant writes nothing; a changed one writes `grant.derived` on `person:<id>` with unit, role, capabilities and the assignment count, or `removed: true`; `protected` never reaches the audit row. Adapters: `adapters/postgres.ts` (`postgresProvisionStore`, upserts units and the grant, replaces assignments, `writeAudit`) and `adapters/memory.ts` (seedable state, snapshot rollback); paired in `composition/people.ts` as `provisioning`, which nothing calls yet (the SCIM routes and the mapping editor will). `tests/provisioning-store-contract.ts` (five scenarios: keys and mapping with a silent re-derivation, several units and the label fallback, fail-closed removal, a mapping change over a group, rollback) runs on memory in `tests/unit/provisioning.test.ts` (plus the pure cases) and on Postgres in `tests/db/postgres-provisioning.test.ts`. Verified in the container: `bun run check` green (636 unit/web tests, dependency rules, purity, documentation check) and `bun run test:db` green (65 Postgres tests). Not done: storing or applying `protected` (D68's open question), the seed still writing the grant directly until the synthetic provisioner pushes groups (step 5).

Step 4 of the SCIM order of work, 2026-11-09 (same branch): the SCIM 2.0 server. Migration `0039_scim_provisioners` (**run `bun run db:init` after pulling**; applied here) adds `auth_provisioner` (id, organisation, label, token digest, revoked_at), gives `auth_group` a server `id` as primary key with `external_id` optional and unique (members reference the id), and `created_at`/`updated_at` on person and group for SCIM `meta`; drizzle-kit could not order the primary-key move without a prompt, so the SQL is hand-written over the generated snapshot. Module `apps/server/src/modules/people/`: `contracts.ts` (TypeBox inputs for User, Group and PatchOp, permissive to extensions as the standard requires; `ScimError` with `scimType`), `domain.ts` (field mapping with D68's requirements checked by name, `parseFilter` for one equality, `pageOf`, `applyUserPatch`/`applyGroupPatch` covering paths, schema-qualified paths, the path-less object form and `members[value eq "…"]`, resources, discovery documents), `ports.ts` (SCIM reads and writes on `ProvisionTx`), `usecases.ts` (`scimUsecases({ store, clock, ids, digest })`: authenticate, create/get/list/replace/patch/delete for users and groups; users scoped to the provisioner's organisation, groups to the installation; `scim.user.*`/`scim.group.*` audit rows with actor `provisioner:<id>` and re-derivation of touched members in the same transaction; a renamed or re-keyed group re-derives all members; `DELETE` user deactivates, idempotent). `routes/scim.ts` is a factory (`scimRoutes(scim)`) mounted at `/scim/v2` in `main.ts` behind its own readiness guard; bearer token on every address, `application/scim+json`, the error resource in English, 201 with `Location`, 204 on delete. `composition/people.ts` exports `scim` (and `provisioning` as the same object). `scripts/provisioner-token.ts add|revoke` issues a token (printed once; Clew keeps `tokenDigest`) with an audit row. Tests: `tests/scim-store-contract.ts` on memory (`tests/unit/scim.test.ts`) and Postgres (`tests/db/postgres-provisioning.test.ts`), `tests/unit/scim.test.ts` (use cases: required attributes by name, uniqueness per organisation, filters and paging, PUT/PATCH/DELETE including Entra's path-less `Replace`, unit and manager groups driving grants, capability key and mapping, renamed unit relabelling, fail-closed on group removal, patch and filter rules), `tests/unit/scim-routes.test.ts` (401 with challenge, discovery, HTTP round trip, error resource). Harnesses and `identity-model.test.ts` moved to the group id. Verified in the container: `bun run check` green (655 unit/web tests, documentation check) and `bun run test:db` green (68 Postgres tests); live on the development stack, a token from `provisioner-token.ts add` answered 401 without and 200 with the token on `/scim/v2/ServiceProviderConfig` and listed the three seeded people, then the token was revoked and answered 401 (the seeded people have no `userName` until `bun run auth:setup` is rerun, so a filter on it finds nothing). Not done: a provisioner running against it (step 5 makes the dev seed one), a real Entra or Metakatalogen client, storing `protected`, groups are not organisation-scoped (one organisation per installation is the assumption; documented in `ports.ts`).

Step 5 of the SCIM order of work, 2026-11-09 (same branch): the synthetic provisioner (D68 item 7). `scripts/provisioner/directory.ts` is the development stack's source: three people with department, manager flag and capabilities, and three units; nothing in `apps/` or `packages/` reads it. `push.ts` maps that to D68's groups (`plan()`, pure: `unit:<id>` labelled by the unit, `unit-manager:<id>` "Chefer · <label>", `capability:<name>` "Clew · <name>") and pushes idempotently (`push()`: find by `externalId` then POST or PUT, PATCH `active: false` for a `dev:` person who left, DELETE for an owned-key group that no longer follows; groups with other keys are left alone), through `client.ts` (a small SCIM client whose `fetch` may be a Hono app's `request`). `index.ts` is `bun run dev:provision` over HTTP (`CLEW_URL`, token from `--token`, `DEV_PROVISIONER_TOKEN` or `.auth-test.env`). `scripts/auth-setup.ts` no longer writes units, assignments or grants: it creates the Keycloak accounts and the person rows with the ids the calendars and fixtures key on (`hillevi`, `hassan`, `mikael`, the identity bootstrap the bindings need until D68 item 6 lands), issues the `dev` provisioner token (reused while its digest is live; `DEV_PROVISIONER_TOKEN` in `.auth-test.env`, `provisioner.added` audit row) and pushes the directory through `scimRoutes(scim)` in-process. Found and fixed on the way: `deriveWithin` compared the stored grant with names outside the closed list filtered away, so Hillevi's legacy `calendar:plan` survived; it now compares as stored and a user PUT always derives, so a push heals a projection from before provisioning (contract case `a grant from before provisioning is rewritten`, both adapters). `docs/guides/writing-a-provisioner.md` is the guide, linked from the documentation home; local development, identity, D68 and the status matrix updated. The Postgres provisioning harness now reuses a capability group the development stack already has and removes only groups it created; two earlier runs with a broken cleanup had left test people in the development database, removed by hand (the `calendar-test-*` people from another suite were left as found). Live: `auth:setup` reported 3 users replaced, 4 groups created, 2 replaced, 1 removed (the leftover `capability:admin.everything` from a test), `dev:provision` over HTTP then 3 replaced and 6 groups replaced, and the grants read back as Hillevi manager of Bergsåkers skola, Hassan employee, Mikael with both capabilities. Verified in the container: `bun run check` green (660 unit/web tests, documentation check) and `bun run test:db` green (69 Postgres tests). Open design point for step 6 (binding at first login): once the provisioner creates people, their ids are server-assigned, and `packages/calendar/src/accounts.ts` and the HR fixture's actor map key on `hillevi`; either they move to `userName` (which `AuthenticatedContext` would then carry) or the development seed keeps creating the person rows as it does now. Protocol 2's `Actor` carries no `externalId` or `userName`, so a real connector has nothing but Clew's id to map a person with; worth a decision before a real source is connected.

Step 6 of the SCIM order of work, 2026-11-09 (same branch): binding at first login (D68 item 6). `bindFirstLogin(issuer, subject, claim, value)` in `packages/db/src/auth.ts`: in one transaction, the enabled person whose `userName` equals the claim's value, exactly one; a person already bound to this issuer with the same subject is simply resolved, with another subject refused (`identity-conflict` in the authentication log, `identity.conflict` audit row on the person: a changed subject needs a review); otherwise the binding is inserted (`onConflictDoNothing` so a subject that another person's login claimed meanwhile is refused) with `identity-bound` and an `identity.bound` audit row carrying issuer and claim name. No match, two matches or a disabled person is `identity-unmatched` with the claim value as actor, so an operator sees which account tried. `completeLogin` in `apps/server/src/auth.ts` calls it when `resolveSubject` finds nothing, with the claim named by `OIDC_BINDING_CLAIM` (default `preferred_username`, new in `authSettings()`); an e-mail is never the match and a login never creates a user. `scripts/auth-setup.ts` no longer writes identity bindings; it still refuses when an existing binding names another Keycloak account. Test: `tests/db/identity-binding.test.ts` (bind and reuse, no grant means no access, changed subject, unknown and disabled userName, an e-mail, a subject already bound elsewhere). Live: Hillevi's development binding was deleted and `bun scripts/auth-dev-check.ts hillevi` signed in through Keycloak; the log shows `identity-bound` then `login` for `person:hillevi` and a new binding to the Keycloak subject (the script then failed at its known "No thread with proposals" limit, unrelated). `identity.md`, D68, the status matrix and local development updated. Verified in the container: `bun run check` green (660 unit/web tests, documentation check) and `bun run test:db` green (71 Postgres tests). Still open: the person-id question noted under step 5 (the seed still creates the three person rows), storing `protected`, the users page and the mapping editor (step 8), connector audiences on groups (step 7).

Step 7 of the SCIM order of work, 2026-11-09 (same branch): audiences on groups (D23's `groups`, which matched nothing until now). `AuthenticatedContext.groups?: string[]` carries the external ids of the person's provisioned groups (`readGroups` in `packages/db/src/auth.ts`, read in `authenticate` and `systemContext`; optional so every test persona still typechecks). `inAudience` in `packages/connectors/src/index.ts` takes an `AudiencedActor` (`Actor & { groups? }`) and matches `audience.groups` against them; `Registry.visible` takes the same, so the registry, the assistant's model choice and the job bindings all honour group audiences. Administration: a `Groups` port (`connectors/model.ts`), `postgresGroups()` (groups with an external id, by name), `listGroups` in the admin use cases, `GET /api/admin/groups`, `api.admin.groups`, `adminQueries.groups`, and the "Grupper" MultiSelector in `pages/admin/audience.tsx` is live on the connector, model and add dialogs (disabled with a message only while no group has been provisioned). Tests: `tests/unit/connectors.test.ts` (a group in the audience is enough, a person outside every group matches nothing), the admin and menu unit deps gained `groups`, the browser mock serves `/admin/groups` from `tests/a11y/data.ts`, and `tests/a11y/admin.spec.ts` chooses a group by keyboard and saves. D23, `identity.md` and the status matrix updated. Verified in the container: `bun run check` green (661 unit/web tests, documentation check), `bun run test:db` green (71 Postgres tests); `sh scripts/a11y.sh admin.spec.ts --timeout 15000` ran 28 cases, 26 passed including the new group case, and two failed on an unmocked `GET /chat-services` from Hem (`the entry point shows only to an administrator`, `Administration opens and closes like Områden`), which this session's changes do not touch: the browser mock in `tests/a11y/support.ts` has no `/chat-services` route since the MCP overlays merged from `main` (`ee638d5`), a pre-existing gap worth closing separately. No manual screen-reader pass of the group selector.

Step 8 of the SCIM order of work, 2026-11-09 (same branch): Administration › Användare and D85's mapping editor. Contracts: `UserAdminRow`, `CapabilityMapping`, `UsersAdminOverview`, `CapabilityMapInput`. `usersAdminUsecases` in `modules/people/usecases.ts` (behind `admin.users`, `ForbiddenError` otherwise): `overview` lists the organisation's people by name with user name, assignments with labels, the active grant, capabilities, groups and whether a binding exists, plus the mapping rows (a row whose group is gone shows `displayName: null`), the groups with an external id and the closed capability list; `map`/`unmap` step up through `persona.authorize()`, refuse an unknown capability or an unprovisioned group (a stale mapping may still be removed), write `capability-map.added`/`removed` on `group:<externalId>` with the capability and the group's name, and re-derive every member in the same transaction. Ports: `assignmentsOf`, `bound`, `setMapping`; `userGroups` now carries `externalId`. Routes `GET /api/admin/users`, `POST /api/admin/users/capability-map`, `POST …/capability-map/remove`; the session's `admin` flag is true for `admin.connectors` or `admin.users`, so the Administration entry shows for either (a person with one capability meets the server's refusal on the other page; a per-row gate is a refinement left open). Web: `pages/admin/users.tsx` (Table on desktop, List on a phone; people with unit · role, capability badges, group count and a status badge: Har loggat in / Inte inloggad ännu / Ingen åtkomst / Avstängd; a form with two Astryx `Selector`s and "Lägg till"; a mapping table with "Ta bort" per row; toasts "Sparat: …" / "Borttaget: …"), the rail row Användare, the route `/admin/users`, `api.admin.users/mapCapability/unmapCapability`, `adminQueries.users`, `capabilityLabel` and `roleOfOne` in `labels.ts`. Tests: `tests/unit/users-admin.test.ts` (overview, forbidden, map/unmap with audit and derivation, stale mapping, unknown capability), the SCIM store contract gained the three ports on both adapters, the browser mock serves the overview and the two actions with `usersOverview()` in `tests/a11y/data.ts`, and `tests/a11y/users.spec.ts` (axe on desktop and phone, the list's words, mapping by keyboard with announcements and removal, the refusal, reflow at 320 px). D62, D85, `identity.md` and the status matrix updated. Verified in the container: `bun run check` green (666 unit/web tests, documentation check), `bun run test:db` green (72 Postgres tests), `sh scripts/a11y.sh users.spec.ts` 6 of 6 (the refusal case first tripped on Astryx's empty assertive live region sharing `role=alert`; the locator now filters on text), and `admin.spec.ts` as before (26 of 28, the two `/chat-services` mock gaps). Not done: a signed-in pass of the page on the development stack (the Keycloak sign-in is the product owner's), a manual screen-reader pass, a comparison against the design canvas, and a per-row gate so a person with only one admin capability does not see the other rows.

Step 9 of the SCIM order of work, 2026-11-09 (same branch, documentation only): `docs/architecture/identity.md` rewritten around what is built (Clew's local authority, Provisioning, Derivation, Administration › Användare, What is not built); D68 and D85 marked built with a "Built" chronology on D68; the status matrix's users row consolidated and the SCIM half of the "External agent MCP and SCIM" row moved into it; the operations runbook gained "Provisioning" (tokens, the daily run, what each audit action means, what an operator reads when a person reaches nothing); the vision names what Clew holds about a person as D68 defines it; the backend structure describes `modules/people`; `CLAUDE.md` gained the users rule (core entities, SCIM only, derived grant, closed capability list, provisioner guide); the guide index and `llms.txt` link the provisioner guide; HANDOVER's next steps (item 13) and open questions (item 11) list what identity still leaves open. Authentication overview for integrators, 2026-11-09: a colleague asked whether MCP has OAuth, since Oliver is about to look at the case (ärende) part and a note on a case would need a write. Wrote `docs/guides/authentication-overview.md` as the orientation page before `authorization.md`: the two lanes side by side, then the native flow a case note follows (OIDC sign-in, the transport key and Clew's RS256 assertion to the connector, the connector's own source credential with token exchange as the "OAuth" of this lane and its `needs-session` cost, and the human decision through `POST /api/threads/:id/act` with the ledger before dispatch), then the MCP lane's state (provider OAuth required by D81, not built; writes confirmed through `POST /api/mcp/:id/decisions`). A case system is a native connector under D35, not an MCP tool. Linked from the guide index, `docs/README.md` and `llms.txt`; `docs:generate` and the documentation check passed. No code changed; nothing committed.

One front end and the connector-owned header, 2026-11-09 (D88, `main`): the product owner showed the proof of concept's header (https://start.sundsvall.dev/portal/start) and asked for it in Clew; a colleague's D87 landed the same morning with the shared header for the portal and the intranet. The product owner chose the web app on 3020 as the place, the header above the rail, that the portal go ("from now on there is only one frontend"), first that an administrator manage the links and the logo in Clew, and then, the same afternoon, that the links come from an external system through a connector (SharePoint, a Sitevision connector) with no header administration in Clew. Done: `apps/portal` removed (port 3024, `dev:portal`/`build:portal`, the `FRONTEND_ORIGINS` default in `auth-setup.ts` and `.env.example`, the docs; the proxy container and the portal's dev server stopped; D67 superseded); the intranet stand-in and the WCAG spec's second server moved to the web app's dev server. `SiteHeader` redrawn as the reference: a band (nested theme `createHeaderBandTheme`, the accent with on-accent text, `color-mix` washes for the selected place and secondary buttons) with the heading row and the places, the current place's pages on a row under the band, rows that wrap, breakpoint `md`, the logo's dark variant on the band at 28 px, no band on the phone bar (the drawer renders inside it); `SiteHeaderPerson` took the unit, D39's blocked-storage message and an icon-only avatar button when compact. The web shell draws it in `topNav` (`variant="section"`), the rail lost `SideNavHeading` and the account row, and on a phone the places are rows in the drawer. The protocol gained `header: { capability }` on the manifest, with rules (read, no input, must exist), a check probe (counts, never words) and `Registry.header(actor)` (first registered declaring connector the person may call, schema-checked, `onRefused` and the next on failure); `GET /api/header` (`apps/server/src/connectors/header.ts`) answers the signed-in person's header or Clew's default. Later the same day the holder became explicit (a handover note found registration order a silent rule, the kind D23 removed): `Governance.header` and the row's `header_holder` (migration 0038), the exclusive switch "Ger sidhuvudet" on the connector's Områden tab with a status line ("Ja", "Kan ge sidhuvudet", or that it cannot), the use case taking the header from the previous holder in the same transaction with `connector.header` audit rows on both, `Registry.header` asking the holder alone with no fallthrough, and the check wording "Kan ge sidhuvudet". The product owner then asked that the header never block a page on an OAuth requirement and, signed in on 3020, why the start page and an intranet link showed different headers (the web build is Neutral and the stand-in's embed defaulted to Sundsvall; the stand-in's static fixture menu had five places and the connector six): `readSiteHeader` got a three-second budget and a per-person stale-while-revalidate cache (`HEADER_TIMEOUT_MS`, `HEADER_FRESH_MS`; a failed exchange or a down connector is the default, nothing interactive), the stand-in takes the web build's profile (`webConfig({ profile })`), and the intranet fixture answers the stand-in tree's four sections. The browser suite's mock had lost its `/admin/models` dispatch during the pivot (the Modeller page showed an error in two runs); restored. A Sitevision exploration ran as a subagent and wrote `docs/reviews/2026-11-09-sitevision-connector.md` (the pane was not signed in to the test site; anonymous reads verified the Model REST API's shape and that array parameters on `headless` fail on that instance). The product owner then parked the Sitevision question, asked that the header stay, first with Sundsvall's blue and logo as installation configuration, then that the colour be skipped (the Neutral black band stays) and only the content be mocked: the intranet fixture now reads `connectors/intranet/menu.json` (or `INTRANET_MENU_FILE`; site, logo, places with pages, optional `roles` per place or page), ships the stand-in tree's four sections with the white dragon as logo, and is restarted in the dev stack; the WCAG suite's stand-in server is given `VITE_BRAND_PROFILE=sundsvall` since its spec looks for the dragon. Content, not colour, is the connector's: a band colour stays the installation's branding profile (D38, `VITE_BRAND_PROFILE`). `connectors/intranet` (4049) is the fixture, registered by `dev:connectors`, started in the dev stack. The stored-header module, its table, migration 0038 and the Sidhuvud admin page were built and then removed the same afternoon when the direction changed (the table was dropped from the dev database, the migration record too). Tests: `tests/unit/intranet-connector.test.ts` (manifest rules, the registry's choice and refusals, the check), the manifest reference covers the new field, `tests/a11y/header.spec.ts`, and `shell`, `branding`, `appearance`, `structure` specs rewritten for the header's placement; the suite's mock now answers `/chat-services` empty, which every page asks for and no spec had mocked. Review: `docs/reviews/2026-11-09-header-from-a-connector.md`. Not done: a signed-in look on 3020 (the owner signs in), a screen-reader pass, a Sitevision or SharePoint connector, a cache of a connector's header, an overflow menu.

Names in the identity tables, 2026-11-09 (same branch, before the pull request merges): the product owner asked to review the naming. `auth_group.display_name` is `label`, as every other name Clew shows (`auth_unit.label`, `unit_label`, menu and option labels); SCIM's `displayName` stays the attribute name at the `/scim/v2` boundary and is mapped in `groupFields`/`groupResource`. `auth_capability_map` is `auth_group_capability`, a row that reads as what it is, with matching names through the code: `groupCapabilities` in the schema and the memory state, `GroupCapabilityRule` in the domain, `GroupCapability`/`GroupCapabilityInput` in the contracts, `groupCapabilities()`/`setGroupCapability` on the port, `addGroupCapability`/`removeGroupCapability` as use cases and web calls, `POST /api/admin/users/group-capabilities` and `…/remove` as routes, `group-capability.added`/`removed` as audit actions, `groupLabel` for the group's name on a row. `auth_provisioner.digest` is `token_digest`. `auth_access_grant` keeps its name: "grant" is Clew's word across D23, the scheduler and the fingerprint, and the table's meaning as the active assignment is documented rather than renamed. Migration `0039_identity_provisioning` regenerated with the new names (the development database reset and re-provisioned again); the table sketch regenerated.
Errands from SupportManagement, 2026-11-09 (D89): the product owner asked how errands from several case systems should be read into Startsidan, then set the constraint: a general errand model, Clew does not keep track of what a person may see, and the surrounding systems may be changed. Read from the case officers' web application (`web-app-draken-public` at `2cb2239e`): SAML login, one client-credentials application for the gateway, the person asserted in `X-Sent-By`, namespace access decided in that application from AD groups (`AUTHORIZED_GROUPS` per installation). D89 therefore replaces D18's asserted identity: an errand connector declares `delegation`, forwards the person's own token and nothing else, and the case system decides access; D35's row gains `state` and `area`. Built `connectors/supportmanagement` (id `sm`, port 4050 since the rebase onto `main`'s D88, whose intranet connector has 4049): `sm.errands` (list, `view: assigned | reported`) and `sm.errand` (detail, `model: false`), an area with the list as a standing action, `scan: false`; `errands.mjs` (port, `toSourceErrand`, a made-up SupportManagement that applies its own access rule from the token's `preferred_username`), `supportmanagement.mjs` (the API adapter, against `GET /{municipalityId}/errands/mine?relation=…`, which does not exist yet, and the existing errand and metadata reads). The connector's README states what SupportManagement, its gateway and the identity provider have to provide. `scripts/auth-setup.ts` creates the audience `supportmanagement`. No new dependency (Node only), so nothing for section 3.

Verification: `bun test tests/unit/supportmanagement-connector.test.ts` (20 cases: the fixture behind the registry with a delegated token, access decided by the source, no free text in a row, denied without or with a refused token, outage, operator configuration, the mapping, the API adapter against recorded answers). Not done: the connector has not been registered in the development stack or opened in the browser (`auth-setup.ts` was not re-run, so Keycloak has no `supportmanagement` audience yet), no screen-reader pass, and nothing has met a real SupportManagement. Next: a scan that carries the delegated token when the person opens Startsidan (cards on Hem), the profile and `clew.errands` when a second case system arrives, then writes (free text in an approved decision; a delegated token at dispatch).

One list for every case system, 2026-11-09 (D89 item 8): the product owner asked that a second errand connector land in the same list, built now. The list profile is in the protocol (`<id>.errands` reserved, `ErrandRow`/`ErrandsOutput` in `@clew/contracts` and the published `$defs`), `Registry.errandSources` lists a person's case systems, and the new module `apps/server/src/modules/errands/` is `clew.errands`: every system asked at once under a budget, rows ordered by the shared state across systems, each carrying the view that opens it in its own system, the systems that did not answer named, a failure when none answers. `connectors/registry.ts` joins it with the composite search into the core connector. It is in scope only for a person with a case system, owns the area "Ärenden" and the menu hint, and is the one errand tool the model sees; the `sm` connector lost its area and menu row. The browser: the registry sets `rowViews` on a view of Clew's own connectors, and `DeclaredView` then lets a row open the `view` it carries and names `unanswered` under the rows (never for a connector outside Clew). The a11y mock now answers `GET /chat-services` with no services: every spec on the default mock failed on that unmocked request since the MCP catalogue landed, which nothing showed because the suite runs by hand (D59).

Verification: `bun run check`; `tests/unit/errands.test.ts` (9 cases) and the connector's 20; `sh scripts/a11y.sh views.spec.ts` (18 cases, two new: the combined list with links into each system, the missing system named and keyboard opening, and 320 px). Model test without a token limit, 2026-11-09: after the migrations the product owner pressed Testa on `gpt-6-luna` and got `HTTP 400: Unsupported parameter: 'max_tokens' is not supported with this model. Use 'max_completion_tokens' instead.` The probe in `apps/server/src/assistant/adapters/openai-compatible.ts` sent `max_tokens: 4`; a real turn through the AI SDK sends no limit, so the probe could never settle D84's reasoning setting on such a model and every turn fell back to the router. The probe now sends no token limit (either parameter name fails on some OpenAI-compatible server), with a case in `tests/unit/assistant.test.ts`.
