Logging, tracing and audit investigation, 2026-11-09 (main tree, nothing committed): the product owner asked what to use for logging and tracking of model requests, error logging and audit, OpenTelemetry-compatible. No code changed. Findings and the recommended shape are in `docs/reviews/2026-11-09-logging-tracing-and-audit-options.md`: keep the in-transaction `audit` table as the system of record and add the model turn's usage, duration and `trace_id` to it; one `pino` logger behind the existing `log` port; `@opentelemetry/sdk-node` with `pg`, `undici` and `@hono/otel` instrumentation exporting OTLP; DBOS on the global tracer provider; `@ai-sdk/otel` for `gen_ai.*` spans with input and output recording off by default. Licences checked with `npm view` (all Apache-2.0 or MIT) and the backends' LICENSE files (Grafana LGTM AGPL-3.0 as separate processes, SigNoz and Langfuse MIT core with `ee/`, Jaeger and the Collector Apache-2.0; Arize Phoenix ELv2 and Sentry FSL excluded). The GenAI semantic conventions are still at Development status. The owner chose to decide and build the same day: D91. Built: `apps/server/src/logging.ts` (`pino`, `logPort`, `dbosLogger`), `apps/server/src/telemetry.ts` (the SDK, exporters, undici, `@ai-sdk/otel`, `currentTraceId`), the first import of `main.ts`, `@hono/otel` on the app, process-level handlers, DBOS on the global tracer with `semconv` names and its logging through Clew's logger (`DBOS_LOG_LEVEL` gone), every `console.*` in the server replaced, `createGenerator` passing `telemetry` with content recording off unless `CLEW_MODEL_TELEMETRY_CONTENT=true`, `MessageMeta.modelCall` and `fallbackReason` in the contracts and in the turn's message and audit row, a `trace` port on the assistant's deps, `tests/unit/logging.test.ts`, the model test in `tests/unit/conversation.test.ts` extended, the `otel` service (`grafana/otel-lgtm`, Grafana on 3023) in the development compose with the app exporting to it, `docs/architecture/observability.md`, the status row and the runbook sentence. Not done: an administration page over the audit rows; spans for `postgres.js` queries; a screen of the traces compared with anything (there is no canvas for this); the app container in the running stack still has the old environment until it is recreated, so the dev server exports nothing until then.

Answer drawn above its question, 2026-11-09: the product owner added a card to the box from Startsidan, asked "Vad är detta?" and saw the assistant's answer above the question in the transcript. A conversation turn writes the question and the answer with one `at` (`assistant/index.ts`, as every use case that writes a pair does), and the Postgres readers ordered messages by `created_at` alone, so equal instants came back in whatever order Postgres chose; the memory adapter keeps insertion order, which is why the unit suite never saw it. `thread_message` now has a `seq` column (bigserial, migration `0039_thread_message_seq`, which also renumbers existing rows so an existing pair reads question first and extends the `thread_message_thread` index), and every reader (`threads/adapters/postgres.ts`, `packages/db/src/threads.ts`) orders by `created_at, seq`. The store contract gained the same-instant case, run on both adapters. Verified: typecheck, `tests/db/postgres-store.test.ts` and `tests/unit/memory-store.test.ts` in the devcontainer, the migration applied to the development database and the owner's conversation read in order. Not done: the other dev stacks apply the migration with `bun run db:init`.

Adding a card to the box enters instead of popping, 2026-11-09 (D92, main tree, nothing committed): the product owner added a card to the box from Startsidan and found the change too fast to follow, and asked whether motion could mark the transition without slowing anything down. Four things changed in one frame (the button's check, the drawer, the chip, the verb row) with the field taking the focus from the same click. Built on Astryx's `useEntryAnimation` alone, the hook D70 and D76 already use: `Drawer`, `Chip` and `Verb` wrappers in `apps/web/src/components/composer.tsx` (slideDown, scaleIn, fadeIn through `xstyle`) and `AddedCheck` in `add-to-conversation.tsx` (the `@` icon button's check scales in; it mounts only when the object is added, so a resting button at page load shows its check at once). A visually hidden `role="status"` in the box's footer says "<chip> är med i samtalet" once per added chip, since a screen reader heard nothing but the input's label before. `useAnimatedLayout` does not reach the composer (D63) and a flying chip with `useAnimator` was rejected; both are in D92. The desktop card's text button ("I samtalet") was left as it is. Verified: `bun run format`, `bun run typecheck`, `sh scripts/a11y.sh card-actions.spec.ts` (two new cases, `no-preference` and `reduce`, on the chip's computed animation, the focus and the status). The product owner then found the swap from Hem to the conversation as abrupt: `Entering` in `apps/web/src/pages/start.tsx` fades the whole column in when it changes hands (keyed by whose turn it is, so the first paint does not animate), the conversation over Hem on a question and Hem back on close, with two cases in `tests/a11y/answer.spec.ts`. Not done: a screen-reader pass, a look at the motion against the canvas (the canvas draws none), the full a11y suite.
Review of the SCIM server and the provisioning boundary, 2026-11-09 (`scim-provisioning` at `2c247dd`): the product owner asked whether SCIM and the user handling are correct, built to the standard, and whether the line to the external system is right. Written as [docs/reviews/2026-11-09-scim-server-and-provisioning-boundary.md](docs/reviews/2026-11-09-scim-server-and-provisioning-boundary.md); no code changed. A throwaway script run in the app container against the development Postgres (prefixed rows, removed afterwards, not kept) found two faults the memory adapter hides: a `unit-manager:` membership without a `unit:` row violates `auth_assignment`'s foreign key to `auth_unit` and answers 500, which the synthetic provisioner's push order (`-` sorts before `:`) would hit on a fresh database; and the same `userName` in a second organisation hits the installation-wide unique index while the use case and its test say per organisation. Also found: `userName` declared `caseExact: false` but compared exactly (filter, uniqueness, first-login match); the sub-app's `notFound` is not carried through `app.route`, so `/scim/v2/Me` answers the parent's Swedish 404 and `/Bulk` 404 instead of 501; a pushed `protected` group is stored, rides in the context's `groups` and appears in both administration editors although the status says it is not stored; groups have no organisation or owner on the server, so any provisioner may delete another's. Recommended order is in the review: the foreign key first with a Postgres contract case, then uniqueness and case together in one migration, then a decision on `protected`. Verification: the four SCIM, provisioner and provisioning unit suites pass on the memory adapter (34 cases). Not done: no fix applied, and `bun run test:db` was not run beyond the probe.

A fifty-person development directory, and finding 1 of the SCIM review fixed, 2026-11-09 (same branch): the product owner wanted richer mock data (organisation, managers, the people under them, about fifty) and asked what the `unit:` key is. `scripts/provisioner/directory.ts` now generates fifty people in eleven units deterministically from name lists: the three personas keep their ids and Keycloak accounts (`login: true`; `auth-setup.ts` creates accounts and person rows for those alone), every other unit's first person is its manager, one person manages two units (ekonomi and HR), one works in two (bibliotek and kommunikation), three plain `ad:` groups (Alla chefer, Intranätsredaktörer, Skyddsombud) exercise D85's mapping and D23's audiences, and the IT manager holds `admin.connectors`. `plan()` maps `alsoIn`, `alsoManages` and `groups`, dedupes members and owns the `ad:` prefix. The push would have failed on every new unit (review finding 1: `unit-manager:` sorts before `unit:`, and the assignment's foreign key needs the unit row), so `deriveAuthority` now yields one unit per assignment, labelled by the `unit:` group, else as the unit table knows it, else by its id, and a new or renamed `unit:` group re-derives the unit's managers too (`managersOfUnit` in `usecases.ts`). Verification: `bun run typecheck`, `deps`, `purity`; the four SCIM/provisioner/provisioning unit suites (36 cases, two new: the manager-before-unit scenario and the contract case that Postgres asserts); `bun test tests/db/postgres-provisioning.test.ts` (11 cases); `bun run dev:provision` against the running server twice: 47 users created and 3 replaced, 21 groups created and 6 replaced, then 0 created and 27 replaced; the database holds 11 units, 27 groups, 52 assignments and 50 grants (10 managers), and petra.nystrom acts as ekonomi's manager with two assignments. Seen in the browser afterwards: Administration › Användare lists the fifty people with unit, role, capabilities and sign-in state, the two-unit persons as `(+1)`, and the group editor offers the `ad:` groups; the SCIM walkthrough (401 without a token, discovery, a page of users, filters on users and groups, the error resource) and a second idempotent push were run in the terminal panel. Not done: Hem was not opened with the larger directory, `bun run auth:setup` was not re-run (the personas' rows already existed), and the review's findings 2 to 9 are open.

Findings 2 and 3, and a second merge with `main`, 2026-11-09 (same branch): the product owner asked whether the branch was ready to merge; it was not, since today's work was uncommitted and `main` had gained D91 with its own migration `0039_thread_message_seq`, colliding with `0039_identity_provisioning`. Committed the directory and finding 1, then findings 2 and 3 as their own commit: `userName` and `externalId` unique across the installation in the use case as in the indexes (a login carries no organisation), `userName` matched without regard to case in the filter, the uniqueness check and `bindFirstLogin`, the unique index on `lower(user_name)`, the route translating a unique violation (`23505`, with the constraint name) into 409 `uniqueness`, and the memory adapter enforcing the same indexes; D68 item 1 records the clarification. Then merged `main`: took its journal and `0039_snapshot.json`, removed this branch's 0039 file and regenerated the identity migration with `drizzle-kit generate --name identity_provisioning` as `0040_identity_provisioning` (snapshot chained to main's 0039), then put the hand-written SQL back in place of the generated one (the DDL was identical; the trigger guard and the two backfills are what the generator cannot know). The development database already had the identity migration under 0039, so it was aligned by hand in one transaction: the migration row retimed to 0040's `when` and hash, main's 0039 statements run and recorded, after which `bun run db:init` had nothing to apply; `bun install --frozen-lockfile` was needed for main's new `pino` and OpenTelemetry packages, and the dev server restarted on a touch. HANDOVER's conflict was both sides appending notes; main's come first. Verification after the merge: `bun run check`; `bun test tests/db/postgres-provisioning.test.ts tests/db/identity-binding.test.ts` (13 cases, two new: the case-insensitive binding and the duplicate insert that the indexes refuse); `bun run dev:provision` against the running server.

SCIM review findings 4 to 9, 2026-11-09 (branch `scim-review-findings-4-9` off `scim-provisioning`, one commit per finding): the product owner asked for the follow-up to be done in the same session. Finding 4: `routes/scim.ts` answers unknown addresses with the SCIM error resource through `app.all('*')`, since Hono does not carry a sub-app's `notFound` through `app.route()` and the running server had answered the parent's Swedish 404; `/Bulk`, `/Me` and the root `/.search` answer 501, `/Users/.search` and `/Groups/.search` take a SearchRequest body; `apps/server/tests/runtime.test.ts` mounts the sub-app as `main.ts` does (`hono` is not resolvable from `tests/unit`). Finding 5 is D93: the `protected` key is refused with 400 `invalidValue` on POST, PUT and PATCH (`assertAcceptedKey` in `domain.ts`), because the review found the group stored, in the context's `groups` and in both administration editors while the status said it was not stored; D68's table and open question, the guide, `identity.md` and `status.md` were corrected; `docs/architecture/figures/group-keys.svg` and its PNG still draw the key and need a redraw. Finding 6: `auth_group.organisation_id` (migration `0041_group_organisation`, generated with drizzle-kit and given a backfill from the members' organisation, else the active provisioner's, before `SET NOT NULL`), set from the provisioner on create and checked by `readGroup`/`listGroups`; `findGroup` stays installation-wide for the uniqueness check; `groupRow` in the SCIM store contract takes the organisation and the contract asserts the scope; the test harnesses that seed groups (`provisioning.test.ts`, `postgres-provisioning.test.ts`, `users-admin.test.ts`, `identity-model.test.ts`) give them one. Finding 7: `writeUser` records `scim.user.deactivated`/`reactivated` when `enabled` flips, with the verb in `details.via`. Finding 8: the body limit is four megabytes, checked on `Content-Length` before the body is read; `deriveWithin` loads the labels of every named unit once and writes only units whose label moved and assignments that differ (`assignmentsOf` compared in unit order), asserted by a counting store in `scim.test.ts`. Finding 9: the guide's "Deactivation, not deletion" paragraph. The dev database took 0041 through `bun run db:init`. Verification: `bun run check`, `bun test tests/db/postgres-provisioning.test.ts tests/db/identity-binding.test.ts tests/db/identity-model.test.ts`, `bun run --cwd apps/server test:node`, `bun run dev:provision` against the running server. Not done: the figure redraw, and the review's remaining small points (`attributes`/`excludedAttributes`, `referenceTypes`, `specUri`, the users overview's query count, token rotation in the runbook).

Personal, a manager's section, 2026-11-09 (branch `staff-section` off `main`, D94): the product owner asked, once SCIM and the fifty-person directory were in, for a section where a manager sees the people under them, as a first step towards notes and utvecklingssamtal; the reference is the proof of concept at `start.sundsvall.dev/portal/verktyg/personal` (title, "enhet · n medarbetare", a Medarbetare table with avatar, name and title; its presence, notices, errands and next-talk columns and its four stat tiles need data Clew does not have). Built: `staffUsecases` in `modules/people/usecases.ts` reads the actor's own assignments, refuses with `ForbiddenError` when none is a manager role, and lists everyone assigned to each managed unit except the actor, with their role on that unit (from `auth_assignment`, not the grant, so a manager of two units sees both), sorted in Swedish; a new port method `unitPeople` on both adapters, asserted in the SCIM store contract; `StaffOverview` in `@clew/contracts`; `routes/staff.ts` at `/api/staff`; `pages/staff.tsx` as a destination at `/staff` (Shell, the administration's Layout, `PageHeader`, one Card per unit with Toolbar, Table on a desktop and List on a phone, `Avatar` initials, a "Avstängd" badge, an EmptyState for the refused case); the rail's Personal row under Verktyg for a session whose role is manager (`useRail` takes `manager`); `tests/unit/staff.test.ts`, `tests/a11y/staff.spec.ts` with a `staffOverview()` fixture and a `staffForbidden` flag in the mock. Lesson from the first a11y run: a test that does not destructure the `api` fixture never installs the mock and lands on the login page. Not built: a person's own page, notes, the talks, search and filters, presence from a connector. Verified in the browser as mikael (not a manager): no Personal row, and `/staff` shows the page's own refusal; as hillevi: the Personal row in the rail and Bergsåkers skola with its five employees, role and address under each name, in the phone layout of the narrow pane.


The layer where Clew owns the feature and its data, 2026-11-10 (exploration, main tree, nothing committed, no code): the product owner asked whether the modularity had gone too far, since features such as the ones a commercial HR system for small and medium-sized companies sells, 1-on-1s with employees and the like, are the same in every organisation, and asked for an exploration of the layer where Clew owns the feature and its data and how it relates to what is built. Written up in `docs/reviews/2026-11-10-owned-domains-against-a-commercial-hr-system.md`. Findings: the vision's ladder (pass-through, derived, core) already allows ownership, but no module owns a business record; `ConnectorKind`'s `core` means "bundled" (`people`, `search`) and `describe.ts` reports it as `passthrough`; utvecklingssamtal lives in the `calendar` module with its topic and deadline as constants and ends when the invitation is answered. Proposed, not decided: a line (Clew may own work done between people, never a fact about a person, so no personkort); three of the reference product's fifteen features as candidates (medarbetarsamtal and 1:1 first, onboarding checklists, the annual plan), reduced to five building blocks (round, record, template, commitment, checklist) of which a commitment is a thread and a round is the D30 obligation made data; an owned domain as a D20 module with its own tables, registered in-process, with its own Astryx pages for writing (shape C), and no swap profile until a second implementation exists; five rules to restate in the decision (principle 7, the ledger for local writes, the assistant, classification-gated inference, what `core` means). Open before any build: the six questions at the end of the review, the manager relation beyond the fixture (D68, multiple assignments), and retention, export, erasure, public-document and archive rules for a municipality, which the review names and does not answer. The reference product's site was read on six pages only; what municipalities lack is an assumption to confirm with Sundsvall. `bun run docs:generate` was run (index and `context.json` updated). No decision file was written: nothing is settled. Discussion the same day: the product owner corrected the premise ("some of the connector systems should be part of clew. Part of clew project is to get rid of some third party dependencies"), so ownership is by choice, not only where nobody else owns, which is wider than `docs/vision.md` says today (the vision is unchanged, to amend with the decision). The review has an Outcome section replacing its line and its advice against a swap seam: the third-party system behind a connector and Clew's own module answer the same capabilities and an installation chooses, the connector layer is the migration path, and an owned domain feeds former dependents through D55 facts. Agreed starting matrix: absorb medarbetarsamtal and 1:1, onboarding and offboarding (the owner stressed offboarding), the annual plan, deviation reporting; unsure absence request and approval, internal errands, internal news, the phone book; keep as connectors payroll, finance, Qlik, calendar, mail, identity. The owner asked for a checklist for maintainers: drafted as `docs/maintainers/core-or-connector.md` (three outcomes, Part 1 not Clew at all, Part 2 six hard stops, Part 3 twelve questions that must all be yes, Part 4 ordering, Part 5 building blocks and the obligations of a core domain, the matrix with the question that decides each row), linked from `docs/README.md` and `docs/maintainers/README.md`, marked draft until a numbered decision backs it. The owner then asked for the decision: recorded as D95 (`docs/decisions/D95-clew-may-own-a-domain-by-choice-decided-with-the-core-or-connector-checklist.md`, accepted, nothing built): ownership by choice, the checklist as the instrument (it changes only with a decision), one numbered decision per owned domain recording its Part 3 answers, the module-with-own-pages shape with the capability seam as a requirement, D55 facts for former dependents, principle 7, D45 and the assistant restated for an owned domain, and `core` in `ConnectorKind` left meaning "Clew's own, in process" (an owned domain registers as `core`; splitting the kind is for the first owned domain's decision; no code changed). With it: `docs/vision.md` rewritten in four places (the paragraph is now "Own the domain where that removes a system", the sentence on systems of record, a new "not a payroll system, a ledger or an employee file" line, the open questions), D3's status refined, the checklist's draft marking removed, a pointer in `docs/architecture/integrations.md`, the review's Outcome linked to D95. `bun run docs:generate` and `docs:check` pass. Nothing committed. The decision was first written as D93 and renumbered to D95 when origin brought its own D93 and D94 (SCIM and the Personal section) the same day. That work also answers half of the prerequisite named above: who manages whom now comes from provisioned assignments (D68) and the Personal section (D94) is where the talks will sit; still missing are a period or history on an assignment, an acting manager, and the planner reading its team from the assignments instead of the calendar's persona fixture (D95 and the review's Outcome item 8 say so). `CLAUDE.md` does not mention the checklist yet. Next: the decision for the first owned domain (medarbetarsamtal and 1:1), which needs the owner's answers on who a talk record belongs to and what HR sees, what Sundsvall uses today, and the legal answers of question 6.

Calendar dialogs cut off in a low window, 2026-11-10 (fixed on `main`): the product owner showed the entry dialog on the calendar page with its actions outside the dialog and nothing to scroll. Cause: `EventDialog` and `BookingDialog` put a `VStack` straight in an Astryx `Dialog`, which caps its height at 75dvh and clips what does not fit; the day strip alone is taller than that in a window under about 950 px. Both now use `DialogFrame` (Astryx's Layout slots: header pinned, content scrolling, actions pinned in `LayoutFooter`), moved from `apps/web/src/pages/admin/` to `apps/web/src/components/dialog-frame.tsx` since it is no longer the administration's alone. The entry dialog passes `scrollLabel`, so the day and the details are a labelled `ScrollableArea` a keyboard can scroll (2.1.1); choosing Flytta puts focus in the new start time and Ställ in brings its question into view, since both sit under the day. The plain error text of both dialogs now stands in the footer beside the actions; the booking's "uncertain" banner stays in the content. Two cases in `tests/a11y/calendar.spec.ts` hold it (600 px and 500 px high windows). Not fixed, and failing before this change too: `calendar.spec.ts` "a click outside office hours is heard too" (the Ny bokning dialog never opens from a click at 17:30). No new dependency.

SCIM conformance and operability review, 2026-11-10 (branch `staff-section`, no code changed): the product owner asked whether the SCIM handling is standard and easy for operations and the Metakatalogen team. Written as `docs/reviews/2026-11-10-scim-conformance-and-operability.md` after a forty-request probe of the routes on the memory store in Entra, Okta and hand-written shapes (probe not kept, Postgres untouched). Verdict: standard and small for a provisioner written for Clew; not plug-and-play for a stock SCIM client; operations lack visibility. Confirmed by the probe: `PATCH` `remove` is refused for attributes Clew does not hold (Entra sends it), a path-less `PATCH` repeating `id` is refused (Okta), a path-less `name` without `formatted` is refused, and malformed keys (`unit:` with no id, `capability:` outside the closed list) are stored as plain groups in silence. Reasoned from the code, not reproduced: `deriveWithin` takes no lock on the person, so two concurrent membership changes for one person can leave a stale grant. Operability gaps: no last-seen time for a provisioner (a stopped one is invisible), no token `list` or rotation with overlap, no administration page or check tool for provisioners, an unmeasured large-group push, and `console.error` at `routes/scim.ts:42` against D91. The findings were built the same day; see the next note.

SCIM conformance and operability, the fixes, 2026-11-10 (branch `scim-conformance-and-operability`, rebased on `main` and opened as pull request 17; the decision was renumbered from D95 to D96 because `main` took D95): the product owner asked for the review's findings to be fixed carefully. `modules/people/domain.ts`: `applyUserPatch` and `applyGroupPatch` take the resource's id, refuse `remove` only for what Clew requires (`mutability`), ignore it for what Clew does not hold, and ignore `id`/`schemas`/`meta` repeated in a path-less value; `assertAcceptedKey` is D96 (a key written wrong is refused, `protected` in any case); `pageOf` defaults a broken count; `project`/`leavesOut` implement `attributes` and `excludedAttributes`. `usecases.ts`: `deriveWithin` locks the person, `deriveAll` derives in id order, group writes lock the group, `nameUnit` writes a `unit:` group's label before anyone is derived, `authenticate` records last-seen (at most once a minute) and audits `provisioner.seen` when a run begins after thirty minutes of silence, and the users overview carries `provisioners` with `silent`. Ports: `lockPerson` (was `person`), `readGroup(…, lock)`, `provisioner(digest, at)`, `touchProvisioner`, `provisioners`. Migration `0042_provisioner_seen_and_rotation` (three nullable columns on `auth_provisioner`; applied to the development database with `bun run db:init`). `routes/scim.ts` takes a `log` option, `main.ts` binds `logPort('scim', 'error')` and answers the starting 503 as the error resource. `scripts/provisioner-token.ts` has `list` and `rotate [--grace-hours]`; `scripts/provisioner-check.ts` with `scripts/provisioner/check.ts` reads what an installation holds. Administration › Användare has an "Inläsning från katalogen" card (Card, Toolbar, List, Badge). Lessons: `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on a person deadlocks against the key-share lock a membership insert in another transaction holds through its foreign key, so the lock is `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`; `tests/db/postgres-scim-concurrency.test.ts` found that, and with the locks removed it also shows two patches of one group losing a member, which the review had not seen. Sorting the derivation order changed which person wrote a unit's label first and broke the manager's label until `nameUnit` made the label independent of order. `bun run -` reads a script from stdin as TSX, so a generic arrow function does not parse there. No dependency was added. Not built: an administration page for tokens, a metric or alarm for a silent provisioner, a run against a real source; the page was not looked at in a browser or read with a screen reader.

SCIM, the review of pull request 17, 2026-11-10 (same branch): the product owner asked for a review and for what it found to be fixed, with readability and upkeep in mind. Eight findings, all fixed; the review document's "Second pass" lists them. The one that matters: a write of a `unit:` or `unit-manager:` group now takes `lockUnits` (a transaction-scoped advisory lock per unit id, `ProvisionTx`) before it reads the unit's managers or locks a person, because the two groups of one unit deadlocked when written at once with a shared person; lock order is group row, units in id order, people in id order. `touchProvisioner(id, at, seenBefore)` is a compare-and-set and returns whether it moved the mark, so one of several simultaneous requests writes `provisioner.seen`. `scimRoutes(scim, log)` requires its log port. In `domain.ts`, `unitOfKey`, `isReadOnly`, `corePath`/`coreKey` and `otherAddress` each replace a rule that was written in several places. Lesson: "locks are taken in one order" was claimed in the first pass without a test for the unit row, which is written by two different resources; the test that posts both groups of a new unit at once found the deadlock on its first round. While this was done another session had unfinished calendar work in the same working tree (type errors and two failing calendar tests of its own), so `bun run check` was run in parts and only the SCIM files were committed.

A two-hundred-person development directory, 2026-11-10 (same branch, pull request 17): the product owner asked for more test data through the SCIM flow, about two hundred users in different groups. `scripts/provisioner/directory.ts` now generates 200 people in 25 units under five administrations. The rules are tables, not index arithmetic: `UNITS` carries each unit's area, size, number of managers and `managedBy`; `MEMBERSHIPS` is one predicate per plain group over a generated person's seat; `SECOND_UNIT` says who works in two units. Fifteen plain groups from nobody (`ad:projekt-e-arkiv`) to 69 members (each administration is a group), one unit with two managers (the gymnasium), two managers of two units, three people in two units, and seven hand-written people for the names a real directory holds, one of them a second Anna Lindqvist. The personas keep their ids, units and roles, so the fixtures are untouched; the generated people's names and units changed, which nothing keys on. The push creates 200 users and 67 groups; two hundred is also the page limit, so the check script reads exactly one full page. The provisioner's unit tests take about two seconds now instead of a quarter. `bun run dev:provision` against the running development server then created 153 users and 40 groups, replaced 47 and 27, and deactivated the three generated people of the old directory whose names the new one does not have; a second run changed nothing, and `provisioner-check.ts` passed (203 users, 200 active).

Talks, the first owned domain, 2026-11-10 (main tree, D96; the decision only, no code yet): the product owner asked which part of the reference HR system to support first and then for a plan run by coding sub-agents. The reference product's own pages for medarbetarsamtal, on- och offboarding and årsplanering were read (the review of the same day had only their taglines for the last two): onboarding runs on employment dates Clew does not hold, mails a private address before the first day and has form steps; the annual plan starts talk rounds. Chosen with the owner: utvecklingssamtal first, in the order round, action points, record, and this plan stops before the record. D96 records it: a `talks` module owning the round (operator script opens it; membership from the assignments at read time; held stored, booked read from the calendar) and action points (`commitment` in code, åtgärdspunkter in the product; manager adds, owner ticks off; a card without text in the owner's stream and a `due` timer; the text sealed), the planner reading topic, deadline and team from the round and the assignments, the reserved profile `<id>.talks` and `<id>.commitments` with the composites `clew.talks` and `clew.commitments`, one answerer per installation (the bundled `talks` connector while enabled, else exactly one external), the fixture `connectors/appraisals`, erasure and export from the first slice, `core` not split and no fact yet. Part 3 of the checklist is recorded as it stands, with questions 6 (retention, secrecy, archive) and 11 (an HR practitioner) open, so the domain runs on synthetic data only. UC-KIM-8 in `docs/personas.md` no longer says that no conversation notes are stored. Competences stay outside the domain: read beside the talk from the system that holds them. The implementation plan (five phases, ten agents in worktrees `talks-<wp>` under `.claude/worktrees/`, each phase merged to local main when its checks pass) is in the session's plan file, not in the repository. Not built: everything. Next: phase 1, contracts, schema with migration 0042 and the module's ports.

Talks built, 2026-11-10 (local `main`, D96; coding by sub-agents in worktrees `.claude/worktrees/talks-<wp>`, each merged fast-forward after `bun run check`, `test:db` where a store or migration changed, and its own WCAG specs; nothing pushed, and `origin/main` gained one commit meanwhile, a calendar dialog change that also appends to this file). Built, in merge order: the profile shapes, `Registry.talkSource` and migration `0042_talks` with the two-person change signal (F1); the composites `clew.talks` and `clew.commitments` in `modules/talk-seam` (S1); the fixture `connectors/appraisals` on 4051, registered as a draft (X1); the owner's card and the `due` timer kind in `composition/talk-work-items.ts` (T1); the planner reading `Team` and `RoundReader` ports, with the school's five people made the same in the directory and the calendar fixture (C1); the answering system chosen from either read (R1, found by the fixture: with a manager-only `<id>.talks` an employee had no source); `modules/talks` with both stores under one contract, the bundled connector and its figure (M1); the wiring in `composition/talks.ts`, the registry, `routes/talks.ts`, the `due` dispatch and the operator scripts `talk-round.ts`, `talks-export.ts`, `talks-erase.ts` (W1); the web's reads, writes, `useTalkAction` and the mock (V0); Personal's columns and the talk as a drawer at `/staff?talk=<personId>` (V1); Mina åtgärdspunkter on Hem with Klar and Ångra (V2). On the owner's instruction mid-way the name of the reference HR product was removed from the tree (review renamed to `docs/reviews/2026-11-10-owned-domains-against-a-commercial-hr-system.md`; history keeps it) and no brief names a vendor since. Corrections made at the merge gate: the module's views declared a row ref, which lets a row be added to a conversation, removed; a read without a person returned only what the reader owns, now also what is about the reader; the mock refused an employee's own tick-off, which the server allows. The development stack: `auth:setup` was run for the first time since D68 (the database held three people; it now holds the fifty-person directory, organisation `poc`), the calendar seed and the fake Graph were re-run for Karin's account, the round "Utvecklingssamtal 2026" is open, and `appraisals` is a draft row. Lessons: `dev:provision` needs the token `auth:setup` writes, so on a stack that never ran it the push fails with "No provisioner token"; a worktree's Postgres tests need their own database (`DATABASE_URL` with another name, `db:init` there), never the shared one, when a migration is in flight; a fast-forward over another session's uncommitted file works by saving their copy, `git merge-file -p` against the base and the branch, `git checkout --` the path, merging, and copying the merged file back, then comparing their hunks before and after. Not done: the switch-over looked at in the browser (the unit test `talks-registry.test.ts` covers it), a signed-in pass, a screen-reader pass. Known red WCAG specs that predate this work: `optimistic.spec.ts` test 1, `menu.spec.ts` (4) and `tools.spec.ts` (2), whose rail lists lack the Personal row of D94, and one case in `calendar.spec.ts` not checked against the base. The panel's fold button floats, 2026-11-10 (main tree, nothing committed; another session's docs edits were in the tree and left alone): the product owner asked why the fold button "now render[s] in a full vertical box". Cause: since 49e2711 (2026-11-06) the button's `VStack` was a row item in the page's `end` slot, so it stood the height of the page, 44 px wide, between the page's scrollbar and the handle, open and folded alike; nothing had changed in the panel or in Astryx (0.7.7), an always-visible scrollbar only makes the strip read as a lane. A first change put the button in the panel's header while open and kept the strip while folded; the product owner then asked for the button to "float to the left of the sidebar". Built: `usePanel` in `apps/web/src/components/panel.tsx` renders the one button through Astryx's `useLayer` (context mode, `placement: 'start'`, `alignment: 'start'`, `offset: 20`, 4 px down: the page's top right corner, clear of a space-taking scrollbar and of the page's header, after the product owner saw the first placement lie over the scrollbar) anchored to the `ResizeHandle`, shown while the panel is beside the page; `useLayer` is exported from `@clew/ui`; `PANEL_EDGE` is 1 (the handle), so Hem's three-track breakpoint is 1785 px, not 1829 (`start.tsx` comment). D63 item 14 carries the revision. Verified: typecheck, format, deps, purity, docs check; `todo.spec.ts` 13 passed (the fold test asserts the button is outside the panel, 18 to 24 px from its edge, above Hem's heading and inside an open popover); screenshots `hem-E-1440` and `hem-E-collapsed-1440` looked at; `keyboard`, `views` and `structure` green (the calendar's day-heading test failed once and passed alone), `tools.spec.ts` red in two tests at lines 51 and 155, where `nav a[aria-current="page"]` now matches the shared header's current place as well as the rail's row (D88), not touched here. Not done: a look with an always-visible scrollbar (headless Chromium hides it); a signed-in live comparison; a screen-reader pass; a page load with a modal dialog already open would show the layer after the dialog and so above it (no address does that today: the review is its own route).

The page blinked on every return to the window, 2026-11-10 (same session, main tree, nothing committed): the product owner reported that the whole page flickers when Chrome gets the focus. Cause: `SessionBoundary` in `packages/ui/src/session.tsx` set the document's opacity to 0 on every window `focus` and `pageshow` until `/api/auth/session` had answered, so each return from another window hid the page for one round trip (there since the first build; not the fold button, not the change stream of b66fbc8). Now a `focus` still asks at once but hides the page only when the last confirmation is older than `FRESH` (the 15 s poll plus 5 s), which is what a page that kept the focus shows anyway; `pageshow` (the back/forward cache, where a sign-out may have left the page behind) and `pagehide` hide as before. Test: `tests/a11y/loading.spec.ts`, "coming back to the window asks about the session without hiding a page confirmed moments ago". Seen while running it, not touched: `loading.spec.ts` "a background refresh preserves content, focus and the composer draft" times out since b66fbc8, because its stand-in change stream says one revision and a first revision now invalidates nothing; `todo.spec.ts:102` runs an axe scan of about 11 s and fails at the 15 s timeout when the machine is busy. The product owner's two recordings could not be read (macOS denies the session the Desktop and the screenshot folder), so the cause is from the code, not the film. Open: the product owner's "I guess the toggle handle should always be visible" (D72 has no handle while nothing is open), asked back as a question.

The panel's button is always there, 2026-11-10 (same session, main tree, nothing committed): after the floating button the product owner wrote "I guess the toggle handle should always be visible … If I then press the X in sidebar it disappears" and, asked what it should do with nothing open, chose an empty panel (over reopening the last closed thing, and over D72 item 1 as it stood). `usePanel` draws the handle and the button whenever the panel fits beside the page; at rest the panel is folded and carries no landmark role; "Visa panelen" shows the heading "Öppet bredvid" and an `EmptyState` "Inget är öppet bredvid sidan"; Esc or the button folds it; closing the last card folds the panel instead of removing it; an open that expands the panel no longer takes the focus from a field being typed in; `usePanel` returns `occupied`, which `start.tsx` reads for its tracks. D72 carries the revision. Verified: typecheck, format, docs check, `todo.spec.ts` 13 passed with the new at-rest, empty-panel and Esc assertions (axe on the empty panel included); screenshot `hem-A-1440` looked at (the button above the date line, the handle's grip at the window's edge). Not done: a screen-reader pass; the canvas has no artboard for the button at rest or the empty panel.

Local main rebased onto origin, 2026-11-10 (worktree `rebase-main`, on the product owner's word): local `main` was 24 commits ahead of `origin/main` and 2 behind (the calendar dialogs fix and pull request 17, SCIM conformance and operability). Both sides had added migration 0042 and a decision D96. Resolved in the rebase: the remote's `0042_provisioner_seen_and_rotation` stands and the talks migration is `0043_talks` (the same SQL, its snapshot regenerated with `drizzle-kit generate` on top of the remote's, its journal entry keeping the original `when` so a database that had applied it as 0042 applies nothing again); both D96 records stay (talks, and the miswritten key refused at the SCIM boundary), as D37 to D39 and D44 to D46 already do, and CLAUDE.md names D96 among them; `scripts/provisioner/directory.ts` keeps the remote's two-hundred-person generator and names the first five generated people of Bergsåkers skola as the calendar fixture's people (the school has twelve now, so the rest are generated), with `tests/unit/provisioner.test.ts` asserting those five and no longer pinning the next unit's generated names; `docs/reference/status.md` takes the remote's users row and says migration 0043; the session log's notes from both sides are kept; the generated indexes were regenerated at each stop. Earlier notes in this log that say migration 0042 for talks mean 0043. The dev database had applied talks before the remote's 0042 existed, and drizzle applies only what is newer than the last applied, so that migration was applied once by hand with its row in `drizzle.__drizzle_migrations`; `db:init` then had nothing to apply. Verified on the rebased branch: `bun run check` (847 unit tests) and `bun run test:db` (108). Every local commit has a new hash: a branch cut from the old `main` (the `talks-*` worktrees) is moved with `git rebase --onto main <old-base> <branch>`. Not pushed.

Decided by the product owner in the session: lenses under one Visa menu instead of tabs, and the three changes the demo led to (an administrator's columns on "Alla", "Behöver dig" first on the opened person, the person as a page). The canvas is **Clew · en entitet, många källor** (link above); a summary outside the repository is https://claude.ai/artifact/MC5SquxUxgPnJbEFhpsqxi. Not done: nothing is built, the artboards were not rendered and checked, no Astryx component was looked up for the new surfaces, the demo's assistant was not asked anything, and D94, D96 and `docs/architecture/working-across-connectors.md` are unchanged until the pattern is built. The installation's theme and logo from administration, and Astryx 0.7.12, 2026-11-10 (branch `installation-theme-and-logo`, cut from `scim-conformance-and-operability`): the product owner asked for a logo for the municipality in the header and a theme chosen from administration at first start rather than from the environment, then for a way to upload "en färgpalett eller liknande som är standard, ett theme som kan användas för att rendera allt", and then to build it, update Astryx and look at what is new. D98 and `docs/reviews/2026-11-10-astryx-0.7.12-upgrade.md`. **Built:** the theme file (`packages/contracts/src/theme-file.ts`: Design Tokens Format Module 2025.10, `light.color` and `dark.color` over Astryx's semantic colour tokens, references to the file's own palette, a closed token list without the error and warning colours, WCAG contrast pairs checked against the base theme, a serialiser, `themeFromAccent` for two colours, and the schema generated into `docs/protocol/theme-file.schema.json` by `scripts/docs.ts`); the core module `apps/server/src/modules/appearance/` with a memory and a Postgres adapter, migration `0044_installation_appearance`, the capability `admin.appearance` in the closed list, the public `GET /api/appearance`, `/api/appearance/theme-file` and `/api/appearance/logo/:variant`, and the administration routes under `/api/admin/appearance`; `packages/ui/src/{branding,theme}.ts` rebuilt around a saved appearance (one Astryx theme per set of colours, the header's band from the accent); `apps/web/src/branding.ts` reading the appearance before the first render with a copy in `localStorage`; the page `apps/web/src/pages/admin/appearance.tsx` (Astryx `FileInput`, a preview in both appearances as nested `Theme`s, the problems as a list, Två färger, the logo with its name) and its row under Administration; `<site-header appearance-src>` in place of `profile`; `themes/sundsvall/theme.tokens.json`. **Removed:** `VITE_BRAND_*` and `VITE_HEADER_LOGO_*`, `apps/web/.env.example`, the `sundsvall` and `custom` profiles as code, `apps/web/tests/branding-config.test.ts` (it guarded build variables that no longer exist). The development directory gives `mikael` the new capability; `bun run dev:provision` was run and created the `capability:admin.appearance` group, and `bun run db:init` applied the migration to the development database. **Astryx:** core, cli and theme-neutral 0.7.7 to 0.7.12 (MIT, section 3). Four regressions found and fixed: from 0.7.12 a toast is announced from the text in its body's element children, so the completion toast of every administration page (`useAdminAction`) was drawn but not spoken, and `CompletionFeedback` now takes its words as children; a disabled Button is `aria-disabled` and no longer `disabled`, which the review overlay's focus rule read, so a step with nothing to approve focused the disabled Attestera; a Popover's content is drawn in its trigger's theme, so the reminder list in the header's band was white on white, and it is wrapped in the page's theme; a resizable `LayoutPanel` slides, so `todo.spec.ts` waits for the fold. **Found on the way, in code this work did not otherwise touch:** `branding.spec.ts` on the Sundsvall profile expected the dark logo variant at 320 px, where the phone's bar has no band (failed before this work too; the expectation is corrected); `views.spec.ts` cannot be loaded by Playwright (`connectors/communication/connector.mjs` "does not provide an export named 'manifest'"), which stops a run of the whole suite at load, so the suite was run with every other spec named; a star re-export from `@clew/contracts` is invisible to the server under tsx (the unit suite on Bun does not see this), so the theme file's names are re-exported one by one. **Verified:** `bun run check` green (763 unit tests), `bun run test:db` green (86, the appearance store contract on Postgres among them), the live server answers the public routes. WCAG: `admin-appearance.spec.ts` (10), `site-header.spec.ts` (6) and `branding.spec.ts` on neutral, sundsvall and custom green; the whole suite but `views.spec.ts` once on 0.7.12 (555 tests: 378 passed, 162 skipped, 15 failed), then the nine failing spec files on 0.7.7 without this work, where 11 of the 15 fail too (`calendar.spec.ts:182`, four in `menu.spec.ts`, `optimistic.spec.ts:15`, two in `reliability.spec.ts`, two in `tools.spec.ts`, `touch.spec.ts:39`: the Personal row and the header's Start row that the menu and tools specs do not expect, and others not looked into); the four that the upgrade caused are fixed and `shell`, `documents` and `todo` pass again (39 of 39). Those eleven and `views.spec.ts` are left as found. **Live check:** `scripts/appearance-check.ts` (run as `docker exec -e BFF_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3000 -w /workspace clew-app-1 bun scripts/appearance-check.ts`) signs in through Keycloak as the administrator and as an employee and runs Utseende end to end on the dev stack: the public read, the refusals without a session, without the CSRF token and without `admin.appearance`, the example theme file saved and read back, a theme with no contrast and an unknown token refused, a logo saved, served sandboxed and its dark variant removed, an SVG with a script refused, six audit rows for six changes and none for a refusal, and the earlier appearance put back; 20 of 20 on 2026-11-10. **Signed-in pass in the browser, same day** (the product owner signed in as the administrator; the session may not enter the test password on `keycloak.orb.local`): two colours refused and saved, the Sundsvall theme file saved, the logo saved with its dark variant, the variant and the logo removed, Neutral restored, a reload in between. It found three things. The preview's `Section` bled over the padding of the card it stood in and covered the heading, so each preview is the `Card` alone. A chosen file in `FileInput`'s dropzone is a bare name and a cross in an empty box, which the product owner called unprofessional: the three file fields use the compact `input` mode and answer the file with a status (the theme's name and colour count or that it was refused, the image's type and size); the status fades in, so the axe test waits for animations before it measures contrast. Progress bars: the preview's accent bar was about 1.1:1 on its track with the Sundsvall example, and measuring showed the product's own bars were worse without any theme. Every bar Clew draws is the `neutral` variant (D71 item 5: week card, Läget's figures, the review), which Neutral fills with `--color-text-disabled` on a track of `--color-border-emphasized`: 2.1:1 light and 1.0:1 dark, the same in 0.7.7. Clew's base theme now draws the track in `--color-track` and the neutral fill in `--color-text-primary` (13.9:1 and 6.5:1; D71 item 5 amended), the theme check holds `text-primary` on `track` at 3:1, and the preview shows a neutral bar and the header's band (the nested band theme) instead of an accent bar no page draws. Open: the week card's `warning` bar is 1.0:1 on the track in the light appearance. **Example folder and command line, same day:** `themes/sundsvall/` holds `theme.tokens.json` (moved from a file directly in `themes/`) and `appearance.json`, which names the theme file and the two dragons in `apps/web/public/branding/`; `bun scripts/appearance-set.ts themes/sundsvall | --reset | --show` saves it where the database is, through the use cases' new `operator` side (same checks, same audit rows, actor `bootstrap:appearance`, no route; D98 item 10). **Not done:** no manual screen-reader pass of the new page; no check of the 0.7.12 touch and keyboard changes on a phone; Clew's own coarse-pointer adaptation in `theme.ts` is kept though Astryx now ships touch targets; a theme file carries colour only; several server instances are not told of a save (a browser re-reads the appearance on every visit).

The WCAG suite loads again and its eleven old failures are settled, 2026-11-10 (a side session in the same checkout as the branch above; nothing committed): the full run of that day left eleven tests red that fail the same on Astryx 0.7.7 without the branch, and `views.spec.ts` could not be loaded at all. **Product fixes.** (1) *Focus after Avfärda and Inte nu on a card over the page* (`apps/web/src/pages/start.tsx`, `apps/web/src/components/panel.tsx`; `optimistic.spec.ts`). Below 1336 px the card is in Astryx's `Drawer`, a modal dialog, and on a phone in the `BottomSheet`. Sent away with its own button the card's row left, the drawer stayed open on a card without buttons, and the page under it is inert, so the hand-over to the next row focused nothing: focus on `body`, and the toast's Ångra unreachable until the drawer was closed by hand (`snoozeFirst` in `tests/a11y/support.ts` did exactly that). The sheet did close, and lost the focus the same way. Now the drawer closes with the card (D66 as amended by D70: a card leaves with its row), and the focus lands on the row after it when the dialog has left the top layer: the drawer through its dialog's `close` event (`onClosed` on `usePanel`), the sheet through `finalFocusRef`. Beside the page (from 1336 px) nothing changed. A refused request brings the row back but does not open the drawer again. (2) *A reminder that is not on its way says so on the row* (`apps/web/src/components/card.tsx`; `reliability.spec.ts`). Commit 51e0392 moved the two sentences from the row to the open card (`TimerNote`) and left the row silent, against the reliability roadmap's "queued work with visible status" (phase 5A). The row now carries "påminnelsen väntar på schemaläggning" on its meta line (not inside D64's settle window) and a warning badge "Påminnelsen stoppad"; the full sentences stay on the open card. Both wordings are mine and the canvas draws neither: the product owner should look at them. **Spec fixes, each lagging an intended change.** `menu.spec.ts` and `tools.spec.ts`: the test person is a manager, so Personal follows Kalender under Verktyg (D94), and "the current row" is asked of the rail because the shared header marks Start as the current place on every page (D88). `calendar.spec.ts` ("a click outside office hours"): the product hears the click; with the shared header above the shell (D88) the 17:30 row lies under the fold of the 800 px window and `page.mouse` reaches only what is on screen, so the spec scrolls the row up first. `touch.spec.ts` (the calendar's head on a phone): still two rows; the 190 px limit counted the shell's bar, which is D88's 61 px heading row now, so the head is measured from the bar's lower edge (137 px, limit 140; a third row is 185). `views.spec.ts`: a load failure, not a test failure. `documents.spec.ts` imports `tests/fixture-connectors.ts`, which lay outside `tests/a11y/package.json` and so was loaded by Playwright as CommonJS together with the connector modules behind it; `views.spec.ts` then imported `connectors/communication/connector.mjs` as an ES module and its `manifest` export was not found. `tests/package.json` (`"type": "module"`, no dependency) makes one loader own the graph; nothing in `apps/` or `packages/` imports a connector. `snoozeFirst` now expects the drawer to close by itself. **Verified:** `sh scripts/a11y.sh --list` loads 42 files; the files named above and the ones the product fixes touch (keyboard, card-actions, undo, todo, announcements, axe, reflow, review, views) pass; `bun run check` is green on the final code (765 unit and web cases, 10 Node cases, the documentation check); the whole suite on the final code: 574 tests, 410 passed, 162 skipped, 2 failed, neither among the eleven (below). Run beside a full a11y run, `bun run check` timed out two `tests/unit/provisioner.test.ts` cases at 5 s; they pass on a quiet machine. **Not done, found on the way:** `loading.spec.ts:74` fails on this branch with a strict-mode violation (the spinner's label "Hämtar sidan …" is also in Astryx's live region), which is not among the eleven and not caused here; `views.spec.ts:333` (a system that does not answer is spoken once) failed once in the whole run and passed eight times of eight alone: `useSourceBanner` (`apps/web/src/components/source-banner.tsx`) announces as soon as the source statuses arrive, so when the areas and marks arrive later the announcement says the connector's id ("finance svarar inte sedan 06:00. Korten från finance …") while the banner on screen says "Ekonomisystemet", and it is not said again; the announcement should wait for the areas; a done card without an undo window still stays in the drawer after its decision, with the same question about focus, and was left alone because D70's receipt flow has its own specs; on a phone a row on the Inte nu and Klart tabs leads to `?card=` but the sheet opens only for a card in the stream, which was not looked into; no manual screen-reader pass of the two product fixes. Two a11y runs in one checkout delete each other's traces (`--output` gives the second its own folder; `docs/architecture/testing.md`).

The theme branch merged with main, 2026-11-10 (branch `installation-theme-and-logo`, for its pull request): main had moved by 29 commits since the branch was cut (talks, D96; sources that attach to an entity, D97) and had taken both the decision number and the migration number the branch used. The theme decision is now **D98** (file, links and every code comment), and its migration is regenerated as **0044_installation_appearance** after main's 0043_talks; a development database that had applied the branch's old 0043 needs that record removed from `drizzle.__drizzle_migrations` and the table dropped before `bun run db:init`, which was done on this machine. Conflicts were settled by keeping both sides: the server's imports, the schema's two tables, the provisioner's directory with main's named school people and the branch's `admin.appearance` key group (68 groups), the status table, the panel's fold button with the side session's `onClosed`, and `todo.spec.ts` with the poll for the panel's slide before main's second bound. `menu.spec.ts` is main's version where both had corrected the same expectations. Pull request 18 (introduction and help) also calls its decision D97 and will need the next free number when it lands. **Verified on the merged tree:** `bun run check` (863 unit and web cases, 11 Node cases, the documentation check) and `bun run test:db` (112).

Failed job steps, D99 proposed, 2026-11-10 (worktree `entity-sources`, branch `entity-sources`, cut from `origin/main` at `e51b9fe`; documents only): the product owner asked how three actions can be made to happen for every sick report (the manager informed, time reporting and a third system called), what happens when one fails, and what can be done about it without asking every connector for rollback. Read against the code: a job's steps settle on their own with no transaction across systems, and only an expired watch reaches a person (`pushCard` is called once in `apps/server/src/modules/executions/usecases.ts`); a failed or unknown execute step shows in `GET /api/jobs/:id` and the audit rows only. `docs/decisions/D99-an-unresolved-job-step-reaches-a-person-and-is-finished-forward.md` proposes forward recovery: a card for every unresolved step, a required flag on a binding, followers that check before they offer, a lookup or a paired watch for every write step, retry of the failed step only (D80's pattern), manual completion with a watch, and a reconciliation scan as the safety net. It leans on D97 for where the cards show (the opened person's "Behöver dig") and for the person key, and lists four open questions, among them that a fact stored in the ledger should carry `externalId` and not a `userName` that can be renamed. **Not established:** nothing is built or tested; the record is proposed, not accepted.
